# SPEC: IronTrellis Core — history format 2 (`irontrellis-event/2`) — EFFECTIVE TEXT

Status: architect's consolidated normative specification, G3 deliverable (Astra X-14, A-55.3). Produced 2026-10-11 by Fable, the architect, from SPEC r2.3 (2026-10-10) with every binding addendum applied in place. SIMULATED; the architect is not a proof or policy-validity authority and consolidates spec text only.

**Revision r2 (G3 gap fill, 2026-10-11).** Mechanical text integration of `ADDENDA-G2-DRAFT.md` (sha256 `f304abd0631e8f579eafaef00d90595fb991fd7ecc20042bfc4596eb4801a4f5`), the text that `ADDENDA-G2.md` binds "as drafted", with the G2 modifications applied. Every `[GAP]` of r1 whose cause was the missing draft is replaced by the drafted text; HF1 citations are resolved where HF1 answers an incorporated-reference gap. No rule is added beyond the draft, `ADDENDA-G2.md` and HF1; draft points that are ambiguous or that G2 does not reconcile stay `[GAP]` (Appendix E).

**Revision r3 (G3 rulings, 2026-10-11).** Architect rulings A-59…A-70 (`ADDENDA-G3.md`) applied in place with `[A-n]` tags: the §10.1 refusal challenge under A-54 (A-59), lifecycle precedence and fence scope (A-60, A-61), prefix `expiresAt` presence (A-62), rotated/witness-bound format-1 sources refused (A-63), type-23 growth (A-64), export shape and closure (A-65), checkpoint attempts (A-66), Merkle cache reuse (A-67), drained `claimedAt` (A-68), id literals (A-69), glossary and encoder (A-70). The r2 gap fill is accepted; `[GAP]` markers remain only for E-1 (`reference-0.3`) and E-10. Before E5: A-59, A-60, A-61.

## Preamble

**P.1 What this document is.** This text is the SPEC r2.3 with the founder's reserve addendum A-0, the G0 addenda A-0.1 and A-1…A-18, the G1 addenda A-19…A-31 and the G2 addenda A-32…A-58 applied in place. All of them are binding, including A-54, A-40.2–3 and A-41, which the founder approved on 2026-10-11. The precedence that produced this text is: an addendum wins over the SPEC for the points it names; a later addendum wins over an earlier one unless it says otherwise; `13-FABLE-G2-COMBINED-RULINGS.md` records the dispositions. Where this document and any earlier text differ, this document is the effective definition. [A-55] **r5 (2026-10-22).** This revision further applies, in place and under the tags of P.4, the G5 addendum A-86…A-90, the G6 series A-91…A-169 (G6, G6.1…G6.10; A-138 and A-139 unassigned, P.7), the AS-01 rulings A-170…A-186 (`ADDENDA-AS01.md`) and the AS-01 part-2 rulings A-187…A-191 (`ADDENDA-AS01-PART2.md`). All of them are binding; the founder's decisions of the AS-01 sitting are 4bo (publication of the v2-regenerated suite as MANIFEST revision 2, A-184.2; A-133.5 and A-163 stand), 4bn (A-182), and 4al and 4am as given their final text by A-179 and A-181. The precedence that produced r5 is **AS01-PART2 > AS01 > G6.10 > G6.9 > … > G6.1 > G6 > G5 > G4 > r4**: the latest text of every chain is the one integrated, each passage carries its final ruling's tag only, and Appendix D lists the chain (A-186.3); `27-FABLE-AS01-RULINGS.md` and `28-FABLE-AS01-PART2-RULINGS.md` record the dispositions. [A-186.1] [A-186.3]

**P.2 Unreleased drafts.** Format 2 is unreleased. r2.3 alone, r2.3+G0 and r2.3+G0+G1 were unreleased drafts; no history under any of them is deployed. The effective format-2 definition is this document together with the conformance suite named in P.3. "No frozen vector changes", as stated by the addenda, is a regression statement about the test suite, not a claim that histories accepted under a draft remain accepted. [A-55.1]

**P.3 Vectors.** The 86 frozen `conformance/v2` vectors predate A-54. A-54 changes the signature bytes of every session-signed declaration, effect and counterparty wrapper and therefore every subsequent event hash, record hash, state root, projection head and all nine pinned roots. The frozen vectors **will be regenerated** from the architect generator at E5 and re-verified with the independent checker; until that regeneration lands, the 86 vectors are a regression suite for the pre-A-54 challenge forms only, and the pinned-root list in the addenda is to be replaced by the E5 list. The 34 legacy, 90 v1 and rc.1 transparency vectors are unchanged. The frozen `conformance/v2` suite as regenerated plus the builder's E5 regenerated suite is the format definition. [A-54] [A-55.2] **r5 status.** The sentences above describe the state at r2.3–r4 and are kept for traceability. The E5 regeneration landed and E-17 is closed [A-171.3]; the frozen suite is the 86 vectors of `conformance/v2-g3` with the nine pinned roots, unchanged by every rule of A-86…A-191 (§19 [A-191.3]); the current conformance manifest and vector ledger, including MANIFEST revision 2 of the regenerated suite, is P.8 [A-184.2] [AR-09].

**P.4 Conventions.** MUST, MUST NOT, SHOULD, MAY are RFC 2119. Section numbers are the SPEC r2.3 numbers; new subsections are inserted where needed and never renumber an existing one. Each changed or added passage is tagged `[A-n]`; a passage taken from the G2 draft is tagged `[A-n, as drafted]`, or `[A-n, as drafted, modified by G2]` where `ADDENDA-G2.md` modifies it; superseded text is removed and listed in Appendix D. Where applying two addenda leaves a gap, the text says `[GAP: …]` and Appendix E lists it; no gap is resolved silently. Where the SPEC says "unchanged", `docs/HISTORY-FORMAT.md` of rc.1-final (cited as **HF1 §n**) remains normative for format 1 and for the common rules, and the frozen `reference-0.3` source is normative for the ported duty-policy schemas of §13; those two references are incorporated by reference and are not reproduced here (Appendix E, item E-1). Dates are UTC. [A-56.23] A passage corrected at the architect's r5 sign-off (`R5-SIGNOFF.md`, register entry AR-09) also carries `[AR-09]`; a passage corrected by the independent review of r5 (`R5-OPUS-REVIEW.md`) or at AS-02 as a pure consistency correction carries `[OR-01]`; neither tag adds a rule, and the ruling tag beside it names the rule the passage states. ## 1. Scope and non-goals

In scope: the on-disk history, the hashes and signatures over it, the replay transition rules, the decision procedure (authority then budgets), the state commitment, the head attestation, the proof projection, the export view and the error codes; and, where an addendum attached them to a section, the writer-side rules that a conforming writer MUST follow before signing (marked "writer contract"). Out of scope: the local API surface names (the builder keeps rc.1-final's `LocalOwner` API and adds the calls listed in ARCHITECTURE.md §8, whose exact signatures and error codes the builder proposed in `G0-CONFIRMATION.md`) [A-56.22], the CLI text, the witness wire formats (the witness protocol stays `irontrellis-witness/1` and the head statement `irontrellis-head-statement/1`, §7.4 [A-5]; the witness's epoch records, admin import and §9.9 bundles gain the additive fields `policy?` and `policyKnown`, and its verifier the obligations of A-100.1, A-111.3 and A-120.1, which `WITNESS.md` specifies [E-10]) [A-177], the Python port (written from this document and the vectors), and any prover (ARCHITECTURE.md §6 says where one attaches).

Not in format 2: summary-seeded histories (a history always starts at its own genesis), budget refunds, Keccak as a format-2 hash — except the fixed literal `rulesHash` of §13.0, carried as a constant and never computed [A-56.9] [A-15] —, threshold-signature semantics inside Core (ARCHITECTURE.md §7).

The public API creates only format-2 histories; if a `format` option is kept its only allowed value is `"irontrellis-event/2"`, anything else `INVALID_INPUT`. The format-1 writer is not reachable from the public API; it stays an unexported test-only hook until G3, when the builder removes it or keeps it internal and records the choice in DEVIATIONS. [A-9] [A-30]

## 2. Delta from rc.1-final (summary table)

| Area | rc.1-final (format 1) | Format 2 | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| History hash | SHA-256 over the canonical text of the whole prefix wrapper (HF1 §4) | Record-hash chain: one fixed-width SHA-256 per event (§4.3); the head hash *is* the chain tip | O(1) prover work per event; no re-hashing of the prefix text |
| Store line `recordHash` | `hashCanonical({version, previousHash, event})` | the §4.3 chain hash | one chain instead of two commitments |
| Amounts | `{"$continuity.bigint":"n"}` marker records | decimal strings `"n"` in schema-typed fields (§3.4); JSON strings only [A-7] | one encoding; no marker parsing in a guest |
| Control challenge | `irontrellis-control-authorization/1` | `/2` with the chain head (§6.1) | domain separation |
| Runtime/admin challenges, proofs, receipts | `continuity-*/0.2` literals | `irontrellis-*/2` literals (§8) | same structures, new encoding of amounts and heads |
| Intents | unsigned `TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED` | session-signed (§8.6); the challenge binds the envelope [A-54] | the ZK work needed signed requests; intent squatting closed |
| Lifecycle records (types 14–16) | unsigned | session-signed `recordAuthorization` (§8.8) [A-21] [A-51] | the recording session is authenticated |
| Refusals | not recorded | `REQUEST_REFUSED`, session-signed, re-derived on replay (§10.1) | first-class, verifiable denials |
| Effects | not recorded | `EFFECT_RECORDED`, session-signed (§10.2) | domain-neutral measurement record |
| Counterparty receipts | none | `COUNTERPARTY_REGISTERED/RETIRED`, `COUNTERPARTY_RECEIPT_RECORDED` (§11) | second receipt layer |
| Budgets | per-authority lifetime caps only | `BUDGET_DECLARED/UPDATED/CLOSED`; subject selectors incl. roles, periods, bounded rollover (§9); nullable metric [A-26] | native budgets (founder D2) |
| State commitment | none | `STATE_CHECKPOINT`, `stateRoot` in every head attestation (§7); the record set determines the replay state (§7.2 invariant) | incremental readers, seals, provers (D3) |
| Projection | none | 160-byte row per event, chained (§12) | guest work without JSON (D3) |
| Duty-policy events, view, finding producers, duty quota | refused (`UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_TYPE`) | ported from Continuity preview.10.2 (§13) | founder D5 and answer 1 |
| Legacy and format-1 writes | read-write | read only; `migrate` re-records into format 2 and fences the source (§16) | zero users; one writer; Continuity Core keeps writing legacy files for ZK users (founder answer 3) |
| Head attestation | `irontrellis-head-attestation/1` {version, domain, head} (HF1 §9) | `/2` {version, domain, head, stateRoot, projectionHead} (§7.4) in the pin and the anchors; witnesses keep `/1` head statements [A-5] | resume points for hooks and provers [A-56.18] |
| Capacity | declared limits; nine always-admitted control types | declared limits never refuse a control event; a fixed reserve below the hard caps is usable only by control events (§5.2) [A-0] [A-1] | a revocation can always be recorded until the history is terminal |
| Event types | 29 | 42 (codes 0–40 and 42; 41 unassigned) (§5) [A-90.5] | |
| Export view | none | `irontrellis-export/2` with roots and records at both ends (§15) | the only interface a prover or an auditor consumes [A-43] |

## 3. Canonical encoding, scalars and hashing

### 3.1 Canonical text
Unchanged from HF1 §2 (`continuity-canonical/0.2`) except as stated here: records with keys sorted by UTF-8 byte order, dense arrays, integers in `-(2^53-1)…2^53-1`, `true/false/null`, depth ≤ 32, ≤ 16 MiB.

**String escaping.** [A-49] Format 2 keeps `continuity-canonical/0.2` escaping exactly: `"` → `\"`, `\` → `\\`, every code point U+0000–U+001F → lowercase `\u00xx` (including the characters that JSON allows as `\b \t \n \f \r`), every other well-formed code point emitted as UTF-8. Hashing serialization MUST follow these rules independently of the host serializer. [A-70.2] One encoder under these rules is conforming for format 2 and for the §16.2 shadow context (the rules were verified identical to rc.1-final `canonical.ts` and the frozen Python `_escape`); a shadow hash MUST equal the source's, and on any divergence the source algorithm governs the shadow and the finding is reported.

**Limits apply to state records.** [A-21.3] [A-44.2] The limits of this section (4,096 list members, 256 keys, 16 MiB) apply to each state record (§7.2) and to each journal operation's encoded record value (§7.5), never to the snapshot container. A transition whose resulting record would exceed them is `TRANSITION_INVALID` (never an encoder throw).

**Difference from format 1:** in a format-2 history the bigint marker record `{"$continuity.bigint": …}` is NOT representable inside event data, challenges, proofs, receipts or state records; a key beginning with `$continuity.` anywhere in format-2 `data` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`. (The marker survives only in the adapter line protocol for legacy and format-1 vectors.)

**Parsing.** [A-7] [A-34, as drafted, modified by G2] A-7's "plain JSON parsing" reads "parsing without marker revival under this grammar"; the platform parser alone does not satisfy points 1–3 below. **Scope:** format-2 store lines, the snapshot, the export view, adapter inputs and outputs for format-2 vectors, and every other JSON text a format-2 engine consumes or emits. Legacy and format-1 readers keep rc.1-final tolerance unchanged (A-28).
1. **Grammar.** A format-2 JSON text MUST be well-formed UTF-8 with no BOM and no lone surrogate (literal or `\uD800`–`\uDFFF` escape outside a valid pair); objects MUST NOT contain a duplicate key; numbers MUST match `0|-?[1-9][0-9]*` with no fraction, no exponent and no `-0`, and lie in `-(2^53−1) … 2^53−1`. Key order and inter-token whitespace stay tolerated (HF1 §3). A violation in a store line or snapshot is `INVALID_HISTORY {position}` (snapshot: `SNAPSHOT_STALE` and `FULL_REPLAY`); in an adapter or API input, `INVALID_INPUT`.
2. **Scalars** (whole-string grammars): §3.3.
3. **Id-keyed structures.** Engines MUST key every id-indexed structure (budgets, roles, agents, sessions, counterparties, intents, nonces, refusals, effects, duties) with maps that have no inherited members; the ids `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString`, `hasOwnProperty` are ordinary Identifiers.
4. **Regenerated-suite negatives:** `"timestamp": 1.0`; `-0`; `1e2`; a duplicate key in `data`; the Amounts `"12\n"` and `"１２"`; and two positives: the ids `"__proto__"` and `"constructor"` accepted as ordinary ids.
5. The Python v2 port and every guest reader MUST implement points 1–3 identically; a divergence is a conformance failure.

No vector or pinned root changes (`budget-v2.jsonl` has no duplicate keys and no non-integer numbers; the builder confirms `vectors.json` under the strict parser). [A-34, as drafted]

### 3.2 Hash
`sha256(bytes)` is FIPS 180-4 SHA-256. `hashCanonical(v) = "0x" + hex(sha256(utf8(canonical(v))))`, 64 lowercase hex digits. Format 2 uses no other hash, except the fixed literal `rulesHash` of §13.0, carried as a constant and never computed [A-56.9] [A-15]. Keccak-256 remains only in the legacy reader and in the `eip191-personal-sign-keccak256` interop suite (§6.3).

**Genesis hashes (glossary).** [A-47.3, as drafted; sentence replaced by A-70.1] There are two hash values: `genesisEventHash = eventHash(e₀)` (§4.2, §15) is the event hash; `historyId` and `genesisHash` are two names for the genesis record-chain hash `recordHash(0)` (§4.3, §11.2, §13.1, A-5). Never substitute `genesisEventHash` for either record-chain-hash field. A glossary fixture showing the equality and the difference joins the regenerated suite. The other A-47 points are applied in §6.3 (custody, A-47.4), §12.3 (217 bytes, A-47.2) and §15 (`historyId`, A-47.1).

### 3.3 Scalars
| name | rule |
|---|---|
| Identifier | 1..256 UTF-8 bytes, well-formed, no C0 control, no DEL (HF1 §5) |
| ProtocolString | ≤ 4,096 bytes, well-formed |
| U53 | integer `0 … 2^53-1` |
| **Amount** | a JSON **string** matching `^(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$`, ≤ 78 characters, value ≤ MAX = 2^256−1 (**non-negative only**). Comparison and arithmetic are on the exact integer value; two Amounts are equal iff their strings are equal (canonical spelling makes these coincide). **Closure:** no stored Amount (state records, headroom results, usage counters) ever exceeds MAX: §9.4 computes allowances with saturation at MAX; an admission whose charge would push a `budgetUsage.amount` above MAX is `DENY AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` from the budget procedure (§9.4 step 5, source BUDGET, `budgetId` set), one that would push a §7.2 `usage.admittedCumulativeAmount` above MAX is `DENY AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` from the authority procedure (§8.1, source CORE); both are `TRANSITION_INVALID` on replay. A u256 implementation therefore never overflows. [A-7] Every Amount field in a format-2 event MUST be a JSON string satisfying this row. A JSON number, a `null` where the schema forbids it, a `$continuity.bigint` marker, or a string failing this row (pattern, > 78 chars, > MAX) is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`. The engine-internal representation is free; every hashed, signed or stored structure and every byte-equality check (admission proof, administrative proof, finding challenge) uses this string |
| ContentHash | `0x` + 64 lowercase hex |
| PublicKey32 | `0x` + 64 lowercase hex (Ed25519 raw public key). **Keys at declaration** (replay rule, `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) [A-32.2, as drafted]: a genesis or rotation owner key with `scheme: "ed25519"`, a session `credentialPublicKey`, or a counterparty `credential` MUST be a canonical encoding of a point that is not of small order and lies in the prime-order subgroup (`[L]A = identity`, one scalar multiplication per declared key, performed once); a prime-order, non-identity point is required (G2, Astra X-8). EIP-191 keys are addresses and are not affected. In a legacy or format-1 history a key failing this test is reported, never refused (§6.3, A-28) |
| EthereumAddress | `0x` + 40 hex, compared lowercase |
| Signature | Ed25519: `0x` + 128 lowercase hex; EIP-191: `0x` + 130 lowercase hex, low-s (HF1 §8.1). Ed25519 verification profile per §6.3 [A-32] |
| Head | `{hash: ContentHash, position: U53, canonicalTime: U53}`; a *short head* is `{hash, position}` |

**Whole-string grammars.** [A-34.2, as drafted] Every grammar of this table (Amount, ContentHash, PublicKey32, EthereumAddress, Signature, the 16-digit record keys, `budgetUsage` period) matches the **whole** string over ASCII only, stated as `\A…\z`; implementations MUST NOT use a `$`-anchored match that admits a trailing newline, locale digit parsing, or digit-separator parsing. Identifier and ProtocolString keep their byte rules above.

**Credential equality.** [A-32 (ii)] Two credentials are equal iff they have the same `scheme` and the same lowercase credential string ("equal" in the key-role rule of §6.3).

**PublicKey32 case.** [A-157] The PublicKey32 grammar is lowercase and whole-string (`\A0x[0-9a-f]{64}\z`). An uppercase or mixed-case PublicKey32 in a native format-2 event, or in any event after a migrated prefix, is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` at C4 (Appendix A A.2), at that event's position, before control authorization and before the point test above; this governs every PublicKey32 slot (a session `credentialPublicKey`, owner keys at rows 0 and 26, attesters, counterparty keys at row 36). No normalization applies: the credential-equality rule's "lowercase" exists for EthereumAddress, whose grammar admits mixed case, and the §7.2 `session` record's "lowercase" is a record rule, not a licence to lowercase an event. Inside a migrated prefix the source format's schema applies as the oracle applies it (A-52.1, A-28); EthereumAddress, including `credentialAddress`, is unchanged.

### 3.4 Where Amounts occur
Every field that was an Amount in format 1 (grant `maxAmount`, `maxCumulativeAmount`; request `amount`; `usageSnapshot[].admittedCumulativeAmount`; receipt payload amounts; intent `amount`) is a §3.3 Amount string in format 2, in the history and in every structure hashed or signed. New Amount fields: budget caps, effect `amount`, counterparty envelope `amount`, headroom results. [A-7] The regenerated suite carries negatives for a numeric Amount, a 79-character Amount and MAX + 1.

## 4. Store lines, the record chain and the head

### 4.1 Lines
A format-2 history file is UTF-8, no BOM, one JSON object per line, `\n`-terminated (HF1 §3 rules on splitting and whitespace unchanged; the line's JSON text is parsed under the strict grammar of §3.1 [A-34]). Each line has exactly the four keys, written in this order:

```
{"version":"irontrellis-portable-file-store/2","previousHash":<null | ContentHash>,"event":<event>,"recordHash":"<ContentHash>"}
```

`previousHash` is `null` on line 0 and the previous line's `recordHash` otherwise. [A-3] The exact line `L_q` for the event at position `q` is `{"version":"irontrellis-portable-file-store/2","previousHash":…,"event":<canonical(e_q)>,"recordHash":"0x…"}` plus one `\n`, with no whitespace; the writer MUST emit exactly `L_q`, because the file-size measure `B(p)` of §5.2 is defined over these canonical lines and not over `stat().size`.

A file's format is decided by line 0's `version`; the genesis `versions.eventSchemaVersion` MUST agree (`UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` otherwise). [A-58.7] `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` is the store-level code with which the rc.1 engines (Python, format-1 TypeScript) refuse a foreign store or event-schema literal at position 0; the format-2 replayer refuses a genesis whose `eventSchemaVersion` is an unknown literal with `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_SCHEMA` (§5) and a `versions` block that is otherwise not the exact format-2 literal set with `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` (§5.1).

**Chain verification of lines.** [A-33] A reader that verifies lines against the chain (eagerly or lazily, §7.5 step (2)) reports a chain break as the store code `INVALID_HISTORY {position}`: "`INVALID_HISTORY` is rc.1-final's chain-break store code (BUILD §4 T3); it gains `{position}`" [A-33, as drafted, modified by G2] (HF1 §3: a file with a chain, encoding or hash defect is `INVALID_HISTORY`). Lazy verification of a line read after a resume recomputes both hashes and fails closed (§7.5, "Lazy line verification") [A-33, as drafted].

### 4.2 Event hash
`eventHash(e) = sha256(utf8(canonical(e)))` over the whole four-key event (`id`, `type`, `timestamp`, `data`), 32 raw bytes.

### 4.3 Record hash (the chain)
```
recordHash(p) = sha256( utf8("irontrellis-record/2") ‖ 0x00 ‖ prev ‖ eventHash(e_p) ‖ be64(p) )
prev = 0x00×32 when p = 0, else recordHash(p−1)
```
`be64` is the 8-byte big-endian unsigned encoding. The preimage is exactly 20 + 1 + 32 + 32 + 8 = 93 bytes. `recordHash` is written to the line as `0x` + 64 hex. `historyId` denotes `recordHash(0)`. [A-5] [A-19.4]

### 4.4 Head
The head at position `p` is `{hash: recordHash(p), position: p, canonicalTime: e_p.timestamp}`. The head *before* an event at `p` is the head at `p−1` (`null` for `p = 0`). There is no wrapper hash and no "empty history hash" in format 2; an empty file has no head. Heads are compared field by field.

### 4.5 Bounds
Per event: `data` ≤ 1 MiB canonical, event ≤ 1 MiB + envelope. [A-0] [A-3] Per history, two **absolute limits** that refuse every event, control events included: `HARD_RECORDS = 1,048,576` state records (§7.2 leaves) and `HARD_BYTES = 1,073,741,824` (1 GiB) of canonical line bytes `B(p)` (§4.1). Since every accepted event adds an `event` record, `HARD_RECORDS` also bounds the history length to under 1,048,576 events (stated in OPERATOR). Below the absolute limits a **reserve** is kept by two fixed constants, not genesis fields: `RESERVED_STATE_RECORDS = 4096` and `RESERVED_FILE_BYTES = 16 MiB = 16,777,216`, giving the thresholds `T_R = 1,048,576 − 4,096 = 1,044,480` records and `T_B = 1 GiB − 16 MiB = 1,056,964,608` bytes. Only control events may use the reserve (§5.2). No whole-history canonical bound exists (the chain never re-hashes the prefix). Declared limits (`capacity.*`) never refuse control events; the two absolute limits refuse everything (§5.2). [A-1]

At an absolute limit the history is **terminal**: a successor history starts at its own genesis and does not carry budget usage, counterparty chains or refusals, until a format-2 continuation is specified (founder D7 as cited in SPEC §7.5, DECISIONS D8). [A-23.4] [A-56.12]

## 5. Event envelope and the 42 types (codes 0–40 and 42; 41 unassigned [A-71.0])

The envelope is unchanged (HF1 §5): `{id, type, timestamp, data}`; `id` unique (`EVENT_ID_DUPLICATE`), position 0 is `DEPLOYMENT_INITIALIZED`, timestamps non-decreasing (`EVENT_TIME_REGRESSION`), closed per-type `data`.

**Replay order per event** (first failure wins): envelope/capture (`INVALID_INPUT`) → genesis position and version probe (`GENESIS_REQUIRED`; an unknown `eventSchemaVersion` literal `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_SCHEMA`; a known schema with a `versions` block that is not the exact §5.1 literal set, or disagreeing with line 0, `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` [A-58.7]) → type known in this format (`UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_TYPE`) → closed schema (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) → duplicate genesis / duplicate id / time regression → **the time rules T1 and T3** for every ◇ and ▲ event (§6.5.6; `TRANSITION_INVALID`; envelope checks that read only the timestamp, the policy in force and the `ownerTime` index, so they precede the session fence and the type's own list [A-172.1]) → **owner control authorization** for control events (`CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`; under an owner policy the quorum of §6.5.3) → **the time rule T2** for every control event (§6.5.6, immediately after control authorization [A-172.1]) → **the type's own ordered checks** where a section lists them (§7.3 checkpoints, §8.3 admissions, §8.6 declarations, §8.8 lifecycle records [A-51], §10.1 refusals, §10.2 effects, §10.3 reconciliation observations [A-71], §11.2 counterparty receipts, §13 duty-policy events: those lists take precedence for their types and name their codes) → for every other type: session authorization (§6.2, whose first check is the epoch fence `SESSION_FENCED` [A-29] [A-58.5]) then transition preconditions (`TRANSITION_INVALID`) → **capacity, as the last check** (§5.2, not applied at position 0) [A-1] [A-3]. A rejected event leaves the state unchanged; the rejection names the position.

The type table. ◆ = control event (owner-signed, §6.1). ◇ = session-signed (§6.2). ▲ = administrative (runtime-signed administrative authorization, HF1 §8.4, `irontrellis-administrative-authorization/2`). The number is the projection type code (§12).

| # | type | signing | `data` (format 2) |
|---|---|---|---|

| 1 | `PRINCIPAL_CREATED` ◆ | ◆ | `principalId` |

### 5.1 Genesis (`DEPLOYMENT_INITIALIZED.data`)
Required: `domain = {protocol:"irontrellis", version:"2", deploymentId: Identifier}`, `adapterPolicyHash` (one of E1–E6, HF1 §6.1), `canonicalLineageId`, `versions` (exact literal set below), `policyVersion` (Identifier; the local API writes `irontrellis-local-owner/2`; a migration always rewrites it to that literal [A-52.4]), `rootRecognitionPolicy: "declared-principal-root/0.2"`, `globalPolicySourceId`, `timeSource: "EVENT_TIMESTAMP"`, `finality: "LOCAL_ONLY"`, `ownerKeys` (1..64, HF1 §6.1; each Ed25519 key subject to the §3.3 declaration test, `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` [A-32.2]). Optional: `nextKeyDigests?`, `capacity?` (`{maxEvents: U53|null, maxStateRecords?: U53|null}`), `migratedFrom?` (§16.2 step 4; its closed schema includes `sourceAttestation` and `migrationId` [A-40.2–3]), `checkpointPolicy?` (`{everyEvents: U53 ≥ 1 | null}`, default `null` = manual; automatic checkpoints per §7.3 [A-46]), `projection?` (`"irontrellis-projection/2" | null`, default the literal = rows are computed; `null` = projection off and `projectionHead` is `null` everywhere), `ownerPolicy?` (the owner approval policy of epoch 0, closed schema and validity per §6.5.2; absent = the default policy `{threshold: 1}`; forbidden in a migrated genesis [A-91.0]), `sessionTimeHorizonMs?` (`U53 ≥ 1 | null`, default `null`; `0` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` at C4; when non-null it enables the session time horizon rule T3 of §6.5.6; the `genesis` record copies it as written, so an absent field stays absent and no existing root moves [A-179.1 (A-22.6)]).

`versions`, format 2, every key required, every value exact:

```
eventSchemaVersion:                 "irontrellis-event/2"
historyHashAlgorithm:               "sha256"
recordChainVersion:                 "irontrellis-record/2"
stateDerivationVersion:             "irontrellis-state/2"
projectionVersion:                  "irontrellis-projection/2"
receiptSchemaVersion:               "irontrellis-receipt/2"
controlAuthorizationVersion:        "irontrellis-control-authorization/2"
queryEnvelopeVersion:               "continuity-query-envelope/0.2"
authorizationProofVersion:          "irontrellis-authorization-proof/2"
runtimeAuthorizationVersion:        "irontrellis-runtime-authorization/2"
administrativeAuthorizationVersion: "irontrellis-administrative-authorization/2"
signatureScheme:                    "ed25519" | "eip191-personal-sign-keccak256"
```

Any other key, a missing key, or a different value is `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` (vector `rejects-format1-literals-in-format2-genesis`); an unknown `eventSchemaVersion` literal is `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_SCHEMA` [A-58.7]. `domain.version` other than `"2"` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`. The genesis is not a control event: it carries no `controlAuthorization` (HF1 §6.1); it is authenticated by the owner-signed pin and by every later control event's chain head.

### 5.2 Capacity (replay rule) [A-0] [A-0.1] [A-1] [A-2] [A-3] [A-56.1]

#### 5.2.1 Rule
In a format-2 history the capacity rules are **replay rules**, applied as the last check for every event after position 0. A writer MUST apply the same rules before signing; a writer refusal and a replay rejection of the same event carry the same code. Legacy and format-1 histories get no replay capacity rule (rc.1-final behaviour; they are read-only). [A-1.1–2]

The declared limits `capacity.maxEvents` and `capacity.maxStateRecords` MUST NOT refuse a control event of any type. rc.1-final's `ALWAYS_ADMITTED_EVENT_TYPES` does not decide control-event admission in format 2 (a deliberate widening over rc.1, which admitted only nine ◆ types at CONTROL_ONLY). Only the absolute limits refuse a control event. [A-1.3]

**Definitions.** [A-3] `R(p)` = number of §7.2 leaves after events `0…p`, `R(−1) = 0`. `B(p)` = Σ over `q ≤ p` of `len(utf8(L_q))`, `L_q` the exact §4.1 line plus one `\n`. `HARD_RECORDS = 1,048,576`; `HARD_BYTES = 1,073,741,824`; `T_R = 1,044,480`; `T_B = 1,056,964,608` (§4.5). `RESERVE_CLASS` is one exported constant set equal to the full non-control class of P.5 (every ◇ type including 14–16, every ▲ type) [A-0.1] [A-56.1]. `M = capacity.maxEvents` (when non-null). `S(p) = eventCount + lifecycleReserved + dutyReserved` over accepted events `0…p`, where `lifecycleReserved` is rc.1-final's lifecycle reservation (`capacity.ts` `account`/`reservedSlots`: one slot per not-yet-produced type of the 11-type lifecycle set for every declared intent; the builder lists that set verbatim from rc.1 `capacity.ts` in DEVIATIONS and the Python port takes it from there [A-56.11]) and `dutyReserved = Σ over activated duties of (4 − dispositions) + (2 − contests)` (§13.5). [A-2]

**Capacity step** (last replay step; first failure wins; not applied at position 0): [A-3]
1. **Absolute limits (every event, control included):** `R(p−1) ≥ HARD_RECORDS`, or `R(p) > HARD_RECORDS`, or `B(p) > HARD_BYTES` → `CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` (so a record-reducing control event is refused at the cap too).
2. **Reserve:** for an event whose type is in `RESERVE_CLASS`, `R(p) > T_R` or `B(p) > T_B`, judged after the event → `CAPACITY_RESERVED`. Every control event is admitted until step 1 refuses it. [A-0.1] [A-56.1]
3. **`maxStateRecords`** (non-control events; declared, non-null): `R(p) > maxStateRecords` → `CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` (every event adds its `event` record, so every non-control event is subject).
4. **`maxEvents`** (non-control events): refused with `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY` **iff** (a) the type is not one of rc.1-final's always-admitted non-control types `TRANSACTION_INTENT_CONSUMED`, `TRANSACTION_OUTCOME_RECORDED`, `RECEIPT_RECORDED`, `OBLIGATION_PERFORMANCE_ASSIGNED`, `OBLIGATION_STATUS_RECORDED`, `OUTCOME_OBSERVATION_RECORDED`, `ATTEMPT_DUTY_REVIEW_CLOSED`, (b) `S(p) > S(p−1)` (the event is growth), and (c) `S(p) > M`. Consequently `TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED`, `ATTEMPT_DUTY_POLICY_ACTIVATED`, `REQUEST_REFUSED`, `EFFECT_RECORDED`, `COUNTERPARTY_RECEIPT_RECORDED`, `RECONCILIATION_OBSERVED` [A-71.8] (and every other event that is growth under the formula below [A-90.3]) are refused when (c) holds; lifecycle records spending their reservation and dispositions/contests within the quota are admitted. [A-2] [A-56.11] [A-72, superseding A-64.1–2] "Growth" is `S(p) > S(p−1)`: for every accepted non-control event `ΔS = 1 + ΔlifecycleReserved + ΔdutyReserved`, and the event is growth iff `ΔS > 0`; whether a record is rewritten in place or a leaf is added is irrelevant. The 11-type lifecycle set is rc.1-final `capacity.ts`'s verbatim and contains `ATTEMPT_DUTY_ASSIGNED` (23), `OBLIGATION_CREATED` (17) and `ATTEMPT_DUTY_CREATED` (22) [A-171.1]: the first type-23 event for a source intent spends that intent's type-23 reservation, and the intent's single type-17 (resp. type-22) event spends its type-17 (resp. type-22) reservation (`ΔS = 0`, admitted at `maxEvents`). A repeat type-23 for the same intent (a second assignment of the one duty) is `ΔS = +1` and refused `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY` when `S(p) > M`. A repeat type 17 or 22 for the same intent is refused earlier as `TRANSITION_INVALID`: A-72 describes the census and removes no rc.1 precondition, so the inherited preconditions "no obligation already for `sourceIntentId`" (17) and "no duty yet for the source intent" (22) stand where Appendix A rows 17 and 22 put them, after the fence and the administrative authorization and before capacity, and a second 17 or 22 for one source intent never reaches the capacity step; the formula's `ΔS = +1` for it is never reached in a valid history. [A-88.1] [A-88.2] One per-intent reservation per type is shared by every duty or obligation referencing that intent (A-64.3's condition is met; under A-88.1 the set it ranges over has at most one obligation and one duty per intent; the builder pins the first-17, first-23, repeat-23 and repeat-17/22 cases at the cap, a repeat 17 or 22 as `TRANSITION_INVALID` [A-88.4]).

**Bounded "always".** At least 2,048 control events (≤ 2 records each) and at least **15** maximum-size control events fit in the reserve (a maximum line is 1 MiB of data plus envelope and wrapper). [A-0.3] [A-0.1.3] `additionalControlAuthorizations` (§6.1) adds no record kind and no growth; a ◆ event carrying 63 co-signatures is still ≤ 1 MiB `data` (§4.5), and the builder states the maximum control-event line size with them in BENCHMARKS [A-95.7].

**Counters.** `lifecycleReserved`, `dutyReserved`, `R` and `B` MUST be kept incrementally in state and be rebuildable from the records and the file (lifecycle from `intent`/`receipt`/`duty`/`obligation` records, `dutyReserved` from `duty.policy` counts, `R` = `recordCount`, `B` from the canonical lines); no per-event rescan. [A-3] (an engine rule of this package; independent verifiers conform by results [A-190])

**`maxStateRecords` is not a latch.** [A-56.24] `maxStateRecords` cannot be raised within the history. Non-control events are refused while their resulting record count would exceed it. Control events that remove records can restore room; there is no permanent control-only latch. At the absolute cap step 1 refuses every event. Operators MUST NOT rely on record removal as a recovery path. OPERATOR and the CLI use this wording; `init` warns when `maxStateRecords` is set [A-23.3].

**Per-actor usage.** One actor's refusal and effect records consume the `maxStateRecords` shared by every actor (stated in SECURITY); a per-actor quota is a possible additive format-3 field, not in format 2. A deployment that wants to bound refusal and effect records sets `maxStateRecords`; hooks and the gateway additionally coalesce and rate-limit refusals per actor before recording them (§10.1.1) [A-23]. A per-actor `REFUSAL_LIMIT` replay rule is **not** adopted (founder option, deferred) [A-23.5].

**Code meanings.** [A-10.4] `CAPACITY_RESERVED` means only the reserve refusal of step 2, in replay and in every API including the duty API; Continuity's "no administrative room" meaning is not carried: a duty activation at `maxEvents` is `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY`; dispositions and contests within the quota are admitted; beyond it, eligibility (§13.2) refuses. `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY`, `CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` and `CAPACITY_RESERVED` are format-2 replay codes and writer pre-check codes with the same meaning [A-10.1] [A-56.8].

**Tests (obligations).** [A-0.5] [A-3] No conformance vector can reach the real caps, so: the package exports the two reserve constants; a unit test with a test-only cap override proves `CAPACITY_RESERVED` for every `RESERVE_CLASS` type at the threshold (including a type-14 event), ◆ admitted up to the cap, `CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` at it, a record-reducing control event refused at the cap, and a non-control event refused when crossing `T_R`; one regenerated-suite vector at the overridden cap and one `REQUEST_REFUSED` at `maxEvents` (`CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY`) [A-2]; `BENCHMARKS.md` reports records and bytes at 10,000 events; `DEVIATIONS.md` states the rule is untested at true scale.

#### 5.2.2 `capacity()` report (API) [A-13] [A-56.7]
The package exports `RESERVED_STATE_RECORDS = 4096` and `RESERVED_FILE_BYTES = 16777216`. For format-2 histories `capacity()` returns rc.1-final's fields plus `stateRecords` (`R` at the head), `maxStateRecords` (declared or null), `fileBytes` (`B` at the head), `dutyReserved`, `reserveThresholds {stateRecords: 1044480, fileBytes: 1056964608}`, `hardLimits {stateRecords: 1048576, fileBytes: 1073741824}` and `mode ∈ {OPEN, CONTROL_ONLY, RESERVED, FULL}` (`FULL`: no event fits; `RESERVED`: at or above a reserve threshold; `CONTROL_ONLY`: a declared limit reached). `mode` describes the head; one event may still be refused under §5.2.1 before the mode changes. rc.1's `controlAlwaysAdmitted: true` is dropped.

## 6. Signatures

### 6.1 Control authorization (owner-signed events, ◆)
`data.controlAuthorization = {keyId: Identifier, signature: Signature}`, signed by the owner key named `keyId` in force at the event's position (HF1 §7.1 epochs, as read in §6.5.4 [A-170.1]), over `hashCanonical(challenge)`:

```
{ "version": "irontrellis-control-authorization/2", "domain": <genesis domain>, "eventId", "eventType", "timestamp",
  "previousHead": null | {"hash": <recordHash(p−1)>, "position": p−1}, "effectHash": hashCanonical(effect) }
```
**The effect.** [A-170.8] The effect of a ◆ event is `data` without `controlAuthorization`, `additionalControlAuthorizations`, `nextKeyAuthorizations` and `sourceControlAuthorization`; `effectHash = hashCanonical(effect)`; the `succession.rule` record stores the effect of a `SUCCESSION_RULE_DECLARED` as written [A-16.3] [A-95.1]. This section states the effect once; the §7.2 `succession` row and Appendix B row 4 refer to it and carry no second list. [A-187.2] [AR-09] The challenge's `effectHash` and the `succession.rule` record encoder use **one shared function** implementing this definition, so that the two can never diverge; in the builder's code that function is `signedControlEffect` (the four-field removal above, which already computes `controlEffectHash`), not `controlEffect`, which keeps `nextKeyAuthorizations`. On a `SUCCESSION_RULE_DECLARED` event the two are byte-equivalent, because only `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` admits `nextKeyAuthorizations` (Appendix B rows 4 and 26; the builder's byte test is recorded in Appendix D and DEVIATIONS), so no record, root or vector changes; A-187.3's test asserts the equality over every suite, and DEVIATIONS names the function used [A-187.2] [AR-09] [A-192.8].

`previousHead` is never `null` for a control event (position 0 is the genesis, which is not a control event; `null` is kept in the schema for the migration shadow context of §16.2 only). Failure is `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`. Because the chain head is signed, no control event can be moved (vector `rejects-moved-control-event`) or re-used in another history; because `effectHash` is signed, no field can change (`rejects-tampered-budget-declaration`).

**Owner policy and co-signatures (pointer).** [A-91.0] [A-92.0] [A-95.1] An owner epoch may carry an owner approval policy (`ownerPolicy` on the genesis or on an `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` event; §6.5.2); an absent policy on a rotation sets the default policy `{threshold: 1}` for the new epoch, and nothing is inherited across a rotation. Under a policy a ◆ event may carry `additionalControlAuthorizations` (1..63 further `{keyId, signature}` entries over the same `hashCanonical(challenge)`), excluded from the effect above, so the `/2` challenge is unchanged. The quorum, the classes and the counting of signers are §6.5.3 (failure `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`); the time rule T2 on ◆ events is §6.5.6; the two-phase writer for a quorum is §6.5.8. Without a policy the rule of this section is unchanged.

**Migrated prefix.** [A-40.1, as drafted] [A-52.1] Verification in the shadow context (replay rule, positions `1 … migratedFrom.position`): every `sourceControlAuthorization` MUST verify as an `irontrellis-control-authorization/1` authorization exactly as the rc.1-final format-1 verifier checks it (HF1 §7) — under the owner-key epochs of `migratedFrom.genesis.ownerKeys` (no rotation can occur in the prefix, §16.2 last paragraph / HF1 §11; complete under A-63), over the source challenge with the shadow head before it (the reconstructed un-transcribed event and `shadowHashes`, §16.2 step 3). Failure is `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID {position}`. Anchors inside the prefix need no exclusion (they do not change keys). In a native history any `sourceControlAuthorization` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` [A-35.1].

**Writer contract.** [A-22.3] A power-reducing control event (P.5) is written with `timestamp = max(now, head.canonicalTime)` instead of refusing with `CLOCK_INVALID`, and the result reports `CLOCK_BEHIND_HEAD {skewMs}`; other writes keep rc.1 behaviour. [A-24.1] [A-176] The witness option `MAX_UNWITNESSED` MUST NOT refuse a power-reducing control event (P.5) **or a `STATE_CHECKPOINT`** (the L12 route of A-119.2 must stay open under lapse). [A-4.3] A writer MUST NOT omit, defer or approximate the state root of any attestation or checkpoint to meet a budget; a writer with no owner signer carries the last valid attestation in the pin (rc.1 `store.ts`) and needs no root for that pin.

### 6.2 Session authorization (session-signed events, ◇)
Each ◇ event carries a *session binding* `{runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch}` (inside the type's authorization record or, for admissions, the proof) and a signature by that session's credential over `hashCanonical(challenge)`, where the challenge is the type's structure in §8 or §10–§11. The time rules T1 (under `ATTESTED`) and T3 (under a genesis `sessionTimeHorizonMs`) of §6.5.6 are envelope checks: they read only the event's timestamp, the policy in force and the `ownerTime` index, never the session, and are applied immediately after `EVENT_TIME_REGRESSION` and before every check of this section, so a fenced session crossing a statement expiry or the horizon is `TRANSITION_INVALID`, not `SESSION_FENCED` [A-172.1]. Replay requires, in this order:
1. the session exists and its `credentialKeyId` matches;
2. **epoch fence** [A-29] [A-58.5]: the session record's `controlEpoch` equals its agent's current `controlEpoch`, else `SESSION_FENCED`. This check runs first, immediately after the session and `credentialKeyId` are found, for every ◇ type (admissions and types 14–16 included) and for the outer ▲ session of an administrative event; the §13.2 finding attester session keeps its own §13.2 codes;
3. the session's agent is active with `currentControlEpoch == controlEpoch` of the binding (after the fence this catches only a binding whose `controlEpoch` differs from the session record's);
4. the session is not expired at the event's `timestamp`: `timestamp < expiresAt`. `expiresAt` is `null` only in a migrated prefix session (§16.2); at every position after the prefix such a session is treated as expired for every purpose — its events fail this check with the type's code, and it does not count as an unexpired session when a fresh finite-expiry session for the same agent and epoch is admitted [A-62.3] — [A-22.1] [A-52.2] [A-56.16];
5. the recorded `hash` (where present) equals the recomputed challenge hash;
6. the signature verifies under the session's credential and scheme (§6.3).

Failure codes: `DECLARATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (§8.6), `REFUSAL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (§10.1), `EFFECT_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (§10.2), `RECORD_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (counterparty wrapper §11.2 and lifecycle records §8.8 [A-51.4]); admissions keep `TRANSITION_INVALID` with the rc.1-final reasons; the fence is always `SESSION_FENCED`. The code `AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` does not exist in format 2 [A-51.4]. With types 14–16 signed, every non-control event's timestamp is bounded by its session's `expiresAt` [A-22.2] (and by T1 and T3 where they apply [A-179.5]).

### 6.3 Suites
`ed25519` signs the 32 raw hash bytes (RFC 8032, no prefix); `eip191-personal-sign-keccak256` recovers over `keccak256(0x19‖"Ethereum Signed Message:\n32"‖hash)` (HF1 §8.1). Default for owner keys, sessions and counterparties is `ed25519`. EIP-191 is an interop suite: allowed for owner keys and runtime sessions, NOT allowed for counterparty keys (§11.1).

**Ed25519 verification profile (format 2, stricter than HF1 §8.1).** [A-32.1, as drafted, modified by G2] In format 2, `ed25519` verification is RFC 8032 §5.1.7 as written (cofactorless: `[S]B = R + [k]A` checked exactly), with these checks performed by the engine before any library call: `S < L`; `R` and `A` decode to canonical encodings (`y < p`, no non-canonical sign bit for `x = 0`); `A` is not of small order. A conforming format-2 verifier MUST itself be cofactorless (RFC 8032 §5.1.7 exact equation) **and** perform these pre-checks; the pre-checks do not make a cofactored library conforming. A verifier that applies cofactored (ZIP-215-style) verification is not conforming for format 2. §6.3's "unchanged from HF1 §8.1" is overridden for format 2 (stricter). Keys are checked at declaration per §3.3 (A-32.2).

**Key roles (writer rules of the owner API, `INVALID_INPUT`; not replay rules).** [A-32.3, as drafted, modified by G2] `admitSession` and `registerCounterparty` refuse a credential equal to any owner key, any session credential, or another counterparty's credential already in the history; re-registering a RETIRED counterparty with a `keyId` previously bound to a different credential, or with a credential previously retired under another `keyId`, is refused unless the caller passes `allowKeyRebind: true`. "Equal" means same `scheme` and same lowercase credential string (§3.3). `verify` and `inspect` list every credential that appears under more than one role or `keyId` ("credential reuse"), and every session or counterparty whose key fails the §3.3 declaration test in a legacy or format-1 history (reported, never refused there — A-28). (Informative, [A-32.5, as drafted]: "the owner signs both the session admission and the counterparty registration, so the threat is owner misconfiguration, not an attacker; §11.1 already states that a RETIRED re-registration 'replaces its keys', and a replay rule could refuse a pinned history the assistant has not inspected.") *Under an owner policy (§6.5), the refusals of A-110.2 as rewritten by A-117.3–A-117.4 — a commitment to, an admission of, or a registration of a committed, owner or attester credential — are replay rules (`TRANSITION_INVALID` at rows 8 and 36; `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`/`TRANSITION_INVALID` at rows 0 and 26); every other key-role refusal stays a writer rule.* (§6.5.7; the rule is guarded by `P_w`, and no pinned history carries a policy, so A-32.5's concern is met.) [A-173.1]

**Regenerated-suite negatives.** [A-32.4, as drafted] The identity key, the order-2 key, a non-canonical `A`, `S = L`, and a session admitted with a key equal to an owner key (writer refusal, tested at the API). No vector or pinned root changes (every frozen key is an ordinary key; the builder confirms by running the suite with points 1–2 enabled).

**Dedicated keys.** [A-47.4, as drafted] CUSTODY and SECURITY state: "Format-2 Ed25519 signatures are over raw 32-byte hashes with no context string. Owner, session and counterparty keys MUST be dedicated to this protocol; a forwarded SSH agent or a shared signing service that signs arbitrary 32-byte values for another protocol is a signing oracle for IronTrellis challenges, and the reverse." [A-94.2] An anchor publisher's chain key is never an owner key (this dedicated-key rule); a committee that holds an owner key holds one signature toward the quorum `M` of §6.5.3, like any key.

### 6.4 Threshold keys
An owner key is "whatever verifies as one RFC 8032 Ed25519 public key" under the §6.3 profile. A FROST or other threshold scheme that produces standard Ed25519 signatures needs no format change; its orchestration lives in the custody layer (`packages/core/src/custody`), outside this specification. Core never learns the share count. [A-48.2, as drafted] ARCH §7 states: "FROST nonce safety (no nonce reuse across sessions, no parallel signing of different messages with one nonce set) belongs to the custody layer; Core never sees shares or nonces."

### 6.5 Owner approval policy
[A-91.0] [A-92.0] [A-186.2] An owner epoch (§7.2 `ownerKeyEpoch`) may carry an **owner approval policy**, `ownerPolicy`: the genesis sets epoch 0's policy; every accepted `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` event sets the policy of the epoch it opens; an absent `ownerPolicy` on a rotation sets the **default policy** `{threshold: 1}` for the new epoch, and no policy is inherited across a rotation — every rotation restates it or returns to the default. On a rotation the policy sits in `data`, so it is bound by `effectHash` (§6.1) and by the chain everywhere. Under a policy a ◆ event may carry `additionalControlAuthorizations` (§6.1, §6.5.3). Every rule of this section is conditioned on the presence of `ownerPolicy` (in the event or in the epoch record in force), of `additionalControlAuthorizations`, of `humanFloor = "ATTESTED"` or of `recovery`, except rule T3 of §6.5.6, which is conditioned on a non-null genesis `sessionTimeHorizonMs` (§5.1) [A-179.3 (A-22.6)] [AR-09]; a history that carries none of them replays exactly as it did without this section, and no frozen vector, pinned root or A-71 vector changes [A-91.0] [A-92.0]. The subsections are: 6.5.1 Terms; 6.5.2 Policy schema and validity; 6.5.3 Quorum, classes and counting; 6.5.4 HF1 §7.1–7.2 as read for format 2; 6.5.5 Human attestation; 6.5.6 Time rules T1, T2, T3; 6.5.7 Credential separation; 6.5.8 Writer contract and API; 6.5.9 Tests and checker defaults [A-186.2]. The invariants the rules are meant to hold are Appendix F; the ceremony, SECURITY and OPERATOR statements are Appendix G [A-186.2].

#### 6.5.1 Terms
The terms below are used by §6.5, §7.3–§7.5, §15, §18, Appendix A and Appendix F. Unless a rule says otherwise, `e` is the event under replay at position `p ≥ 1`, and quantifiers range over accepted histories.

- **States and times.** [G6.2 Terms] `σ(p)` is the replay state (every §7.2 record) before the event at `p`; `σ(p+1)` the state after it. `τ(p)` is the head's `canonicalTime` before `p`, that is `e_{p−1}.timestamp`. Timestamps are non-decreasing (`e_p.timestamp ≥ τ(p)`, §5).
- **Epochs and policies.** [G6.1 Terms] [G6.2 Terms] [A-91.4] The epoch in force at `p` is the `ownerKeyEpoch` record with the greatest `fromPosition ≤ p`; a rotation accepted at `r` opens the epoch with `fromPosition = r + 1`. `K = K(p)` is the keys of that epoch (the keys in force at `p`, HF1 §7.1); `D = D(p)` is its next-key commitment, `nextKeyDigests ?? []`, read as a set; `P_w(p)` is its `policy` field **as written** (absent, or the object); `P = P(p) = P_w(p) ?? {threshold: 1}` is the **policy in force**, the policy **before** the event, evaluated for every ◆ event at `p` including an `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` itself [A-91.4]; `t_set(p)` is the timestamp of the event that opened the epoch in force at `p` (the genesis for epoch 0). The resulting epoch of a rotation at `p` carries the rotation's own `ownerPolicy`, or the default, from the position HF1 §7.1 names for the new keys [A-91.4]. A **committed epoch** is one whose record's `nextKeyDigests` is non-null (equivalently `D ≠ ∅`, since C4 requires 1..64 entries) [G6.2 Terms] [A-175.2].
- **"Present" means non-null.** [A-175.2] Wherever this section, Appendix A or Appendix F says that a `nextKeyDigests` is "present", it means non-null; the `ownerKeyEpoch` record keeps `nextKeyDigests: [ContentHash]|null` (§7.2), and Appendix F's model shorthand `nextKeyDigests?` is read the same way.
- **Keys and credentials.** [G6.1 Terms] [A-114.0] A key is `{keyId, scheme, credential}`. `cred(k)` is the §3.3 credential identity `(scheme, lowercase(credential))`; `digest(k)` is the HF1 §7.2 `ownerKeyDigest` (`irontrellis-owner-key-digest/1`), a function of `cred(k)`. `creds(K)` and `keyIds(K)` are the sets of `cred(k)` and of `keyId` over the members of `K`. `revealed(e)` is the set of entries of `e.data.ownerKeys` whose `keyId` is not in `keyIds(K(p))`.
- **Signatures, admissibility and counting (defined in §6.5.3 and §6.5.4).** [A-92.1] [A-97.2] [A-107.1] [A-107.2] [A-110.1] [G6.2 Terms] `S(e)` is the list `[controlAuthorization] ++ (additionalControlAuthorizations ?? [])`; `adm(e)` is the admissible signer set (§6.5.4); an entry of `S(e)` **resolves** to the unique key of `adm(e)` with its `keyId`, or to none. `M_eff(e)` and `M_rot(P)` are the effective thresholds (§6.5.3); `cls` is the single total class function (§6.5.3); `C(e) ⊆ S(e)` is the counting set (§6.5.3). `isRecovery(e)` (§6.5.4) and `isRestoration(e)` (§6.5.5) are structural predicates decided from `e.data`, `S(e)` and the epoch record before any signature is verified; `isCheckpoint(e) := e.type = STATE_CHECKPOINT`; `G(e, p)` is the policy guard of §6.5.4; `X(e) := PR_eff(e, σ(p)) ∨ isRestoration(e) ∨ isCheckpoint(e)` is the counting exemption of §6.5.3 [A-107.2]. `isAtt(P) := P.humanFloor = "ATTESTED"`; an absent `humanFloor` has `DECLARED` semantics but differs from the written value `"DECLARED"` as written [A-114.0] [A-112].
- **Power-reducing sets.** [A-116.1] [A-116.2] [G6.2 Terms] `PR_types` is the set of **seven** type names of §6.5.3; `PR_eff(e, σ)` is the per-event predicate of §6.5.3, decided from `e.data` and `σ(p)`; `LOW_types = PR_types ∪ {STATE_CHECKPOINT}`. Every rule of this section that speaks of power reduction uses `PR_eff` (per event) or `PR_types` (per type name). The P.5 list *Power-reducing types* (eight names) is **not** redefined: it serves the writer and witness rules A-22.3, A-24.1 and A-92.4 only, and harmonization is E-26 [G6.2 Terms] [A-176.1].
- **Attestation terms.** [A-107.1] [G6.3 Terms] `stmt(P, c)` is the statement of `P.attestations` whose `subject` credential is `c` (at most one, §6.5.2 rule 4); `started(P, c, t) := stmt(P, c)` exists `∧ stmt.notBefore ≤ t`; `attCreds(P)` is the set of `cred` of `P.attesters` (empty when absent); `H(p) := max{a.expiresAt : a ∈ P(p).attestations}` is the **attestation horizon**, defined whenever `isAtt(P(p))` (§6.5.2's policy-time rule (a) requires at least one statement); `hc(e) := |{s ∈ C(e) : cls(s) ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED}}|` is the number of **human-class counting signers**.
- **Credential separation terms.** [G6.3 Terms] [A-117.1] `foreign(σ)` is the set of `cred` of every `session` record of `σ` (whatever its `expiresAt` or its agent's epoch or status: session records are never removed) and of every key of every `counterparty` record of `σ` (whatever its `status`). It is a function of the §7.2 records alone, so a checkpoint resume evaluates it exactly as full replay does; replay keeps it as a derived index, not a record and not in the state root (§7.1, §6.5.7) [A-117.1]. A counterparty key replaced by re-registration leaves `foreign` (Appendix F item 43). `introduced(e, p) := set(e.data.nextKeyDigests ?? []) \ D(p)` is the set of digests a rotation adds (at the genesis, every digest).
- **Budget and authority terms used by `PR_eff`.** [G6.3 Terms] `MAXT := 2^53 − 1`. `rollsOver(B) := B.period.kind = "FIXED_WINDOW" ∧ B.period.carryUnused = true` (a budget to which §9.5 applies). `anc(A', σ)` is the set of authority records reached from `A'` by following `grant.parentAuthorityId` through `σ.authority` (the chain stops at a record without `parentAuthorityId` or at a dangling id); a GLOBAL prohibition has no parent and an empty `anc`.
- **Units.** [G6.2 Terms] [A-109.2] Every timestamp, `notBefore`, `expiresAt`, window and interval of this section is in **milliseconds**, as U53 (`0 … 2^53−1`), one unit everywhere; "30 days" is `2,592,000,000`.
- **"As written".** [G6.1 Terms] [G6.2 Terms] [A-112] Canonical-encoding equality of the field: an absent field differs from every present value; maps compare as their canonical key-sorted entry lists; arrays compare in order, except where a rule says "as a set". `labels` and `acknowledged` take part in the comparison (§6.5.2).

#### 6.5.2 Policy schema and validity
**Schema (closed; shape failures `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` at C4, Appendix A A.2).** [A-91.1] [A-97.1] [A-93.1] [A-107.4] On the closed `data` of `DEPLOYMENT_INITIALIZED` (Appendix B row 0) and of `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` (row 26):
```
ownerPolicy?: {
  threshold:     U53, 1..64,
  byType?:       { [eventType: string]: U53 1..64 },      // ≥ 1 key; each key is the name of a ◆ type of §5 (types 1–11, 20, 25–28, 32–37)
  keyClasses?:   { [keyId: Identifier]: "HUMAN" | "AGENT" }, // ≥ 1 key
  labels?:       { [keyId: Identifier]: string },          // ≥ 1 key; 1..64 Unicode scalar values (A-89.2 measure)
  acknowledged?: "SINGLE_SIGNER",
  recovery?:     "COMMITTED_NEXT_KEYS",                    // §6.5.4 [A-97.1]
  humanFloor?:   "DECLARED" | "ATTESTED",                  // §6.5.5 [A-93.1]
  attesters?:    [{keyId: Identifier, scheme: "ed25519", credential: PublicKey32}], 1..16,   // [A-93.1]
  attestations?: [HumanAttestation], 1..64                 // [A-93.1]
}
```
Unknown keys, an empty object or map, a value outside its range, a `null` anywhere in `ownerPolicy`, or a `$continuity.` key are `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` (§3.1) [A-91.1]. The `1..64` ranges are checked at C4; the `byType` key names are validity rule 3 below, after control authorization on row 26 — the same code either way, and the order is stated [A-102 X-9]. `labels` and `acknowledged` are **annotations**: replay checks their shape and the cross-field rules below, compares them as written for the policy-rotation test of §6.5.4 (a change of either is a policy rotation), and never consults them for authorization [A-112]. `recovery` is replay-significant: it is part of the policy, so enabling or disabling it is a policy rotation (§6.5.4) or a key rotation, authorized by the pre-event quorum with agents counting zero [A-97.1]. `humanFloor` absent has today's semantics (a key is human by label, `"DECLARED"`); `"ATTESTED"` is the strict profile under which only attested keys are human for counting purposes (§6.5.3, §6.5.5) [A-93.1]. `attesters` are the trust roots the epoch accepts: each credential is subject to the §3.3 declaration test, and `keyId`s and credentials are pairwise distinct [A-93.1].

**`HumanAttestation` (inside `attestations`).** [A-93.1] [A-107.4]
```
HumanAttestation = {
  version:       "irontrellis-human-attestation/1",
  audience:      <genesis domain: {protocol: "irontrellis", version: "2", deploymentId}>,
  subject:       {keyId: Identifier, scheme: "ed25519", credential: PublicKey32},
  profile:       Identifier,            // the pinned ceremony profile name; opaque to Core
  attesterKeyId: Identifier,            // names an entry of attesters
  evidenceDigest: ContentHash,          // sha256 commitment to the external evidence bundle
  notBefore:     U53, expiresAt: U53,   // notBefore < expiresAt
  historyId?:    ContentHash,           // recordHash(0) of this history; rule 3 below [A-107.4]
  signature:     Signature              // attester's Ed25519 over hashCanonical(statement without signature), §6.3 profile
}
```
The signed body is the attestation object **without** `signature` (Appendix C). The body carries no biometric material, no personal identifier and, apart from `historyId`, no history hash: a pre-genesis statement binds `audience` to the chosen `domain`, which is known before the genesis exists, and the genesis then commits the statement, so there is no hash cycle [A-93.1]. `evidenceDigest = sha256(bundle)` over the bundle as an opaque octet string; the ceremony profile named by `profile` defines the bundle's serialization. It is report-level only: replay never reads the bundle (§6.5.5) [A-102 X-13].

**Validity at the setting event (cross-field; `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`).** [A-91.2] Let `K` be the `ownerKeys` of the same event (the resulting epoch's keys), `N` the number of **distinct credentials** (§3.3 credential equality) among `K`, and `P'` the resulting policy. On row 0 these rules are in the genesis validity list; on row 26 they run after control authorization, T2 and the §3.3 key test, and before the HF1 §7.1–7.2 readings of §6.5.4 (Appendix A) [A-102 X-9] [A-170.7].
1. `threshold ≤ N`; every `byType` value `≤ N`.
2. `threshold ≥ 1` and every `byType` value `≥ 1` (the floor; `0` is not a Core value — "0" in an installer means "no co-signers", which is `threshold: 1`, and for non-Core gates it means "no gate", which Core never sees). (Satisfied by the C4 ranges of the schema; stated for completeness, A-102 X-9.) [A-170.5]
3. Every `byType` key names a ◆ type of §5 (the type literal, e.g. `"AUTHORITY_GRANTED"`); the genesis, ◇ and ▲ types and unknown names are refused.
4. `byType.OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED`, when present, is `≥ threshold`. The policy-changing action is never easier than the default. `M_rot(P) = byType.OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED ?? threshold` is therefore always `≥ threshold`.
5. Every `keyClasses` key and every `labels` key is the `keyId` of a member of `K`. A member without a `keyClasses` entry is class `HUMAN` **by declaration** (a label, not evidence; §6.5.5 distinguishes declared from attested). When `ownerPolicy` is present, the credentials of the resulting `ownerKeys` are pairwise distinct (`cred` equality); a credential under two `keyId`s is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`, so `N = |K|` whenever a policy is set [A-99.0].
6. At least one member of `K` is not `AGENT`; and the number of distinct credentials among non-`AGENT` members of `K` is `≥ M_rot(P')`. (No lock-out: the resulting policy can be changed by humans alone. Consequence: with `H` human keys, `threshold ≤ H`; an "agent at M=1" is not refused at policy time — it is simply a key that can authorize nothing alone, §6.5.3 — and `verify` lists any agent key that can count toward no type.)
7. `acknowledged: "SINGLE_SIGNER"` requires `threshold = 1` **and `byType` absent** [A-102 X-19].
8. The attestation rules below (rules 0–5 and the policy-time rules (a)–(c)) for `humanFloor`, `attesters` and `attestations` [A-93.2].
9. A migrated genesis (`migratedFrom` present) MUST NOT carry `ownerPolicy`: the prefix is verified under the default policy and `/1` authorizations (§6.1); the first native rotation after the prefix may set one. The §16.2 migration options MUST NOT set `ownerPolicy`; `migrate` prints that a policy is set by the first native rotation [A-91.5] [A-170.5].
10. For every `T ∈ PR_types` with `byType[T]` present: `byType[T] ≤` the number of distinct credentials of `K` that are not classed `AGENT` (class by credential, A-99.0). With rule 6 this gives `M_eff(T) ≤` non-agent credentials for every `T ∈ PR_types` in every epoch: no agent key is ever needed to reduce power [A-106.3] [A-116.2].
11. `byType[T] < threshold` is permitted only for `T ∈ LOW_types = PR_types ∪ {STATE_CHECKPOINT}` (rule 4 already forbids it for type 26). A threshold below the default exists so that fewer humans can reduce power or write an operational root, never for anything else; `ANCHOR_RECORDED` is not in `LOW_types` [A-116.2].

A rotation that shrinks `N` re-validates every threshold against the new `N` (rule 1 is stated on the resulting keys). The pre-event policy `P(p)` decides whether the rotation itself is authorized (§6.5.3); the resulting policy is validated by these rules. Both must hold [A-91.2] [A-91.4].

**Attestation validity at the setting event (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`; evaluated as rule 8).** [A-93.2] Let `t_set` be the setting event's `timestamp`, `K` the resulting `ownerKeys`, `D` the resulting `nextKeyDigests ?? []`, `P'` the resulting policy, and `carried(a', p)` mean `a' ∈ P(p).attestations` as written (at the genesis nothing is carried).
0. The §3.3 declaration test and the pairwise distinctness of `attesters` (`keyId`s and credentials) are checked here, before rule 1, after control authorization on row 26 [A-102 X-10].
1. `attestations` present ⇒ `attesters` present. `humanFloor: "ATTESTED"` ⇒ both present.
2. No `attesters` credential equals a credential of `K` (an owner key never attests itself or a co-owner; §6.3's dedicated-key rule names attester keys too) [A-93.2]; no `attesters` credential's `digest` is a member of `D` (an attester never attests itself, now or after it is revealed) [A-97.5]; no `attesters` credential is in `foreign(σ(p))` [A-117.4]; and under `humanFloor = "ATTESTED"`, `|attesters| ≥ 2` (independence of attesters stays a ceremony fact, Appendix G; cardinality is Core's) [A-112]. These checks fire at policy validity, first; the separate digest-rule check of §6.5.4 (`TRANSITION_INVALID`, over the digests a rotation introduces) is not dropped, because the two differ in domain [A-173.2].
3. For each attestation `a'`: `audience` equals the genesis `domain`; `attesterKeyId` names an `attesters` entry of `P'` (a carried statement whose `attesterKeyId` no longer names an attester of the resulting policy fails here and must be dropped with its attester [A-118.1]); `signature` verifies under that attester (a signature that fails is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`, the A-40.2 precedent for an embedded attestation); `subject` equals a member of `K` exactly (`keyId`, `scheme`, `credential`) **or** its `irontrellis-owner-key-digest/1` digest is a member of `D` (a pre-attested next key); `notBefore < expiresAt`; `expiresAt > t_set` (an already-expired statement is never useful and is refused) — **waived for every statement with `carried(a', p)`, on every type-26 event** (policy rotation, restoration, key rotation, recovery rotation), and applied in full to every statement that is not carried; a carried expired statement makes its subject `LAPSED` in the new epoch and nothing more [A-118.1]; the subject is not classed `AGENT` by `keyClasses` (a contradiction is refused, never resolved by precedence). **`historyId`:** at position 0 it MUST be absent (the value does not exist yet); for a statement in a policy set at `p > 0` that is not carried, `historyId` MUST be present and equal `recordHash(0)` of this history; a carried statement keeps its original form. Two histories sharing a `domain` therefore share pre-genesis statements only (stated limit, Appendix F item 40) [A-107.4].
4. At most one attestation per `subject` credential.
5. **Policy-time rules under the strict floor.** When `P'.humanFloor = "ATTESTED"`: (a) the number of distinct credentials of `K` whose statement in `P'` satisfies `notBefore ≤ t_set < expiresAt` is `≥ M_rot(P')` and `≥ 1` (next keys do not count; a policy whose only statements start later is refused) [A-107.5]; (b) for every `T ∈ PR_types` with `P'.byType[T]` present: `P'.byType[T] ≤ |{c ∈ creds(K) : started(P', c, t_set)}|` (statements with `notBefore ≤ t_set`, carried expired ones included, not-yet-valid ones excluded) [A-118.3]; (c) other unreachable `byType` values are listed by `verify` ("types unreachable under the attested set of epoch n"), not refused [A-107.5]. `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` for (a) and (b). Because a restoration rotation (§6.5.5) carries or renews every subject, and a carried statement keeps its `notBefore ≤` the old `t_set ≤` the new while a renewal has `notBefore ≤ e.timestamp`, (b) never blocks a restoration that (a) admits; and at every `t ≥ t_set` each credential counted by (b) is `HUMAN` or `LAPSED` (a started statement is valid or expired, never `NONE`), so `≥ byType[T]` human-class credentials can count on every `PR_eff` event without an agent (Appendix F invariant 32) [A-107.5] [A-118.3].

Attestations are **prospective**: they take effect for events after the setting event, through the epoch record. A declared-`HUMAN` key with no attestation is valid under `DECLARED` and counts for nothing under `ATTESTED`; an omitted class or missing attestation never satisfies the strict floor. The first activation (`DECLARED` → `ATTESTED`) is authorized by the pre-event declared policy and must itself satisfy rule (a); a disabling rotation (`ATTESTED` → `DECLARED`) requires the old strict quorum of §6.5.3 and is permitted by Core (whether the product forbids it is a ceremony-policy choice). Every change of attesters, attestations, floor or classes is a policy rotation (§6.5.4) and therefore counts no agent (§6.5.3) [A-93.2]. **Stated rc.2 limitation:** an attestation `subject` is `ed25519`; an `eip191-personal-sign-keccak256` owner key cannot be attested, so under `ATTESTED` it is `NONE` unless classed `AGENT` (it has no statement and no class by credential match), and `verify` lists "owner keys of a scheme the attestation profile cannot name"; a later addendum may add such a subject scheme (E-22) [A-102 X-12].

#### 6.5.3 Quorum, classes and counting (`M_eff`, `PR_types`, `PR_eff`, `cls`)
**The field.** [A-92.0] Every ◆ type (Appendix B rows 1–11, 20, 25–28, 32–37) may carry `additionalControlAuthorizations?: [{keyId: Identifier, signature: Signature}]`, 1..63 entries. It is excluded from the §6.1 effect beside `controlAuthorization`, `nextKeyAuthorizations` and `sourceControlAuthorization`, so the `/2` challenge is unchanged and every co-signer signs the identical `hashCanonical(challenge)` of §6.1; an event carrying it is nonetheless fully committed by the chain, since `eventHash` covers the whole event. Its shape is C4 (Appendix A A.2); inside a migrated prefix it is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` at C4, coded against `migratedFrom` [A-91.5].

**Terms.** [A-92.1] [A-106.2] [A-116.2] For a ◆ event `e` at `p` with pre-event policy `P = P(p)`:
- `M_rot(P) = P.byType.OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED ?? P.threshold` (§6.5.2 rule 4: always `≥ P.threshold`).
- `M_eff(e)`: for `e.type ∈ PR_types` with `P.byType[e.type]` present and `< P.threshold` and `¬PR_eff(e, σ(p))`, `M_eff(e) = P.threshold`; in every other case `M_eff(e) = P.byType[e.type] ?? P.threshold` (for type 26, `M_eff(e) = M_rot(P)`). A lowered `byType` value therefore applies to an event of a `PR_types` type only when that event reduces power by effect: a cap-raising `BUDGET_UPDATED` under `{threshold: 2, byType: {BUDGET_UPDATED: 1}}` needs two signatures and, by step 2 below, the array [A-106.2] [A-116.2].
- `S = S(e)`, the list `[controlAuthorization] ++ (additionalControlAuthorizations ?? [])`.
- The **counting set** `C = C(e) ⊆ S` and the **floor**, as the counting rule below gives them; agents never count on a policy change, and every type 26 is a policy change (§6.5) [A-92.1] [A-107.2].

**Replay rule (control authorization under a policy; code `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` throughout).** [A-92.2] [A-97.2] [A-107.2] Before any signature is verified or counted, `M_eff(e)`, `PR_eff(e, σ(p))`, `isCheckpoint(e)` and, for type 26, `isRestoration(e)`, `isRecovery(e)` and `adm(e)` are decided from `e.data`, the `keyId`s of `S(e)`, the epoch record and `σ(p)` ("step 0", Appendix A) [A-116.1] [A-110.1]. Then, in order, first failure wins:
1. `controlAuthorization` verifies as in §6.1: its `keyId` resolves in the admissible signer set `adm(e)` of §6.5.4 (the keys in force at `p`, extended on a recovery rotation only) and its signature verifies over the §6.1 challenge under the §6.3 profile [A-97.2].
2. **Presence:** `additionalControlAuthorizations` is present iff `M_eff(e) ≥ 2`. The condition depends on policy state, and C4 stays state-free, so a stray array under the default policy is `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (the M1 code `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` for "extra array under M=1" is superseded). The reading is the **event's effective threshold**, not the global default: a policy `{threshold: 1, byType: {AUTHORITY_GRANTED: 2}}` requires the array on grants and forbids it everywhere else.
3. **Each entry** of `additionalControlAuthorizations` verifies exactly as `controlAuthorization` does: the same admissible signer set `adm(e)`, the same challenge hash, the §6.3 profile. An entry whose key is not in `adm(e)`, or whose signature does not verify, fails here. Surplus entries are permitted but must verify: the array never carries a signature that does not check. C4 accepts either §3.3 `Signature` form; a well-formed signature of the wrong scheme for the named key fails here [A-102 X-14].
4. **Distinctness:** the `keyId`s of `S` are pairwise distinct **and** their credentials are pairwise distinct (§3.3 credential equality). A repeated key counts once and is refused, not deduplicated. A revealed key's credential is never one the history declared (HF1 §7.1), so a recovery signer can never alias a current key [A-97.4].
5. **Quorum:** `|C| ≥ M_eff(e)`, with `C` the counting set below (for type 26, `|C| ≥ M_rot(P)`) [A-107.2] [A-97.4].
6. **Floor:** the floor condition of the counting rule below holds (under `DECLARED`, `C` contains at least one entry whose class is not `AGENT`; for type 26 this is implied by step 5 since `C` excludes agents). An agent key is never sufficient alone, at any `M_eff`, including `M_eff = 1` [A-107.2].

Then the time rule T2 (§6.5.6; under `ATTESTED`, immediately after this rule, because it reads `C(e)`) and the type's own checks continue as today — for row 26: the §3.3 key test with §6.5.4's foreign-key clause, the §6.5.2 validity of the resulting policy, the HF1 §7.1–7.2 readings of §6.5.4 including `nextKeyDigests`/`nextKeyAuthorizations`, then capacity step 1 [A-92.2] [A-170.7]. Every quorum, presence, distinctness and floor failure is `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`; every shape failure is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`; the §18 replay set stays at 26 [A-92.6]. The writer's two-phase contract and pre-check for this rule are §6.5.8 [A-92.4].

**Direction of "power" (binding for every rule below).** [A-106.0] Power is what the decision procedure allows an agent, session or counterparty to do, and what a key can authorize. A budget is a **stop rule** (§9: it only denies, §9.4; it matches by `subjects`, §9.2; it stops matching at `expiresAt`). Therefore raising a cap, **narrowing** `subjects`, moving `expiresAt` **earlier** or closing a budget all *expand* agent power; lowering a cap, widening `subjects`, moving `expiresAt` later and declaring a budget *reduce* it. `BUDGET_CLOSED` and `WITNESSES_UNBOUND` are never power-reducing by effect: closing a budget removes a stop rule; unbinding a witness removes rollback detection [A-106.0].

**`PR_types` and `LOW_types`.** [A-116.2] `PR_types = {AUTHORITY_REVOKED, CONTROL_EPOCH_ADVANCED, AGENT_TERMINATED, AGENT_UNAPPOINTED, BUDGET_DECLARED, BUDGET_UPDATED, COUNTERPARTY_RETIRED}` (seven). A type outside `PR_types` is never `PR_eff`. `LOW_types = PR_types ∪ {STATE_CHECKPOINT}` (§6.5.2 rule 11); `ANCHOR_RECORDED` is not in it, since it is in no `X(e)` disjunct and a lowered anchor threshold was unusable under lapse.

**`PR_eff(e, σ)` (exact predicate; decided from `e.data` and `σ = σ(p)` before any signature is verified or counted; `t = e.timestamp`).** [A-116.1] [A-127.2]

| `e.type` | `PR_eff(e, σ)` is true iff |
|---|---|
| `AUTHORITY_REVOKED` | `A = σ.authority[e.data.authorityId]` exists, `A.revoked = false`, `A.grant.kind = "PERMISSION"`, and no authority record `A'` of `σ` with `A'.revoked = false ∧ A'.grant.kind = "PROHIBITION"` has `A ∈ anc(A', σ)` (a prohibition delegated under the permission would lose effect under the descendants reading). Revoking a `PROHIBITION` (ROOT or GLOBAL) widens power and is never `PR_eff`. A dangling id yields `false` (the event then fails at the transition as today, or at control authorization if its signers needed the exemption). |
| `CONTROL_EPOCH_ADVANCED` | `σ.agent[e.data.agentId]` exists with `status = "ACTIVE"` and `controlEpoch = e.data.fromEpoch`, `e.data.toEpoch > e.data.fromEpoch`, and no `session` record of `σ` with `agentId = e.data.agentId` has `controlEpoch = e.data.toEpoch` (the fence then invalidates every session of the agent and revives none; where the engine's own precondition already guarantees this, the clause is vacuous and the builder says so in DEVIATIONS). |
| `AGENT_TERMINATED` | `σ.agent[e.data.agentId]` exists with `status = "ACTIVE"`, and no authority record `A'` of `σ` with `A'.revoked = false ∧ A'.grant.kind = "PROHIBITION"` has `e.data.agentId` equal to `A'.grant.grantorId`, or to the `grantorId` or `granteeId` of any `A ∈ anc(A', σ)` (an unrevoked prohibition must not die with its anchor path under a lowered threshold, Appendix F ASM-2; "live", where the prose of this row, §8.1.1 (e), F.3 H0 (e), F.4 ASM-2 and G.4 uses it of a prohibition, means unrevoked, exactly this clause, with no activity or window term, so that the row is decidable from `σ` alone; fence the agent instead, Appendix G) [A-127.2] [A-203.1]. |
| `AGENT_UNAPPOINTED` | with `a = e.data.agentId`, `r = e.data.roleId`: no `budget` record `B` of `σ` has all of: `B.status = "ACTIVE"`; `B.subjects.roleIds ≠ null ∧ r ∈ B.subjects.roleIds`; `B.subjects.actorIds = null ∨ a ∈ B.subjects.actorIds`; and not (`B.expiresAt ≠ null ∧ B.expiresAt ≤ t`). (A budget that can never match `a` through `r` at any time `≥ t` does not block; ending a tenure releases the agent from every budget selected by that role while it may keep acting under another role, §9.2, so when such a budget exists the unappointment needs the ordinary quorum. Terminating the agent is available instead.) |
| `BUDGET_DECLARED` | **always.** The event's own schema and transition rules (§9.1: caps, closed `Subjects`, unique `budgetId`, ≤ 256 budgets) are its preconditions; a declaration adds a budget to the matching set `M` of every request it selects, each budget only denies (§9.4) and `BUDGET_SET_TOO_LARGE` only denies; a new budget's rollover starts at `(firstPeriod, 0)`; no other record changes. |
| `BUDGET_UPDATED` | `B = σ.budget[e.data.budgetId]` exists with `status = "ACTIVE"`, `¬rollsOver(B)`, and with `new = e.data`, `old = B`: (caps) for each `c ∈ {amountCap, countCap, perOperationCap}`: `new.c = null ⇒ old.c = null`, and when both are non-null `new.c ≤ old.c`; (subjects) for each `s ∈ {actorIds, roleIds, actions, resources}`: `new.s = null`, or (`old.s ≠ null ∧ set(old.s) ⊆ set(new.s)`); (expiry) `old.expiresAt = null ⇒ new.expiresAt = null`, and when both are non-null `new.expiresAt ≥ old.expiresAt`. (No cap rises, no subject set narrows, the stop rule does not end earlier.
