For a budget that rolls over, §9.5 stores `carryBase` on every update, and an update before the budget's first period makes rollover accrue over periods the budget never had; such an update needs the ordinary quorum; a rollover update is never `PR_eff`, and E-27 does not change this [A-132] [A-192.3]. To tighten a rollover budget at a lowered threshold, declare a new non-rollover budget over the same group.) |
| `COUNTERPARTY_RETIRED` | `σ.counterparty[e.data.counterpartyId]` exists with `status = "ACTIVE"` (vacuous given the row's precondition; stated for the oracle). |
| every other type | never (`BUDGET_CLOSED`, `WITNESSES_UNBOUND`, `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED`, `AUTHORITY_GRANTED` included). |

`PR_eff` is a function of `(e.data, e.timestamp, σ(p))` (Appendix F invariant 31). Under the default policy `M_eff = 1` for every type and `PR_eff` is never consulted [A-116.1]. The non-expansion property that `PR_eff` events are meant to satisfy, and the five authority-procedure assumptions it relies on (ASM-1…5), are Appendix F [A-116.3] [A-116.4]; what the owner can therefore always reduce, with substitutes, is Appendix G [A-116.5].

**The class function `cls`.** [A-107.1] [A-118.4] [A-99.0] For a ◆ event `e` at `p`, `t = e.timestamp`, `P = P(p)`, `K = K(p)`, `D = D(p)`, and a credential `c`: let `agentCred(c) :=` some `keyId` of `K` with credential `c` has `P.keyClasses[keyId] = "AGENT"` (class is a function of the credential: a credential is `AGENT` if `P.keyClasses` classes **any** `keyId` of `K` carrying that credential as `AGENT`; redundant with §6.5.2 rule 5 inside a policy epoch, and stated so that a default-policy epoch needs no case [A-99.0]); let `stmt(c)` be `stmt(P, c)` (§6.5.1; its subject is in `K` or has its digest in `D` by policy validity). `cls(P, K, D, t, c) ∈ {HUMAN, AGENT, LAPSED, NONE}` is total over every credential:
- `P.humanFloor ∈ {absent, "DECLARED"}`: `cls = AGENT` if `agentCred(c)`, else `HUMAN` (a recovery signer, §6.5.4, is `HUMAN` by declaration; `LAPSED` and `NONE` never occur).
- `P.humanFloor = "ATTESTED"`: `cls = HUMAN` if `stmt(c)` exists and `stmt.notBefore ≤ t < stmt.expiresAt` (equality at `expiresAt` fails; `t` is the event's signed timestamp, which is determinism and not wall-clock truth); else `AGENT` if `agentCred(c)`; else `LAPSED` if `stmt(c)` exists and `t ≥ stmt.expiresAt`; else `NONE` (no statement, or `t < stmt.notBefore`: a far-future statement confers nothing until it starts). The `AGENT` branch is unreachable for a key with a statement (§6.5.2 rule 3 refuses the contradiction); it is kept so that the function is total.
`cls` depends on `(P, K, D, t, c)` only, never on a `keyId` and never on the event [A-99.0]. Whether a signer entry resolves at all is steps 1 and 3 of the replay rule (`adm(e)`), decided before `cls` is applied; `cls` is applied only to resolved signers (`cls(s) = cls(P(p), K(p), D(p), e.timestamp, cred(k))` for the entry's key `k`), to pin signers (§7.4) and to witness-statement signers (§7.4) [A-118.4]. A lapsed pre-attested next key on a recovery rotation is therefore `LAPSED`; it never counts there, and `verify`, the witness result and Appendix F list it as `LAPSED` [A-107.1]. A key whose statement was removed by a policy rotation is `NONE` in the new epoch: revocation is an act of the quorum and is final; lapse is passive and recoverable (§6.5.5).

**Counting.** [A-107.2] For a ◆ event `e` at `p` with `S = S(e)`, resolved entries classed by `cls`, and the exemption `X(e) := PR_eff(e, σ(p)) ∨ isRestoration(e) ∨ isCheckpoint(e)`:
- under `humanFloor ∈ {absent, "DECLARED"}`: `e.type = OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED`: `C = {s ∈ S : cls(s) ≠ AGENT}`; otherwise `C = S`, floor: `C` contains ≥ 1 entry with `cls ≠ AGENT`;
- under `"ATTESTED"`: `e.type = 26 ∧ ¬isRestoration(e)`: `C = {s : cls(s) = HUMAN}`; `e.type = 26 ∧ isRestoration(e)`: `C = {s : cls(s) ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED}}`; `e.type ≠ 26 ∧ X(e)`: `C = {s : cls(s) ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED, AGENT}}`, floor: ≥ 1 `HUMAN` or `LAPSED`; `e.type ≠ 26 ∧ ¬X(e)`: `C = {s : cls(s) ∈ {HUMAN, AGENT}}`, floor: ≥ 1 `HUMAN`.
Then `|C| ≥ M_eff(e)` (step 5). `NONE` counts nowhere; `AGENT` never satisfies a floor and never counts on type 26; for type 26 no separate floor check exists ("floor implied": `M_rot ≥ 1` and `C ⊆ {HUMAN, LAPSED}`). Non-counting signatures of every class remain permitted in the array, must verify (step 3), and are listed by `verify` as non-counting [A-107.2]. A lapsed key (a key the epoch's own policy attested, whose statement has expired at `t`) thereby keeps the power to reduce (a `PR_eff` event), to renew (a restoration rotation, §6.5.5) and to sign a `STATE_CHECKPOINT`, which adds no authority; it gains nothing else [A-107.2].

**What the rule does and does not change.** [A-92.3] [A-174.1] [A-112]
- `nextKeyDigests` and `nextKeyAuthorizations` (HF1 §7.2) are unchanged except as §6.5.4 reads them. The admissible signer set for a rotation's `controlAuthorization` is `adm(e)` of §6.5.4, and step 3 admits the same set for every co-signature — no wider, no narrower. A deployment with `recovery` and `M_rot ≥ 2` MUST commit at least `M_rot` next-key digests or accept that recovery needs `M_rot − |committed|` surviving current keys (A-92.3's sentence, made unconditional by the opt-in of §6.5.4; stated once here, and §6.5.4 points to it) [A-97.4]. A signer admitted by HF1 that has no `keyClasses` entry is `HUMAN` by declaration; the ceremony MUST commit next keys under the same class discipline as current keys (Appendix G states this; `verify` cannot check it; under the `ATTESTED` floor a next key counts only if pre-attested, which closes the route for strict deployments) [A-92.3] [A-97.3].
- Unanimous new-member consent, agent-only recovery, unsigned emergency rotation, automatic `M` reduction and any relabelling of `AGENT` to `HUMAN` without a policy rotation are **not** Core behaviour.
- Head attestations (§7.4), witness head statements, pins, anchor-log entries and the genesis stay single-key objects; they are not converted to M-of-N [A-174.1]. Two consequences are ruled: (a) **Resume roots:** a `/2` head attestation signed by a key whose class under `P(head.position)` is `AGENT` is **not** a resume root (§7.5 step 3 refuses it; the reader falls back to `FULL_REPLAY` with the reason reported); it still validates a prefix and still serves rollback detection; the full resume-root rule, with its `ATTESTED`, file-head and `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` clauses, is §7.4 [A-92.3]. (b) `STATE_CHECKPOINT` and `ANCHOR_RECORDED` are ◆ **events** and need `M_eff` counting signatures like every other; **checkpoints need the full quorum, default 1** — the `/2` pin that cites a checkpoint's root is single-key, and §7.4 ties the two (a pin seeds a resume only where `M_eff(35) = 1`) [A-174.1]. A deployment that wants single-signer operational checkpoints or anchors sets `threshold: 1` and raises other types through `byType`, or lowers `byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT` under rule 11 (Appendix G's SHOULD). An automatic checkpoint or handover seal is `CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason: NO_SIGNER}` when the writer's signers cannot form a counting set of `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` entries, as §6.5.8 defines it over the counting set and T2 (A-92.3(b)'s sentence as replaced by A-119.3); it is never written short (§7.3) [A-119.3] [A-174.2].
- At `M_eff = 1` the array is forbidden (step 2), so an `AGENT` key can never be a counting signer of such an event, and a human endorsement of an agent-initiated event is recorded only at `M_eff ≥ 2` [A-112].
- The `/2` challenge, every hashed preimage, the projection table (§12) and the decision procedure (§8) are unchanged [A-92.3].

#### 6.5.4 HF1 §7.1–7.2 as read for format 2 (policy, recovery, restoration rotations; proof of possession)
[A-96.0] [A-170.1] HF1 §7.1–7.2 apply to format 2 with the readings of this subsection. Each reading is conditioned on `ownerPolicy` (in the rotation or in the epoch record in force) or on `recovery`, which no format-1 history carries; format 1 and HF1 itself are untouched. HF1 §7.2 is thereby amended for format 2: a **policy rotation** is a third kind of rotation beside key rotations and commitment-only rotations, valid out of a committed epoch exactly once per policy change and any number of times in sequence; E-19 (a dedicated lifecycle type) is not adopted for renewals, revocations, reclassifications, attester changes, floor changes or `recovery` changes, which are all policy rotations with an unchanged key set [A-96.0].

**Definitions.** [A-96.1] [A-108.3] Presuming C4 (`ownerKeys` has 1..64 entries with pairwise distinct `keyId`s, HF1 §6.1, `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`), a type-26 event `e` at `p` **keeps every key** iff `|data.ownerKeys| = |K(p)|` and every entry equals a member of `K(p)` in `keyId`, `scheme` and lowercase `credential` (reading (a): the same member supplies both the id and the material; every comparison is against members of `K(p)`, which passed the §3.3 test, so a malformed entry compares unequal) [A-108.3]. Let `P'` be the resulting policy as written (`data.ownerPolicy`, or absent). `e` is a **policy rotation** iff it keeps every key **and** `P' ≠ P_w(p)` as written (§6.5.1; `P_w(p)` is absent when the epoch was set without `ownerPolicy`, so setting any policy on an epoch that has none, or dropping one, is a policy rotation). A rotation that keeps every key with `P' = P_w(p)` is a commitment-only rotation if it carries `nextKeyDigests` out of an uncommitted epoch (HF1 unchanged) and otherwise changes nothing and is refused (`TRANSITION_INVALID`, HF1's reason text) [A-96.1]. A **restoration rotation** is the policy rotation of §6.5.5 (`isRestoration(e)`), which a lapsed quorum may perform [A-107.3].

**Guard.** [A-108.1] `G(e, p) := e.data.ownerPolicy is present ∨ P_w(p) is present`. The readings marked "under `G(e, p)`" apply exactly when the rotation or the epoch in force carries a policy; a version gate was rejected because it would change the acceptance of future policy-free histories, whereas a guard on `ownerPolicy` presence keeps Appendix F invariant 20 verbatim and still covers every recovery rotation, whose epoch carries `recovery` and hence a policy [A-108.1].

**HF1 §7.1 item 1: the admissible signer set `adm(e)`.** [A-97.0] [A-97.2] [A-110.1] Recovery through committed next keys is an opt-in per policy (`recovery: "COMMITTED_NEXT_KEYS"`, §6.5.2); absent means HF1 literal (no next-key signer). A rotation at `r` is a control event like any other: its `controlAuthorization`, and every `additionalControlAuthorizations` entry (§6.5.3 step 3), is checked against the **admissible signer set** `adm(e)` at `r`. `adm(e)` is the set of owner keys in force at `r` (the set before the rotation; the new keys cannot authorize their own introduction), **extended, when `P(r).recovery = "COMMITTED_NEXT_KEYS"` and `e.type = OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED`, by every key of `e.data.ownerKeys` that is revealed by `e` (its `keyId` is not in `keyIds(K(r))`) and whose `digest` is in the commitment `D(r)` of the epoch in force.** For every other ◆ type `adm(e)` is the set in force at `r`. A signer in `adm(e)` that is not in force is a **recovery signer** [A-97.2]. `isRecovery(e) := e.type = 26 ∧ P(p).recovery = "COMMITTED_NEXT_KEYS" ∧ ∃ s ∈ S(e), ∃ k ∈ e.data.ownerKeys : s.keyId = k.keyId ∧ k.keyId ∉ keyIds(K(p)) ∧ digest(k) ∈ D(p)`; it is structural, decided from `e.data`, `S(e)` and the epoch record before verification. An entry of `S(e)` naming a `keyId` outside `K(p)` that is not such a key fails §6.5.3 step 3 (`CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`). A recovery signer's class is `cls` (§6.5.3): `HUMAN` by declaration under `DECLARED`; under `ATTESTED` it counts on type 26 only as `HUMAN`, that is only when `P(r)` carries a statement whose subject digest is in `D(r)` and equals the revealed key by credential, valid at `e.timestamp`; a lapsed pre-attestation is `LAPSED` and does not count. The §7.4 witness result reports this predicate as `recovery` [A-110.1] [A-107.1]. HF1 §7.2 rules 1–5 apply to a recovery rotation as read below (every revealed key, each recovery signer included, is proved by a `nextKeyAuthorizations` entry over the same challenge; every revealed key's digest is in `D(r)`; `nextKeyDigests` present) [A-97.2] [A-108.2].

**Quorum on a rotation.** [A-97.4] §6.5.3 steps 2–6 are unchanged with `adm(e)` as the signer set of step 3: `|C(e)| ≥ M_rot(P)`, agents never count, distinct `keyId`s and credentials. The MUST on committed digests for a deployment with `recovery` and `M_rot ≥ 2` is stated once, in §6.5.3 [A-97.4].

**Check order for a revealed signer.** [A-108.4] §6.5.3 steps 1 and 3 resolve a `keyId` of `S(e)` in `adm(e)`; a revealed key admitted as above is identified by `keyId` and digest (`digest` is a function of the credential string, computed at step 0), and its §6.3 scheme pre-checks run inside signature verification. A malformed revealed key that signs therefore fails with `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`; the §3.3 key test over the whole `ownerKeys` runs afterwards and gives `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` for a malformed key that did not sign. Stated, not changed.

**HF1 §7.2 as read for format 2.** [A-96.2] [A-108.2] [A-170.6] [A-107.2]
(i) **Rotating out of a committed epoch.** Rule 3 ("at least one key is revealed") reads: at least one key is revealed, **or `e` is a policy rotation**. For a policy rotation out of a committed epoch: `nextKeyAuthorizations` MUST be absent (rule 1 admits no entry, since no key is revealed; a present array is `TRANSITION_INVALID`, reason `owner key rotation: a policy rotation reveals no key`); rule 4 (proof of possession) is waived; rule 5 reads: `data.nextKeyDigests` is present **and equal as a set to the commitment of the epoch in force** (`TRANSITION_INVALID`, reason `owner key rotation: a policy rotation carries the current commitment unchanged`). The policy rotation opens the epoch `{fromPosition: r + 1, keys: data.ownerKeys, nextKeyDigests: data.nextKeyDigests, policy?: data.ownerPolicy}`: the commitment carries over unchanged, so a key in force alone can still neither replace nor drop a commitment [A-96.2].
(ii) **Rotating out of an uncommitted epoch.** A rotation that keeps every key is valid iff it adds `nextKeyDigests` (commitment-only, HF1 unchanged) **or is a policy rotation**, or both; with neither it changes nothing and is refused (`TRANSITION_INVALID`, HF1's reason text). A policy rotation out of an uncommitted epoch MAY start a commitment [A-96.2].
(iii) The pre-event policy `P(p)` authorizes every rotation (§6.5.3, with the counting set of its counting rule); the resulting policy is validated by §6.5.2 [A-96.2] [A-107.2].
(iv) **Rule 4 (proof of possession) under `G(e, p)`.** Rule 4 reads, when `G(e, p)` holds: `nextKeyAuthorizations` is present and **for every revealed key there is exactly one entry whose `keyId` is that key's** (rule 1 makes each entry verify over the challenge); `TRANSITION_INVALID`, reason `owner key rotation: every revealed key must prove possession`. When `G(e, p)` does not hold, rule 4 is HF1's literal text (the array is present). Rules 1–3 and 5 are unchanged. Out of an uncommitted epoch nothing changes (HF1 requires no proof there; adding a key one does not hold to an uncommitted epoch harms only the quorum that does it). The parenthetical of the admissible-signer reading above (each recovery signer proved by its own entry) follows from this rule for every recovery rotation [A-108.2].
(v) Beyond (i)–(iv), the admissible signer set `adm(e)` above, `isRecovery(e)`, and the owner-key and commitment readings below, nothing in HF1 §7.1–7.2 changes. The `verifyRotationEvent` result fields (`policyRotation`, `restoration`, `recovery`, `stateChecked`) live once, in §7.4 [A-170.6].

**Owner keys and foreign credentials (the §3.3 key-test stage of row 26; `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`).** [A-117.2] At row 0 with `ownerPolicy` present (vacuous: `σ(0)` has no records) and at row 26 under `G(e, p)`: **every** key `k ∈ e.data.ownerKeys` has `cred(k) ∉ foreign(σ(p))`. A kept key, a revealed key and a recovery signer are all covered. With the converse rule of §6.5.7 (no admission or registration of an owner, committed or attester credential under `P_w`) this gives `creds(K) ∩ foreign(σ) = ∅` in every epoch whose record carries `policy`. Consequence: a policy-free history whose owner credential was admitted as a session credential can adopt a policy only after that key leaves `K` (by a key rotation under HF1's unchanged rules); Appendix G states it.

**Commitments and foreign credentials (the HF1 §7.2 digest-rule stage; `TRANSITION_INVALID`).** [A-117.3] [A-173.2] At row 0 with `ownerPolicy` present and at row 26 under `G(e, p)`: no digest in `introduced(e, p)` equals the digest of a credential in `foreign(σ(p))`, of a credential in `attCreds(P(p))`, or of a credential in `attCreds(P')` where `P'` is the resulting policy. A digest carried from `D(p)` is not re-tested: the rotation cannot change it (Appendix F invariant 15). A carried digest that names a foreign credential is **dead while a policy is in force**: the owner-key rule above refuses its reveal under `G`; a quorum that resets the policy (§6.5.8 lists it, "policy reset at p") may reveal it policy-free, which is that quorum's act (Appendix G); `verify` lists dead carried commitments (§18). Adopting a policy is thereby always possible: a policy-free commitment to a later session credential blocks no policy rotation [A-117.3]. **Two rules, two stages, two codes:** at policy validity, an `attesters` credential of the resulting policy equal to a credential of `K`, in `foreign(σ(p))`, or whose digest is in the resulting `D` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` (§6.5.2 rule 2; fires first). At the digest-rule stage, a digest in `introduced(e, p)` equal to the digest of a credential in `foreign(σ(p))`, in `attCreds(P(p))` or in `attCreds(P')` is `TRANSITION_INVALID` (this rule). The two differ in domain (the resulting `D` including carried digests, versus introduced digests against the pre-event attesters), so neither is dropped. At row 0 both checks are in the validity list (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) [A-173.2].

**Consequences stated.** [A-96.3] A policy rotation cannot add, remove, rename or re-key an owner key (it keeps every key). A rotation that both changes keys and changes the policy is a key rotation under HF1's unchanged rules and §6.5.2 (nothing new). Under `M_rot = 1` the single key in force can change the policy alone, within §6.5.2's bounds (≥ 1 non-agent, non-agents ≥ `M_rot`); that is what `threshold: 1` means, and Appendix G's H2 states it. The `ownerKeyStatus()` counters gain `policyRotations` beside `keyRotations` and `commitmentRotations`. The writer MUST NOT reset a policy silently (`INVALID_INPUT {reason: POLICY_RESET}`, §6.5.8) [A-96.4]. Out of a committed epoch the key set changes only by a rotation that reveals a committed key with proof of possession; a quorum that holds no committed key can change the policy, neutralize a key and reduce power, but cannot add, remove or replace a key (Appendix G; E-24 defers a quorum-recommit option) [A-110.4].

**Stage order of row 26 (Appendix A).** [A-170.7] [A-102 X-9] The stages of a rotation run in this relative order: step 0 (`M_eff`, `PR_eff`, `isRestoration`, `isRecovery`, `adm(e)` from `data`, the `keyId`s of `S(e)`, the epoch record and `σ(p)`) → control authorization (§6.5.3 with `adm(e)` and the counting rule) → T2 (§6.5.6, under `ATTESTED`) → the §3.3 key test with the owner-key rule above (every key of `ownerKeys` under `G(e, p)`) → `ownerPolicy` validity (§6.5.2, rules 1–11 with the attestation rules) → the HF1 §7.1–7.2 readings above with the commitment rule (introduced digests) → capacity step 1. Appendix A row 26 carries the list with its codes.

#### 6.5.5 Human attestation
**What replay establishes.** [A-93.0] Human-attestation evidence enters Core minimally: Core-committed admission statements with the full evidence external (placement B of the PHL Core questions, §2 of that memo [AR-09]), carried inside `ownerPolicy` on the two policy-setting events and therefore inside the `ownerKeyEpoch` record (§7.2). A registry alone cannot enforce the founder's rule by replay and is kept as the ceremony layer beneath this section; dedicated lifecycle event types and status records are E-19 (post-rc.2) and are not needed while a policy rotation can renew, revoke and reclassify (§6.5.4). Core verifies a **trusted attester's signed, bounded statement about an exact key**; it never inspects biometrics, vouches, devices or registries, and it never consults a clock, a URL or a live status service. The claim a verifier obtains from an accepted history is exactly: *"the owner quorum in force named these attester keys, and an attester so named signed that key `K` was associated with a human under profile `π` for the interval `[notBefore, expiresAt)`, with evidence committed by `evidenceDigest`."* Nothing more is established by replay. The statement's schema, its signed body and every check that runs at the setting event (rules 0–5, the policy-time rules (a)–(c), the `historyId` rule, the carry waiver and the prospective-effect paragraph) are §6.5.2 and are not restated here [A-93.0] [A-93.2].

**The attested set and the class of a signer.** [A-93.3] For a ◆ event `e` at `p` with `t = e.timestamp` and pre-event policy `P = P(p)`, the **attested set** `A(P, t)` is the set of subjects, by credential, of the statements of `P.attestations` with `notBefore ≤ t < expiresAt` (equality at `expiresAt` fails; `t` is the event's signed timestamp, which is determinism and not wall-clock truth). Under `humanFloor = "ATTESTED"` a resolved signer whose credential is in `A(P, t)` is `HUMAN`; every other class is given by the single total function `cls` of §6.5.3, and the counting set `C(e)` by its counting rule [A-93.3] [A-107.1] [A-107.2]. A key's statement is judged under the **pre-event** policy only: a rotation that admits or attests `K'` cannot be signed by `K'`, which under `P` is either out of epoch or `NONE`. A pre-attested committed next key that `adm(e)` admits as a rotation signer (§6.5.4, `recovery`) is `HUMAN` under `P` iff its statement in `P` is valid at `t`; a signature of class `NONE`, `LAPSED` where it does not count, or non-counting `AGENT` is permitted in the array, must verify (§6.5.3 step 3) and is listed by `verify` as non-counting [A-93.3].

**Freshness.** [A-107.0] [A-107.4] A statement is fresh in an epoch only through the history's own acts: freshness is defined by **strict renewal** (a renewal's interval is later than the statement it replaces, below) plus the **`historyId` binding** of §6.5.2 rule 3 (every statement set after the genesis that is not carried names `recordHash(0)`). A subject whose statement a quorum removed never returns by restoration; a not-yet-valid statement confers nothing until it starts (`NONE`); an unrenewed co-owner stays `LAPSED`, because a restoration carries expired statements as written. Attester-side withdrawal stays outside Core (E-19, by design: only the quorum embeds and removes statements); `maxAttestationValidity` is deferred (E-25) [A-107.0].

**Lapse and the exemption.** [A-98.0] [A-107.2] A key that the epoch's own policy attested, whose statement is no longer valid at the event's timestamp, is `LAPSED` (§6.5.3). The founder's rule that the owner must always be able to reduce power outranks attestation freshness, and the safe direction is the direction lapsed keys may move. A lapsed key therefore keeps exactly the powers the counting exemption `X(e)` of §6.5.3 gives it, and gains nothing else: it counts on a `PR_eff` event (reducing power by effect, §6.5.3), on a restoration rotation (renewing, below) and on a `STATE_CHECKPOINT` (which adds no authority); it counts on no other type and on no other rotation [A-98.0] [A-107.2]. A ceremony MUST alone ("renew before expiry") was rejected, because Core would still freeze every revocation after a missed renewal, which is the scenario the rule exists for (Appendix G, H4). A key whose statement was removed by a policy rotation is `NONE`, not `LAPSED`: revocation is an act of the quorum and is final; lapse is passive and recoverable [A-98.0] [A-107.1].

**Restoration rotation (`isRestoration(e)`).** [A-107.3] [A-118.2] A type-26 event `e` at `p` with `P(p).humanFloor = "ATTESTED"` and `e.data.ownerPolicy = P'` present is a **restoration rotation** iff all of:
1. it is a policy rotation (§6.5.4: keeps every key, `P' ≠ P_w(p)` as written);
2. `P'.humanFloor = "ATTESTED"`;
3. `P'.threshold`, `P'.byType`, `P'.keyClasses`, `P'.attesters`, `P'.recovery` and `P'.acknowledged` each equal `P_w(p)`'s as written (absent equals absent); `labels` may differ;
4. `e.data.nextKeyDigests` is present iff the epoch record's `nextKeyDigests` is present (§6.5.1: present means non-null), and when present the two are equal as sets;
5. the set of subject credentials of `P'.attestations` equals the set of subject credentials of `P(p).attestations` (no subject added, none removed; a key whose statement a quorum removed is `NONE` and never returns by restoration);
6. every `a' ∈ P'.attestations` is either **carried** (`a' ∈ P(p).attestations` as written) or a **renewal** of the statement `a ∈ P(p).attestations` with the same subject credential: `max(a.notBefore, t_set(p)) < a'.notBefore ≤ e.timestamp` and `a'.expiresAt > a.expiresAt` (with §6.5.2 rule 3, `a'.expiresAt > e.timestamp`). A renewal has therefore started at the event and its subject is `HUMAN` under `P'` at `e.timestamp`; a statement issued before the epoch in force opened can never replace the embedded one, and a stashed statement that predates the embedded one can never replace it either: a renewal is a later attester act or nothing Core can see [A-118.2].

The predicate is decided from `e.data` and the epoch record alone, before any signature is counted, so the refusal code never depends on evaluation order (§6.5.3 step 0). A restoration keeps every key, has no recovery signer, and never changes `D` (it carries the commitment unchanged, present or absent). It is the only type-26 event a lapsed quorum can perform; it can renew and nothing else: a lapsed quorum cannot change attesters, classes, thresholds, keys, floor or `recovery` [A-107.3]. Whether the new statements are fresh is **not** part of the predicate: §6.5.2's policy-time rule (a) separately requires `≥ M_rot(P')` statements valid at `e.timestamp` and refuses a stale restoration with `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` after the quorum step (§6.5.4 stage order) [A-107.3]. Consequence (Appendix F invariant 12): for every subject credential `c`, `cls(P', K, D, e.timestamp, c) ≥ cls(P(p), K, D, e.timestamp, c)` in the order `NONE < LAPSED < HUMAN` (carried: equal; renewed: `HUMAN`); no subject's class drops on a restoration, and the started-statement count of §6.5.2 rule (b) never drops either [A-118.2] [A-118.3]. The attester-refusal scenario (H3) is Appendix G; the cardinality rule `|attesters| ≥ 2` under `ATTESTED` is §6.5.2 rule 2 [A-112]. The §7.4 witness result reports this predicate as `restoration` [A-170.6].

**Evidence, export and privacy.** [A-93.5] [A-102 X-13] The external evidence bundle (enrollment manifests, biometric-method result, vouch statement, attestation chains, status receipts) is committed by `evidenceDigest = sha256(bundle)` over the bundle as an opaque octet string (§6.5.2) and lives in the ceremony registry; it is **not a replay input** and **not an export dependency** (§15 unchanged; `EXPORT_DEPENDENCIES_MISSING` never names it). A verifier reports "ordinary Core history verified" and, separately, whether the committed bundle was supplied and matches its digest: a second, optional report, never a condition of Core acceptance. Core, the epoch record, the export view and any chain anchor carry no biometric template, image, embedding or personal identifier; the attestation carries a key, a profile name, two timestamps, a digest and (after the genesis) the history id. A zero-knowledge relation over `(historyId, epoch record, attestation body)` is outside Core (E-21) [A-93.5].

**Stated rc.2 limitation.** [A-102 X-12] An attestation `subject` is `ed25519` (§6.5.2); an `eip191-personal-sign-keccak256` owner key cannot be attested and is `NONE` under `ATTESTED` unless classed `AGENT`; `verify` lists it (§6.5.2, E-22).

#### 6.5.6 Time rules T1, T2, T3
Three replay rules bound an event's `timestamp` beyond the envelope's `EVENT_TIME_REGRESSION` test (§5). Each is guarded: T1 and T2 by `isAtt(P(p))` (§6.5.1), T3 by a non-null genesis `sessionTimeHorizonMs` (§5.1); a history that carries neither replays exactly as before. All three are `TRANSITION_INVALID` with the reason text given below; replay reads no clock under any of them [A-109.1] [A-119.1] [A-179.3 (A-22.6)].

**Placement.** [A-172.1] T1 and T3 sit in the **common prefix** of every ◇ and ▲ row, immediately after `EVENT_TIME_REGRESSION` (Appendix A A.2 C5) and before the type's own list, T3 immediately after T1; T2 sits **immediately after control authorization** on every ◆ row other than 0 (§6.5.3, then T2, then the type's own checks). The epoch fence stays first *among the type's own checks* (§6.2, A-82); T1 and T3 read only the envelope timestamp, the policy in force and the `ownerTime` index, never the session, so they belong with the envelope tests, and placing them after the fence would make an envelope property's code depend on a session lookup. A fenced session crossing a statement expiry or the horizon is therefore `TRANSITION_INVALID`, not `SESSION_FENCED`; both are refusals, and no accepted history changes [A-172.1].

**Rule T1 (session writers cannot lapse an owner).** [A-109.1] For every ◇ or ▲ event `e` at `p ≥ 1` with `P(p).humanFloor = "ATTESTED"`: for every `a ∈ P(p).attestations`, `a.expiresAt > τ(p) ⇒ e.timestamp < a.expiresAt`. Failure is `TRANSITION_INVALID`, reason `time: a session-signed event may not pass an owner attestation's expiry`. Consequence (Appendix F invariant 33): if `τ(p) < a.expiresAt ≤ e.timestamp` for an accepted `e`, then `e` is a ◆ event; every crossing of an attestation expiry is owner-signed. A session writer whose clock has passed an expiry stops writing until an owner renews or an owner writes a ◆ event; the writer pre-check is §6.5.8 [A-109.1].

**What T1 does not bound (stated limits).** [A-118.5] [A-131.4] [A-179.5] Rule T1 bounds expiries, not activations: a session writer may carry canonical time past a statement's `notBefore`, turning its subject from `NONE` to `HUMAN` earlier in real time than the attester intended. This adds a counting human and removes nothing; it is within Appendix F item 26 (Core has no clock) and is stated, not refused [A-118.5]. Rule T1 bounds a session writer only by the statement expiries still ahead of the head. Once every statement of `P(p)` has expired (`τ(p) ≥ H(p)`), T1 is vacuous and a ◇/▲ event may carry canonical time up to its session's `expiresAt − 1` (Appendix A A.3), or to `ownerTime + sessionTimeHorizonMs` when a horizon is set, whichever is lower; owner-side time stays bounded by T2. A later restoration must then renew with `expiresAt > max(stmt.expiresAt, t)` (Appendix F, L5). Within item 26; stated, not refused [A-131.4] [A-179.5]. A session-carried advance is permanent, and `CONTROL_EPOCH_ADVANCED` does not reverse it (Appendix G) [A-179.5].

**Rule T2 (a single human-class signer is bounded by the attestation horizon).** [A-119.1] For every ◆ event `e` at `p ≥ 1` with `isAtt(P(p))`: if `hc(e) ≤ 1` (§6.5.1: the number of counting signers of class `HUMAN` or `LAPSED`) then `e.timestamp = τ(p)` or `e.timestamp ≤ min(H(p), MAXT − 1)`, where `H(p)` is the attestation horizon and `MAXT = 2^53 − 1` (§6.5.1). Failure is `TRANSITION_INVALID`, reason `time: a single human signer may not advance time past the attestation horizon`, checked immediately after control authorization (it needs `C(e)`) and before every type-specific check of the row (for row 26: before the §3.3 key test, §6.5.4 stage order). `AGENT` entries never raise `hc`; `NONE` entries never count. A single human-class signer either leaves time where it is or advances it at most to the horizon, and never to `MAXT`; two human-class signers can still set any time, which is a quorum under Appendix G (A-113.1(1)) and is the residual of Appendix F item 37. T2 is not a per-event advance cap (a cap was rejected: it would force a writer returning after a long absence to lie about time or write catch-up events, and a rogue could still advance by the cap per fence event); a returning single writer past the horizon writes at the stale head time, renews, and only then advances [A-119.0] [A-119.1] [A-109.0].

Consequences: a single human-class key (a `LAPSED` key on a `PR_eff` event or a checkpoint, a `HUMAN` key at `M_eff = 1`, a sole recovery signer, a single key under `M_rot = 1`) can force every co-owner to lapse (recoverable by a restoration, Appendix F L5), can cross every expiry up to the horizon, and can never make `expiresAt > t_set` unsatisfiable; after the horizon it writes at the head's time until the horizon is extended by renewals (which, under `M_rot ≥ 2`, need a second human-class key). A statement with `expiresAt = 2^53 − 1` is legal, is crossed by no single signer, and cannot be renewed in the sense of §6.5.5 (its `expiresAt` cannot grow); it needs no renewal, since its subject cannot lapse before head time `2^53 − 1`, which no history reaches without `hc ≥ 2` (Appendix F invariant 42; attesters SHOULD issue `expiresAt ≤ 2^53 − 2`, Appendix G) [A-119.1] [A-126.4]. Under `DECLARED` there is no horizon and no T2: pinning time there expires sessions, budgets and dated grants, which is a reduction, and no rotation depends on time [A-119.1]. T2 makes `1` the safe recommended value for `byType` on `PR_types` under `ATTESTED` as under `DECLARED` (Appendix G, A-113.1(2)) [A-119.0]. The time-advance liveness property L12 (session time crosses an expiry only after some ◆ event at or past it is accepted; a single `HUMAN` or `LAPSED` holder suffices for every expiry `≤ 2^53 − 2` when `byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT = 1`) is Appendix F; the checkpoint SHOULD it rests on is Appendix G (A-113.1(8)); the writer's `MAX_UNWITNESSED` exemption for `STATE_CHECKPOINT` is §6.1 [A-119.2] [A-126.3].

**`ownerTime(q)` (derived index).** [A-179.2 (A-22.6)] `ownerTime(q)` is the `timestamp` of the latest accepted control event (◆, P.5) at a position `r`, `1 ≤ r ≤ q`, with `e_r.timestamp > e_{r−1}.timestamp`; `e_0.timestamp` when none exists. A control event that does not advance time (one written at `max(now, head)` by a writer whose clock is behind, §6.1 writer contract) never moves it. Prefix ◆ events count. Derived from `event` records (`type`, `timestamp`) as an index rebuildable under §7.1; not a state record (§7.2).

**Rule T3 (session time horizon).** [A-179.3 (A-22.6)] For every ◇ or ▲ event `e` at `p ≥ 1` with `¬inPrefix(p)` (P.5), when `genesis.sessionTimeHorizonMs` is non-null: `e.timestamp − ownerTime(p−1) ≤ sessionTimeHorizonMs` (a difference; non-negative after C5). Failure is `TRANSITION_INVALID`, reason `time: a session-signed event may not pass the session time horizon`, checked immediately after T1, before the type's own list; the §13.2 attester session shares the timestamp and is not tested separately. Control events are not subject. Replay reads no clock. The genesis field (`sessionTimeHorizonMs?: U53 ≥ 1 | null`, default `null`; `0` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` at C4) is §5.1 and Appendix B row 0; the `genesis` record copies it as written, so no existing root moves [A-179.1 (A-22.6)]. The writer contract, the not-recorded reason and the CLI options are §6.5.8; the OPERATOR and SECURITY statements are Appendix G [A-179.4 (A-22.6)] [A-179.5].

**What time cannot do (pointer).** [A-109.3] Owner-side time is known not to hold as an invariant: any owner key that can authorize a ◆ event alone chooses `e.timestamp` within T2 only, and writers that never advance time keep every statement valid, since Core has no clock (E-23). Appendix F items 26 and 37 state this; the `verify` listings of ◆ events advancing time beyond `--time-window` (default 30 days, `2,592,000,000` ms) and of ◆ events that crossed an attestation expiry are report-level (§18, §6.5.8) and the OPERATOR text is Appendix G [A-109.3] [A-109.2].

#### 6.5.7 Credential separation
**Scope.** [A-110.2] [A-117.0] Under a policy, an owner credential, a committed next-key credential and an attester credential are kept apart from every session and counterparty credential by replay rules, so that "the agent runtime becomes the owner" is closed in replay and not only by the writer. Without a policy every such refusal stays the writer rule of §6.3 (Key roles, `INVALID_INPUT`; the `verify`/`inspect` "credential reuse" listing stands) [A-173.1]. The rules are guarded by `G(e, p)` (§6.5.4) on rows 0 and 26 and by the presence of `P_w(p)` on rows 8 and 36; no frozen, pinned or regenerated history carries a policy, so none is affected [A-117.0] [A-173.1].

**`foreign(σ)`.** [A-117.1] `foreign(σ)` (§6.5.1) is the set of credentials of **every** `session` record and of every key of **every** `counterparty` record of `σ`, whatever their status, expiry or agent epoch: "live only" was considered and rejected, since session records are never removed and the set must be a function of the §7.2 records alone so that a checkpoint resume (§7.5) and full replay agree. Replay keeps it as a derived index, not a record and not in the state root (§7.1). A counterparty key replaced by re-registration is in no record and therefore leaves `foreign`: a stated limit (Appendix F item 43); a guarded `counterparty.formerKeys?` record field is E-28 (post-rc.2) [A-117.0] [A-117.1].

**The four rules, by stage.** [A-117.2] [A-117.3] [A-117.4] [A-110.2] [A-173.2]
| event (row) | stage | refused | code |
|---|---|---|---|
| genesis (0) with `ownerPolicy`; `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` (26) under `G(e, p)` | §3.3 key test | any key `k ∈ e.data.ownerKeys` with `cred(k) ∈ foreign(σ(p))` (kept, revealed or recovery signer; vacuous at 0) — §6.5.4 | `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` [A-117.2] |
| genesis (0) with `ownerPolicy`; 26 under `G(e, p)` | policy validity (rule 2) | an `attesters` credential of the resulting policy equal to a credential of `K`, in `foreign(σ(p))`, or whose digest is in the resulting `D` — §6.5.2 | `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` [A-117.4] [A-173.2] |
| genesis (0) with `ownerPolicy`; 26 under `G(e, p)` | HF1 §7.2 digest rule (at 0: the validity list) | a digest in `introduced(e, p)` equal to the digest of a credential in `foreign(σ(p))`, in `attCreds(P(p))` or in `attCreds(P')` — §6.5.4 | `TRANSITION_INVALID` (0: `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) [A-117.3] [A-173.2] |
| `RUNTIME_SESSION_ADMITTED` (8), `COUNTERPARTY_REGISTERED` (36), while `P_w(p)` is present | the row's transition stage | a credential in `creds(K(p))`, a credential whose `digest` is in `D(p)`, or a credential in `attCreds(P(p))` | `TRANSITION_INVALID` [A-110.2] [A-117.4] |

**The converse (rows 8 and 36).** [A-110.2] [A-117.4] While the epoch in force carries `P_w` (its record's `policy` is present, §6.5.1): a `RUNTIME_SESSION_ADMITTED` or `COUNTERPARTY_REGISTERED` whose credential is in `creds(K(p))`, or whose credential's `irontrellis-owner-key-digest/1` digest is in `D(p)`, is `TRANSITION_INVALID` [A-110.2]; a `RUNTIME_SESSION_ADMITTED` or `COUNTERPARTY_REGISTERED` whose credential is in `attCreds(P(p))` is `TRANSITION_INVALID` at the same stage [A-117.4]. The §6.3 dedicated-key rule thereby becomes a replay rule under a policy; the writer listing of §6.3 stands beside it. Appendix A rows 8 and 36 carry the item ("committed, owner or attester credential under a policy") at their transition stage [A-110.2] [A-117.4].

**What the rules give.** [A-117.0] [A-117.2] [A-117.3] With the owner-key rule over the whole resulting key set and the converse above, induction over the history gives `creds(K) ∩ foreign(σ) = ∅` in every epoch whose record carries `policy` (Appendix F invariant 34, with the attester clause read over the attesters **in force and resulting**, never over every attester ever named: a credential that no longer attests is a credential, and under `ATTESTED` it counts only with a statement from a current attester). Because the digest rule tests the digests a rotation **introduces** and not the carried commitment, a policy-free commitment to a later session credential blocks no policy rotation: the carried digest is accepted and is dead while a policy is in force (its reveal fails the owner-key rule under `G`), `verify` lists it (§6.5.8), and a quorum that resets the policy may reveal it policy-free, which is that quorum's act (Appendix G). Adopting a policy is therefore always possible; a policy-free history whose owner credential is already a session credential adopts one only after that key leaves `K` by a key rotation under HF1's unchanged rules (Appendix G) [A-117.0] [A-117.2] [A-117.3]. Invariant 24 remains known not to hold for credentials Core has never seen (Appendix F) [A-110.0]. Custody of committed keys is a stated limit with a ceremony MUST, and the escrow coupling (a custodian who holds `M_rot` committed keys recovers alone under `recovery`) is Appendix G [A-110.0] [A-110.4].

#### 6.5.8 Writer contract and API
Everything in this subsection is a writer, API or report rule, not a replay rule; replay is literal under §6.5.2–§6.5.7 and no `--opt` of the checker changes it [A-96.4].

**Two-phase control events.** [A-92.4] Signing a ◆ event under `M_eff(e) ≥ 2` is two-phase. `prepareControlEvent(handle, type, input)` fixes the envelope (`id`, `timestamp` — for a power-reducing type per §6.1's writer contract [A-22.3] —, `data` without authorizations) at the current head and returns the §6.1 challenge **structure** (not only its hash) and `M_eff(e)`. The ceremony collects signatures by any means (local signer, SSH agent, remote signer); transport, encryption, timeouts and presence are the ceremony protocol and are **not verifiable by replay** (Appendix G says so plainly). `submitControlEvent(handle, prepared, authorizations)` re-checks under the writer lock that the head equals the challenge's `previousHead` (else `HISTORY_CONFLICT`; the ceremony prepares again — an old consent is never re-signed onto a new head), applies the §6.5.3 replay rule and T2 (§6.5.6) as a pre-check with the replay meaning (`CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` thrown before any append, carrying informative `{required, present}`; T2 as below), then appends. The remote-signer registry, sealed transport and timeout state machine are outside Core; a signer missing past its deadline is a ceremony failure, never a relaxation of `M`. The challenge is single-use by construction (`previousHead` + `eventId`); a transport nonce is transport-only. The encryption key of a remote signer MUST differ from its signing key (§6.3 dedicated-key rule). An agent signer is a remote signer whose deterministic signing policy renders the `effectHash` preimage; the LLM behind it is advisory input and never the proof [A-92.4]. When the owner API's pre-check refuses an `AGENT_TERMINATED` at a lowered threshold because the agent is on a live prohibition's anchor path (§6.5.3, the `PR_eff` row), it names that clause [A-127.3].

**Codes.** [A-92.6] [A-172.2] The §18 replay set stays at 26 (§6.5.3). Store/API: `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` is a writer pre-check code with the replay meaning (the §18 pre-check list [A-56.8]), with informative `{required, present}`; `HISTORY_CONFLICT` on a moved head at submit (existing code) [A-92.6]. The API never throws the replay code `TRANSITION_INVALID`: a transition the writer declines is `TRANSITION_REJECTED {reason, …}` (§18 [A-8] [A-12]), and the time-rule pre-checks below carry the reasons `ATTESTATION_EXPIRY {expiresAt}` (T1), `ATTESTATION_HORIZON {horizon}` (T2) and `SESSION_TIME_HORIZON {ownerTime, horizonMs}` (T3). A builder that shipped `TRANSITION_INVALID` in any of these pre-checks renames it and records the rename in DEVIATIONS [A-172.2].

**Time-rule pre-checks.** [A-109.1] [A-119.1] [A-125.3] [A-172.2] [A-179.4 (A-22.6)] A session writer whose clock has passed an attestation expiry stops writing until an owner renews or an owner writes a ◆ event: an appending call for a ◇ or ▲ event whose timestamp would fail T1 throws `TRANSITION_REJECTED {reason: ATTESTATION_EXPIRY, expiresAt}` [A-109.1] [A-172.2]. A ◆ event whose timestamp would fail T2 is refused at submit (or at a single-phase append) with `TRANSITION_REJECTED {reason: ATTESTATION_HORIZON, horizon}` [A-119.1] [A-125.3] [A-172.2]. A writer of a ◇ or ▲ event whose timestamp would fail T3 does not sign: an appending API call throws `TRANSITION_REJECTED {reason: SESSION_TIME_HORIZON, ownerTime, horizonMs}`; `run()`, hook and gateway recording return the not-recorded reason `SESSION_TIME_HORIZON` (§10.1.1 item 7); a spooled entry stays spooled until an owner event moves `ownerTime` (`inspect` surfaces the count). Decisions are unaffected. No writer invokes an owner signer to satisfy the rule (§10.1.1 item 9(c) [A-38.3]). The ▲ pre-check sits in the administrative prepare step beside the expiry check. `init --session-time-horizon <ms>` and the `migrate` option set the genesis field; `init` and `budget declare` warn when the horizon is `≥` a declared `FIXED_WINDOW` `lengthMs`; `migrate` with the field set prints that sessions surviving the prefix cannot write until the first post-migration time-advancing owner event [A-179.4 (A-22.6)].

**Policy reset.** [A-96.4] [A-112] An absent `ownerPolicy` on a rotation sets the default policy in replay (§6.5); the local API MUST NOT reset a policy silently. `rotateOwnerKey`, `commitNextKey` and `irontrellis owner rotate-key` / `commit-next-key` refuse with `INVALID_INPUT {reason: POLICY_RESET}` when the pre-event policy is not the default and the call supplies no `ownerPolicy`, unless the caller passes `resetPolicy: true` / `--reset-policy`; `commitNextKey` restates the current policy as written by default (the resulting commitment-only rotation then keeps the policy). "Not the default" is an **effective** comparison: the effective policy differs from `{threshold: 1}`, so an explicit `{threshold: 1}` is the default; the `verify` listing "policy reset at p" (every rotation whose resulting policy is the default while the pre-event policy was not) compares the same way, whereas a policy rotation compares as written (§6.5.4) [A-96.4] [A-112]. The `ownerKeyStatus()` counters are `keyRotations`, `commitmentRotations` and `policyRotations` (§6.5.4) [A-96.3].

**Checkpoints, the handover seal and `NO_SIGNER`.** [A-119.3] [A-174.2] `CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason: NO_SIGNER}` (§7.3, report-level) applies when the writer's signers cannot form a counting set of `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` entries with `cls ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED, AGENT}` that contains at least one `HUMAN` or `LAPSED` entry, or when every such set would violate T2 at the writer's intended timestamp; an automatic checkpoint under lapse is therefore not suppressed for want of a `HUMAN` signer, because `STATE_CHECKPOINT` counts `LAPSED` (§6.5.3) [A-119.3]. The `succeed()` handover seal, an automatic checkpoint and a single-phase `checkpoint()` under `M_eff(35) ≥ 2` are governed by §7.3 ("Checkpoints under a quorum and the handover seal"), which states the rule in full: the seal is suppressed with this reason and never written short, the succession events go through the two-phase writer above when `M_eff ≥ 2`, and a short single-phase `checkpoint()` throws `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID {required, present}` (the pre-check above) [A-174.2]. The writer's automatic checkpoint and `MAX_UNWITNESSED` MUST NOT refuse a lapsed-signed `STATE_CHECKPOINT` that replay accepts (§6.1 [A-24.1] [A-176]) [A-119.2].

**`verify` and `inspect` listings (report-level; §18 carries the list).** [A-95.6] [A-98.7] [A-105.3] [A-109.3] [A-115.3] [A-125.3] For the rules of §6.5 the report-level listings are: the counting and non-counting entries of every ◆ event with the class of each (`LAPSED` included) and every agent key that can count toward no type [A-95.6] [A-105.3]; attester credential reuse, attestation intervals, keys without a statement under `ATTESTED`, owner keys of a scheme the attestation profile cannot name (§6.5.5), types unreachable under the attested set of epoch `n` (§6.5.2), and evidence-bundle presence (§6.5.5) [A-95.6] [A-105.3]; given `--as-of <U53>` (optional; default the head's `canonicalTime`), every statement of the head epoch with `expiresAt ≤ asOf + window` (window default 30 days, `2,592,000,000` ms) and the head epoch's counts of `HUMAN`, `LAPSED`, `AGENT` and `NONE` keys at `asOf` — replay never consults `asOf` [A-98.7] [A-109.2]; "policy reset at p" (above); every ◆ event whose timestamp exceeds `τ(p)` by more than `--time-window` (default 30 days) and every ◆ event that crossed an attestation expiry [A-109.3] [A-115.3]; the `PR_eff` verdict per `PR_types` event, rollover budgets updated by the ordinary quorum (§6.5.3), carried statements and carried expired statements per rotation (§6.5.5), dead carried commitments (§6.5.7), and pins and anchor-log entries whose signer is `AGENT`, `NONE` or `LAPSED` at its `canonicalTime` (§7.4) [A-115.3] [A-125.3] [A-111.3]. A spooled refusal count under T3 is an `inspect` item (above). `inspect`, here and wherever this document writes "`verify` and `inspect`", names the engine's read-only inspection surface (the state-inspection API of §13.3–§13.6 and the writer's engine-private side files it reads), carried by the CLI's existing diagnostic command and by the hook's and gateway's status output, not by a separate `inspect` command; file-only listings are `verify` output [A-196]. None of these is a condition of acceptance.

#### 6.5.9 Tests and checker defaults
**Ledger (names, not counts to add to anything).** [A-115.6] [A-125.6] [A-127.3] [A-133.7] [A-179.6] The frozen 86 vectors of `conformance/v2-g3` [A-191.3], the nine pinned roots and the A-71 group are unchanged by every rule of §6.5: no frozen history carries `ownerPolicy`, `additionalControlAuthorizations`, `humanFloor`, `recovery` or `sessionTimeHorizonMs`, and under the default policy `M_eff = 1` for every type and `PR_eff` is never consulted. The architect generator and the independent checker produce every new case from this text; the builder's self-built pins are not acceptance [A-95.9]. The groups are:

| group | composition (by ruling) | re-specified or retired | pinned root |
|---|---|---|---|
| `conformance/v2/owner-policy/` (≈ 80 names) | A-91.7 (14 + 2 builder-pinned), A-92.7 (≈ 14), A-96.5 (6), A-97.7 (6), A-99.2 (3), A-102 X-19 (1), A-106.6 (11), A-108.5 (2), A-110.5 (7), A-116.6 (8), A-117.5 (6), A-127.3 (1) | `rejects-duplicate-credential` retired [A-108.5]; `accepts-policy-rotation-same-keys` rebuilt out of a committed epoch [A-96.5]; `rejects-recovery-unrevealed-next-key` fails step 3 under the structural predicate [A-110.5]; `accepts-budget-update-lowering-cap-at-pr-threshold` uses a `NONE`-period budget [A-116.6]; `rejects-revealed-key-equal-to-session-credential` keeps its name on the R3F-1 construction [A-117.5] | `accepts-policy-history` (`ownerKeyEpoch.policy` at epochs 0 and 1; construction unchanged since A-96.5) |

## 7. State commitment

### 7.1 State root
After every event the state is a finite set of **state records** `(kind, key, record)` (§7.2). Each contributes one leaf:

```
leaf = sha256( 0x00 ‖ utf8(kind) ‖ 0x00 ‖ utf8(key) ‖ 0x00 ‖ sha256(utf8(canonical(record))) )
```
Leaves are ordered by `(kind, key)` under UTF-8 byte order (`compareProtocolStrings`), and the **state root** is the RFC 6962 §2.1 Merkle tree hash over the ordered 32-byte leaves, with the §7.1 leaf hash in place of 6962's own leaf hashing:

```
MTH([])      = sha256("")            (= e3b0c442…b855)
MTH([l])     = l
MTH(L)       = sha256( 0x01 ‖ MTH(L[0:k]) ‖ MTH(L[k:n]) ),  k = largest power of two < n
```
`recordCount` is the number of leaves. `stateRoot` is `0x` + hex. `rootAt(p)` denotes the root of the state after applying events `0…p` (it includes the `event` record of `p`). The empty-state root never occurs.

**Exact everywhere.** [A-4.1] Every `stateRoot` written, signed, compared or returned MUST be exactly the MTH over the complete sorted leaf list at that position (checkpoints, every `/2` attestation, snapshot validation, export view, adapter results). [A-67, rewording A-4.1] An implementation MAY cache leaf hashes and reuse the hash of any complete aligned subtree `MTH(L[i·2^m : (i+1)·2^m])` whose `2^m` leaves are, at the same indices, identical in value to those over which it was computed; it MUST NOT reuse any node whose leaf range differs in content or index. After an in-place leaf change at sorted index `j` this is every aligned subtree not containing `j`; after an insertion or removal at `j` it is every aligned subtree lying entirely before `j`. A property test MUST show random insert/update/delete sequences give the same root from the cache as from scratch; the pinned roots MUST reproduce. (Informative [A-4.2]: an in-place leaf change is O(log n) with cached subtrees; an insertion or removal at sorted index `j` is O(n − j + log n); every accepted event inserts at least its `event` leaf.)

Inclusion of any single record is proved by the standard 6962 audit path against the root (the witness package already implements `verifyInclusion`); non-membership of a `(kind, key)` follows from the two adjacent leaves in sorted order. Equality of two roots proves equality of the whole record set.

**Invariant (normative).** The replay state of an accepted history at position `p` — everything `replay`, `authorize`, the queries, the receipts and the duty-policy procedures read — is a function of the record set at `p`, where a record may refer to an event by `position` and the referenced event's data is then read from the history file. An engine MUST be able to rebuild its full state from (the record set, the events it references) without re-validating the prefix, and that rebuilt state MUST answer every operation exactly as a full replay does (builder test T2, BUILD §5). Indices that rc.1-final keeps (`eventPositionsById`, `currentTenureIdsByAgent`, `runtimeSessionIdsByAgent`, `credentialKeyIds`, `credentialAddressKeysByAgent`, `attemptDutyIdsBySourceIntent`, `obligationIdsBySourceIntent`, `reservedAgentIds`, `recognizedRoots`, `authorityUsage`, `intentOutcomeStates`, `shadowHashes`) and the format-2 counters of §5.2.1 and the per-actor latest-refusal index of §10.1.1 [A-3] [A-23.1] are derived from the records below; the insertion order rc.1 relies on is the order of each record's `position`. So are the format-2 derived indices `foreign(σ)`, over the `session` and `counterparty` records (§6.5.1, §6.5.7 [A-117.1]), the `(streamId, eventRef)` observation index (§10.3 [A-86.1] [A-87]) and `ownerTime`, over the `event` records' `type` and `timestamp` (§6.5.6 [A-179.2 (A-22.6)]); none is a record or in the root.

**Identity.** Identity under this section is the record set: the leaves in `(kind, key)` order, `rootAt(p)`, `projectionHead(p)`, `recordCount`, the replay verdict, and the answer of every operation named above. `ReplayState` is an implementation of that function. The iteration order of its Maps and Sets, and the presence of an entry in a bookkeeping index whose absence derives the same record (an index entry equal to the empty record), are not part of identity and are unspecified in the public API; a comparator that needs an order sorts by key. Where the engine itself relies on an order, it is the order of each record's `position` (above). [A-188.2]

**Standard.** [A-189.1] This section binds answers and semantic state, not object shape. A live-versus-rebuilt difference is a Core defect iff (i) an operation named above (replay, authorize, the queries, the receipts, the duty-policy procedures) or a `verify`/`inspect`/export output can answer differently, or (ii) a field of a public `ReplayState` value type (`RoleTenureRecord`, `AuthorityRecord`, …) differs in presence or value. Iteration order and empty bookkeeping entries are outside identity (A-188.2). The record-only API is not narrower than live by design: `rebuildReplayState` is the inverse of the derivation table and MUST reproduce every semantic field; an authenticated-body overlay is never required to reach parity.

### 7.2 State records
Every record is a closed JSON record. Absent optional values of fields **this table names** are written as `null` (never omitted), **except `ownerKeyEpoch.policy`, which is present iff the epoch's setting event carried `ownerPolicy` and is never written as `null` [A-91.3]** [A-175.1]; all Amounts are §3.3 strings; all lists are in the order stated. Fields described as **"as written"** copy the event's JSON value exactly: absent keys stay absent, no defaults are filled (for example a genesis without `capacity`, `checkpointPolicy`, `projection` or `sessionTimeHorizonMs` [A-179.1 (A-22.6)] keeps them absent), and the only transformation is the byte-order sorting stated for `grant` lists. Keys are Identifiers unless stated. `position` is the position of the event that created the record, where a row lists `position`; `intent` carries `declarationPosition` instead (pinned by the r2.3 roots) [A-16.6]. The `digest` of an `intent.outcomes[]`, `intent.observations[]` or `duty.reviews[]` entry is `hashCanonical(event.data)` of the entry's event (as `admissionDigest`/`consumptionDigest` are); `findingHash` is `hashCanonical(data.finding)` [A-16.2]. The kinds (and nothing else) are:

| kind | key | record fields |
|---|---|---|
| `event` | `position` as a 16-digit zero-padded decimal | `{position, eventId, type, timestamp, eventHash, recordHash, actorId: Identifier\|null, subjectId: Identifier\|null}` — one per accepted event; `actorId`/`subjectId` are the §12.2 selection for the type. This record carries the id index, the order and the per-position hashes |
| `genesis` | `"0"` | the genesis `data` **without** `ownerKeys`, `nextKeyDigests` and `ownerPolicy`, otherwise as written [A-175.1] |
| `ownerKeyEpoch` | decimal epoch number (`"0"`, `"1"`, …) | `{epoch: U53, fromPosition: U53, keys: [{keyId, scheme, credential}] (as declared), nextKeyDigests: [ContentHash]\|null, policy?: <ownerPolicy as written>}` — `policy` is present iff the epoch's setting event (the genesis for epoch 0, the `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` event otherwise) carried `ownerPolicy`, is never `null`, and carries `P(p)` as written (§6.5.1); the field name is `policy` (a guest that reads this record refuses on presence of `policy`, the C1 enrolment `ownerPolicy: "REFUSE"` on presence of the key `ownerPolicy` in `data` of types 0 and 26) [A-175.1] [A-175.3]. `nextKeyDigests` keeps `[ContentHash]\|null`; wherever this document says a `nextKeyDigests` is "present" it means non-null [A-175.2] |
| `principal` | principalId | `{principalId, eventId, position}` |
| `agent` | agentId | `{agentId, principalId, controllerId, controlEpoch: U53 (current), status: "ACTIVE"\|"TERMINATED", eventId, position, terminatedEventId: Identifier\|null, terminatedPosition: U53\|null}` |
| `role` | roleId | `{roleId, principalId, exclusive: boolean, currentTenureId: Identifier\|null, eventId, position}` |
| `tenure` | roleTenureId | `{roleTenureId, roleId, agentId, tenureNumber: U53, status: "CURRENT"\|"ENDED", eventId, position, endedEventId: Identifier\|null, endedPosition: U53\|null}` |
| `succession` | ruleId | `{rule: <the §6.1 effect of the SUCCESSION_RULE_DECLARED event, as written [A-170.8]>, eventId, position}` |
| `session` | sessionId | `{sessionId, agentId, controllerId, controlEpoch, credentialKeyId, credentialScheme, credential (PublicKey32 or EthereumAddress, lowercase; a record rule — a PublicKey32 is lowercase by its §3.3 grammar and is never lowercased by the engine [A-157]), expiresAt: U53\|null (`null` when the migrated source carried no expiry: normalized from absence, A-62.2 [A-73]; [A-22.1] [A-52.2]), eventId, position}` |
| `authority` | authorityId | `{grant: <the grant as written, with actions, resources and requiredIntersectionIds each sorted by byte order>, eventId, position, revoked: boolean, revokerId: Identifier\|null, revocationEventId: Identifier\|null, revocationPosition: U53\|null}` |
| `usage` | authorityId | `{authorityId, admittedTransactionCount: U53, admittedCumulativeAmount: Amount}` — one record for every authority that has been a controlling authority of at least one admission, counting every such admission (count +1, amount + request amount or 0). **This is a format-2 counter distinct from rc.1-final's `authorityUsage`** (which exists only for capped grants, counts only the capped dimension, and still drives the decision and the proof's `usageSnapshot`); an implementation maintains both. `authorityUsage` is derived from the admitted `intent` records (`admissionPosition`) by reading each admission body's `controllingAuthorityIds` and `request.amount` for capped, unrevoked grants, in position order, inside the A-65.3 closure of §15; whether it equals the capped projection of this `usage` record is a builder-tested property, not assumed by the text [A-148.5] |
| `nonce` | `hashCanonical([actorId, nonce])` | `{actorId, nonce, intentId, position}` — **created by `TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED`** (format-2 rule; rc.1 reserved nonces at admission): a declaration whose `(actorId, nonce)` already has a record is `TRANSITION_INVALID` (vector `rejects-duplicate-nonce-declaration`); an admission requires the intent's nonce record to name that intent (a `nonce` record for `(actorId, binding.nonce)` that names an `intentId` other than `binding.intentId` is `DENY / NONCE_ALREADY_ADMITTED` at the authority procedure's step 10, §8.1.1; the intent's own reservation never collides [A-137.3]) |
| `intent` | intentId | `{intentId, status, actorId, action, resource, amount: Amount\|null, metric (the declaration's `metric`, or `"amount"` when absent), counterpartyId: Identifier\|null, termsCommitment: ContentHash\|null, roleId, roleTenureId, nonce, adapterProfile (as written), declarationEventId, declarationPosition, declarationDigest (= `declarationAuthorization.hash`; `null` for a migrated prefix intent), admissionEventId: Identifier\|null, admissionPosition: U53\|null, admissionDigest: ContentHash\|null (= hashCanonical of the admission's `data`), consumptionEventId, consumptionPosition, consumptionDigest (likewise, or null), outcomes: [{eventId, position, status, digest}] (every TRANSACTION_OUTCOME_RECORDED event of the intent in position order — rc.1 accepts a terminal CONFIRMED/FAILED followed by DISPUTED and keeps both; the list keeps all; at most 16 [A-51.5]), observations: [{eventId, position, digest}] (OUTCOME_OBSERVATION_RECORDED events in order; at most 64 [A-51.5])}`; `status ∈ {DECLARED, ADMITTED, CONSUMED, CONFIRMED, FAILED, DISPUTED, OUTCOME_UNKNOWN}` is derived: after outcomes it is the terminal outcome if one exists, else the latest outcome (exactly rc.1-final's `latest`/`terminal` rule). **"Admitted"** elsewhere in this document means `status ∈ {ADMITTED, CONSUMED, CONFIRMED, FAILED, DISPUTED, OUTCOME_UNKNOWN}` |
| `receipt` | receiptContentHash | `{receiptContentHash, receiptArtifactCommitment, intentId, issuerAgentId, eventId, position}` — at most 16 per intent, derivable from state [A-51.5] |
| `obligation` | obligationId | `{record: <the OBLIGATION_CREATED record as written>, status, performanceAssigneeId (current), eventId, position, assignmentEventId: Identifier\|null, assignmentPosition: U53\|null, statusEventId: Identifier\|null, statusPosition: U53\|null}` — at most one per source intent (the rc.1 precondition stands, A-88.1), so the source-intent lookup is singular [A-88.3] |
| `duty` | dutyId | `{record: <the ATTEMPT_DUTY_CREATED record as written>, status: "OPEN"\|"CLOSED" (= `record.status` as written; format 2 never sets `CLOSED`; review state is the derived `attemptDutyReviewStatus` [A-16.1]), creationActorId, currentAssigneeId, eventId, position, assignments: [{eventId, position, toAgentId}] (at most 64 [A-51.5]), reviews: [{eventId, position, digest}] (at most 64 [A-51.5]), reviewClosedEventId: Identifier\|null (the latest review's event id [A-16.1]), policy: null \| {descriptor (as written), descriptorHash, activationAuthorityId, actorId, eventId, position, activatedAt, priorHead: short head, dispositionCount: U53, contestCount: U53}, dispositions: [{eventId, position, type: "ATTEMPT_DUTY_DISPOSITION_RECORDED"\|"ATTEMPT_DUTY_CONTEST_RECORDED", findingHash}]}` (§13) — at most one per source intent (the rc.1 precondition stands, A-88.1), so the source-intent lookup is singular [A-88.3] |
| `anchor` | `position` as a 16-digit zero-padded decimal | `{position, eventId, anchor, head: Head}` — one per `ANCHOR_RECORDED` |
| `witnessBinding` | verifierKey | `{url, verifierKey, name: \|null, boundAtPosition: U53, policy: {quorum, requireOnOpen, maxUnwitnessedEvents}}` (unbound witnesses are removed; re-binding a bound witness replaces its record: `boundAtPosition` and `policy` become the re-binding event's [A-16.5]) |
| `witnessPolicy` | `"0"` | `{policy: <boundPolicy as written>, position}` — present iff a `WITNESSES_BOUND` **or** `WITNESSES_UNBOUND` event has been accepted; carries the newest such event's policy [A-16.4] [A-56.17] |
| `shadow` | `"0"` | `{count: U53, root: MTH over the source prefix hashes (32-byte values, in position order)}` — migrated histories only (§16.2); present from position 0 at every prefix position as `{count: p+1, root: MTH(shadow hashes 0..p)}`, unchanged after `migratedFrom.position` [A-17.3] |
| `budget` | budgetId | `{budgetId, subjects, metric: Identifier\|null [A-26.1] [A-56.15], period, amountCap, countCap, perOperationCap, notBefore, expiresAt, status: "ACTIVE"\|"CLOSED", declaredAtPosition: U53, firstPeriod: U53, carryBase: null \| {periodIndex: U53, carryIn: Amount}, updatedAtPosition: U53\|null, closedAtPosition: U53\|null}` (§9) |
| `budgetUsage` | `budgetId + "/" + periodIndex` (decimal) | `{budgetId, periodIndex: U53, amount: Amount, count: U53}` |
| `counterparty` | counterpartyId | `{counterpartyId, keys: [{keyId, scheme:"ed25519", credential}], status: "ACTIVE"\|"RETIRED", lastReceiptDigest: ContentHash\|null, receiptCount: U53, position (of the latest registration)}` — re-registration keeps `lastReceiptDigest` and `receiptCount` (§11.1) |
| `refusal` | refusalId | `{refusalId, refusalSource, decision, code, budgetId: \|null, actorId, action, resource, amount: \|null, evaluationTime, eventId, position}` |
| `effect` | effectId | `{effectId, intentId, measurerKind, measurerId, metric, unit, amount, observedAt, eventId, position}` |
| `observation` | observationId | `{observationId, reconcilerAgentId, source (as written), call (as written), match (as written), equivocates: U53\|null, eventId, position}` (§10.3) [A-71.4] |
| `reconciliation-cursor` | `hashCanonical([reconcilerAgentId, streamId])` | `{reconcilerAgentId, streamId, lastEventRef: Identifier, lastPosition: U53, count: U53}` — created by the first accepted observation of the pair with `count = 1`; every later one sets `lastEventRef = source.eventRef`, `lastPosition = p`, `count += 1`, so `count` equals the number of accepted observations of the pair [A-89.1]; `eventRef` is opaque (§10.3) [A-71.4]. Beyond the `event` record that every event adds (§5.2.1 step 3), an accepted observation adds its `observation` record and, for the first observation of a `(reconcilerAgentId, streamId)` pair, the `reconciliation-cursor` record: `R` grows by **3** for the first observation of a pair and by **2** after. The `(streamId, eventRef)` index is derived, not a record (§7.1 invariant). [A-87] [A-170.2] |

Not state records (derivable or deliberately outside): checkpoints (they are committed to as `event` records only), pending rotations (side file), the pin, and the derived indices `foreign(σ)` (§6.5.7 [A-117.1]), the `(streamId, eventRef)` observation index (§10.3 [A-86.1]) and `ownerTime` (§6.5.6 [A-179.2 (A-22.6)]). The conformance history pins the roots of five states (`replay/v2/accepts-prefix-through-cp-1/2/3`, `accepts-budget-history`, `accepts-genesis-only`) and two more in the export vector; it exercises the kinds `event, genesis, ownerKeyEpoch, principal, agent, role, tenure, session, authority, usage, nonce, intent, budget, budgetUsage, counterparty, refusal, effect` (these pinned roots are regenerated at E5 under A-54, P.3). The kinds `succession, receipt, obligation, duty, anchor, witnessBinding, witnessPolicy, shadow`, `observation, reconciliation-cursor` (A-71's own pinned root, after E5 [A-71.9]), `ownerKeyEpoch` > 0 and the consumption/outcome/observation fields of `intent` are pinned by the builder's regenerated suite and cross-checked independently before acceptance (BUILD G2.5).

### 7.3 `STATE_CHECKPOINT` ◆
```
data = { checkpointId: Identifier, head: {hash, position} (short head), stateRoot: ContentHash, recordCount: U53,
         derivationVersion: "irontrellis-state/2", projectionHead: ContentHash | null }
```
Checks, in order: schema; step 0 and control authorization (§6.1; under an owner policy the §6.5.3 replay rule — a checkpoint is a ◆ **event** and needs the full `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` counting signatures, default 1, `LAPSED` keys counting [A-174.1] [A-107.2]); the time rule T2 (§6.5.6, under `ATTESTED`, immediately after control authorization) → `TRANSITION_INVALID` [A-172.1]; `head` MUST equal the short head immediately before this event (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); `checkpointId` MUST be unique among accepted checkpoints of the history (`TRANSITION_INVALID`; the derived index is rebuilt from `event` records of type 35, `subjectId` = `checkpointId`, §12.2 row 35) [A-46.3, as drafted]; `stateRoot` and `recordCount` MUST equal `rootAt(position−1)` and its leaf count (`STATE_ROOT_MISMATCH`); `projectionHead` MUST equal the §12.3 projection head at `position−1`, or be `null` iff the genesis turned the projection off (`PROJECTION_HEAD_MISMATCH`); capacity (§5.2, as a control event: absolute limits only). A checkpoint adds only its own `event` record.

**Emission (writer contract).** The local API emits one on `checkpoint()`, at the end of `succeed()` (the handover seal) and, when `checkpointPolicy.everyEvents` is set, automatically. [A-46, as drafted, modified by G2] The `checkpointPolicy.everyEvents` counter counts accepted events other than `STATE_CHECKPOINT`, so `everyEvents: 1` emits one checkpoint after each non-checkpoint event and never loops [A-46.1, as drafted]; it resets at every accepted checkpoint, manual or automatic, including the `succeed()` seal, and an automatic checkpoint never triggers another [G2]. An automatic checkpoint is suppressed when the state after the triggering event would make a one-record non-control event refusable under §5.2.1 step 2 (`R(p) + 1 > T_R` or `B(p) ≥ T_B`), or when `capacity()` reports `RESERVED` or `FULL`; manual `checkpoint()` and the `succeed()` handover seal are unaffected (they are control events admitted until the absolute cap, A-0) [A-46.2, as drafted]. The skipped checkpoint is reported once per open as `CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason: RESERVE}` [A-46.2, as drafted]; a failed automatic checkpoint is reported (`CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason}`) and never obscures the committed triggering event's result [G2]. [A-66] The counter is the number of accepted non-checkpoint events since the most recent accepted `STATE_CHECKPOINT` (manual, automatic or seal), derived from history and recomputed on reopen; attempted or failed checkpoints do not reset it. After an append that leaves the counter `≥ everyEvents` the writer attempts at most one automatic checkpoint, subject to the suppression above; `reason ∈ {RESERVE, NO_SIGNER, SIGNER_FAILED, STORE_FAILED}` (`RESERVE` once per open, the others on every occurrence). Opening a history never appends; the next qualifying append retries once; an append whose outcome is unknown after a crash is resolved from the file on reopen (a committed checkpoint line counts as accepted). The default policy is `null` (manual plus after handover; founder answer 5). The API refuses to write a checkpoint whose root disagrees with its own state with `CHECKPOINT_MISMATCH` (a self-check that must never fire).

**Checkpoints under a quorum and the handover seal.** [A-174.2] [A-119.3] The `succeed()` handover seal and an automatic checkpoint are subject to the same rule: when the handle's signers cannot form a counting set of `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` entries (§6.5.8, which defines `CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason: NO_SIGNER}` over the counting set and T2: the writer's signers cannot form a counting set of `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` entries with `cls ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED, AGENT}` that contains at least one `HUMAN` or `LAPSED` entry, or every such set would violate T2 at the writer's intended timestamp [A-119.3]), the seal is not written, `succeed()` completes and reports `CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason: NO_SIGNER}`; it is never written short. The succession events themselves are ◆ events under their own `M_eff` and go through the two-phase writer (§6.5.8) when `M_eff ≥ 2`. Single-phase `checkpoint()` under `M_eff(35) ≥ 2` with insufficient local signers throws `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID {required, present}` (the §6.5.8 pre-check); the ceremony uses `prepareControlEvent`/`submitControlEvent` for type 35 [A-174.2]. An automatic checkpoint under lapse is therefore not suppressed for want of a `HUMAN` signer, because `STATE_CHECKPOINT` counts `LAPSED` (§6.5.3) [A-119.3]; an in-file checkpoint is a resume root at any position (§7.4) [A-111.2]. The witness option `MAX_UNWITNESSED` MUST NOT refuse a `STATE_CHECKPOINT` (§6.1 [A-176]).

### 7.4 Head attestation (pin, anchors)
`irontrellis-head-attestation/2`:
```
body = { "version": "irontrellis-head-attestation/2", "domain": <genesis domain>, "head": <full head>, "stateRoot": ContentHash, "projectionHead": ContentHash | null }
attestation = { ...body, keyId, scheme, signature over hashCanonical(body) }
```
`domain` is a plain label for auditors (`head.hash` already commits to the genesis); format 1 kept it too (HF1 §9). The pin file `<history>.head`, the local anchor log and the Rekor/custom anchors carry version-2 attestations for format-2 histories (format-1 files keep version 1). [A-5] [A-56.18] Remote-witness head statements do **not** carry `/2` attestations: the witness protocol stays `irontrellis-witness/1` and the head statement stays `irontrellis-head-statement/1` (WITNESS §9.1), read for format 2 as `historyId = recordHash(0)`, `domain` = the format-2 genesis domain (`version: "2"`), `head` = the §4.4 head (position 0 has `hash = historyId`), owner-signed over `hashCanonical(statement)`; `Register {genesis}` accepts a genesis valid under §5.1 and computes `historyId = recordHash(0)`, and seeds epoch 0's `policy` from the genesis `ownerPolicy` [A-100.1]; the witness key-event path (WITNESS §9.2, admin import, §9.9 bundles) verifies a format-2 `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` against the §6.1 `/2` control challenge with `previousHead` the chain head before the event, its `nextKeyAuthorizations` over that same challenge, **and its `additionalControlAuthorizations` under the §6.5.3 replay rule with the pre-event policy `P` taken from the witness's epoch in force at `previousHead.position + 1` (`epochs[i].policy`, default `{threshold: 1}` when absent), the admissible signer set `adm(e)` of §6.5.4 against that epoch's `nextKeyDigests`, and the counting rule of §6.5.3; the witness then records the new epoch with `policy?` copied as written from `data.ownerPolicy`** [A-100.1]; head statements carry no `stateRoot`/`projectionHead` (a witness-held root would be a new statement and protocol version, not format 2); format-1 histories keep `/1` statements with `domain.version "1"`; legacy histories stay unwitnessable.

**The witness's result and an older witness.** [A-100.1] [A-111.3] [A-120.1] [A-170.6] [A-177] `verifyRotationEvent(event, {domain, previousHead, epochs})` returns, beside `preRotated` and `commitmentOnly`, `policyRotation`, `restoration` and `recovery` (booleans: the predicates of §6.5.4 and §6.5.5), `stateChecked: false` (a constant, for readers of the result [A-120.1]) and the counting listing of §18 (counting and non-counting entries with the class of each) [A-100.1] [A-170.6]. A witness whose epochs lack `policy` for an epoch that was set with one (an older witness) is **behind**, not wrong: it reports `WITNESS_POLICY_UNKNOWN` for that rotation and does not accept it; admin import and §9.9 bundles carry the epoch records with `policy` [A-100.1]. The witness's epoch record gains `policyKnown: boolean` (a policy-free epoch set under §6.5 is `policyKnown: true, policy: absent`) [A-111.3]. A witness that holds `policy` for the epoch in force at a head statement's `head.position` verifies the statement's signer's class under that policy (`cls` of §6.5.3 with `t = head.canonicalTime`): `DECLARED`: not `AGENT`; `ATTESTED`: `HUMAN` at `head.canonicalTime`. An unqualified signer is reported as `WITNESS_SIGNER_UNQUALIFIED` and the statement is not recorded as the history's head; a witness without `policy` for that epoch reports `WITNESS_POLICY_UNKNOWN` as before [A-111.3]. The witness protocol version stays `irontrellis-witness/1` (the statement shape is unchanged; only the verifier's inputs grow): format 2's only obligations on the witness text are A-5, A-100.1, A-111.3 and A-120.1, which `WITNESS.md` specifies (E-10) [A-100.1] [A-177].

**What a rotation verifier checks (`acceptSF`).** [A-120.1] [A-131.2] [A-120.2] The witness, the resume step that derives epochs from key events (§7.5 step 0) and the export key-event verifier (§15) check the **state-free subset** of Appendix A row 26, `acceptSF(e, epoch, previousHead, genesis)`: row 26 with these rules omitted — the owner-key rule of §6.5.4 (foreign owner keys, A-117.2), the `foreign` clause of the commitment rule of §6.5.4 (A-117.3) and the `foreign` clause of the attester rule of §6.5.2 (A-117.4). The `attCreds(P(p))` clause of the commitment rule **stays** in `acceptSF`, since the epoch record carries `P(p)` [A-131.2]. Everything else (step 0, control authorization with counting, T2, the §3.3 key test, policy validity with `historyId = recordHash(0)`, HF1 §7.1–7.2 as read in §6.5.4) is computable from the event, the epoch record in force, `previousHead` (its `canonicalTime` is `τ`) and the genesis; the rules that read `σ` records are replay-only. `verifyRotationEvent` implements `acceptSF`. Full replay is the authority for every rotation: `Accepted(e, p) ⇒ acceptSF(e, epoch(p), head(p − 1), genesis)` (Appendix F), so an `acceptSF` verifier never refuses a rotation full replay accepted, and accepts a rotation full replay refuses only through the omitted foreign-credential rules, which it does not claim to check; on an accepted history the epochs derived by any `acceptSF` verifier equal the epochs of full replay [A-120.1] [A-120.2].

Verification yields four facts and `valid = all`: `signatureValid` (the body names this history's domain and the signature verifies under an owner key in force at `head.position`), `prefix` (the history's record hash at that position equals `head.hash`), `stateRootValid` (`rootAt(head.position)`), `projectionValid` (the §12 head at that position, or both `null`). Vectors: `attestation/v2/*`. An asynchronous signer (KMS, threshold) may leave a pin without an attestation for a while; a reader treats such a pin as "no signed root yet" and falls back to the latest checkpoint (§7.5).
