**Which attestations may seed a resume; mismatch is misuse evidence.** [A-20] [A-101.0] [A-111.1] [A-120.0] A head attestation is a resume root only if it verifies under a key of the owner epoch in force at the file's **current head** (read as the **append epoch**, the epoch in force at `h + 1` for head position `h`: its policy governs the next event, and the current owners vouch for a root [A-120.0]); an attestation by a key retired at or before the head still validates a prefix (rc.1 rule) but never a snapshot. Under an owner policy (§6.5) a `/2` head attestation at `head.position` is a resume root (§7.5 step 3) only if its signer's credential satisfies **all** of (class by `cls` of §6.5.3, applied to the pin signer; under the default policy every key is `HUMAN` by declaration and nothing below changes):
(a) under `P(head.position)` at `t = head.canonicalTime`: `cls ≠ AGENT`, and under `ATTESTED` `cls = HUMAN` (the signer is in the attested set `A(P(head.position), head.canonicalTime)` of §6.5.5) [A-92.3] [A-93.4] [A-101.0];
(b) under the policy of the append epoch at the file's current head (the epoch already used for key membership above): `cls ≠ AGENT`, and under `ATTESTED` the credential is the subject of a statement in that policy, any interval. For a non-member, class is by credential: `AGENT` iff some `keyId` of that epoch's keys with the same credential is classed `AGENT`. The time test is (a)'s only, at `head.canonicalTime` of the attestation; (b) tests membership and class, never time: a pin signed at `5` under `[0, 10)` remains a root when the file head reaches `11` provided (b) and (c) hold [A-101.0] [A-111.1] [A-120.0];
(c) under that same append-epoch policy, `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT) = 1` (`byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT ?? threshold`). When it is `≥ 2`, no `/2` head attestation is a resume root: only an in-file `STATE_CHECKPOINT`, which carried `M_eff(35)` counting signatures, seeds a resume; the deployment's checkpoint threshold defines the assurance of every resume root, and the `/2` pin that cites a checkpoint's root is single-key [A-111.1] [A-174.1].
Either failure: not a resume root; the reader falls back to `FULL_REPLAY` with the reason reported; the attestation still validates its prefix and still serves rollback detection [A-101.0]. In-file `STATE_CHECKPOINT`s, bound by the chain, remain valid resume roots at any position (they passed §6.5.3 under their own epoch) [A-111.2]. Full replay is never substituted by an asserted root that an unqualified key signed: a `/2` attestation signed by an `AGENT`, `NONE` or `LAPSED` key is never a resume root, and a pin signed while `HUMAN` stays one under this rule [A-93.4] [A-111.1]. A `/2` attestation inside an external anchor obeys the same rule when used as a resume root [A-94.2]. The same rule, with "file head" read as the view's last epoch, governs an export view's `attestations.from` (§15) [A-101.1] [A-111.1]. Every opener that has replayed (`FULL_REPLAY` or a fallback) and `verify` check `stateRoot` and `projectionHead` of the pin attestation and of every `/2` anchor-log entry against `rootAt` and the projection head at that position; a valid signature over a wrong root is `ATTESTED_STATE_ROOT_MISMATCH {reason: PIN | ANCHOR_LOG}`, reported as evidence of owner-key misuse, never as `SNAPSHOT_STALE`. [A-57.1] `ATTESTED_STATE_ROOT_MISMATCH` is emitted only when a successfully replayed history disagrees at the attested position with the signed `stateRoot` or `projectionHead`; if the replay itself fails, the store failure is reported and no misuse is inferred from any cache (`attestation/v2/wrong-state-root` pins `stateRootValid:false`). `verify` lists every pin and anchor-log entry whose signer is `AGENT`, `NONE` or `LAPSED` at its `canonicalTime` (report-level, §18) [A-111.3].

**Anchoring, federation and the companion documents (informative).** [A-94.0] [A-94.1] [A-94.2] [A-186.4] Anchoring and federation are outside Core. The only Core objects an external anchor carries are the `/2` head attestation (verified exactly as this section says), the head, the state root and the projection head; an anchor statement is never a Core event, challenge or record, and `ANCHOR_RECORDED` (type 25, HF1 §10) remains available unchanged for a history-side note of an external anchor and is not required [A-94.0]. SECURITY states: "An anchored head is evidence that a committed prefix existed, not proof that no newer head exists; a chain transaction authenticates its publisher, not the owner." "Anchoring and federation do not change what a format-2 verifier accepts; no certificate, inclusion proof or quorum can admit an event that replay refuses or refuse one it admits." The pre-dispatch barrier (finality before a governed effect) is a host/writer policy outside Core; it uses no Core refusal code; a delayed anchor is reported, never recorded as a refusal [A-94.1]. An anchor publisher's chain key is never an owner key (§6.3), and a committee that holds an owner key holds one signature toward `M`, like any key [A-94.2]. The anchor outbox applies the signer test of this section before publishing a `/2` attestation (informative here; the profiles document makes it a profile MUST) [A-111.3] [A-185.2]. The anchoring profiles are specified in the companion document **`SPEC-IRONTRELLIS-ANCHORING-PROFILES-r1.md`** (A-140–A-145, A-150, A-154, A-155, A-158–A-165, A-167–A-169, A-181.3, A-182, A-183, A-185.2) and the zero-knowledge ABI in **`SPEC-IRONTRELLIS-ZK-ABI-v2-r1.md`** (A-146, A-147, A-152, A-153, and the profile rule of A-148.2 [A-198.4]); both use this document's tag convention and are drafted after r5. Core carries only the `verify` listings they feed — the committee-key listings (A-140.1), the checkpoint-threshold listing (A-151.2) and the anchor-time bracketing listing (A-181) — which §18 lists, and the Appendix E items E-32…E-39 (E-38 and E-39 from A-183) [A-186.4] [OR-01].

### 7.5 Trust modes for readers
A reader opens a format-2 history in one of two modes:
* `FULL_REPLAY` (default for `verify`, `migrate`, the Python verifier, provers): replay every event from the genesis.
* `CHECKPOINT_RESUME` (default for the local API, hooks and the gateway): the store keeps a **snapshot** `<history>.state` = `{version: "irontrellis-snapshot/2", position, stateRoot, records: [{kind, key, record}]}` (engine-private file, closed schema), optionally with a journal of record operations after the base (builder E3 plan) [A-19]. Resume procedure, in order:
  0. **Owner keys from the file, never from the snapshot.** [A-19.1] Before any snapshot or journal record is used, the reader establishes the owner key epochs from the history file: epoch 0 from line 0, whose `recordHash` MUST equal `recordHash(0)` (and the configured `historyId` when one is set, else `HISTORY_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` [A-19.4]); each later epoch from an `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` line at a position ≤ the resume position that satisfies `acceptSF` (§7.4; A-120.1 with A-131.2: control authorization and every `additionalControlAuthorizations` entry under the previous epoch's policy with `adm(e)` of §6.5.4 and the counting of §6.5.3, T2, the §3.3 key test, policy validity and the HF1 §7.1–7.2 readings of §6.5.4) against the previous epoch and the chain head before it; the reader establishes each epoch's `policy` from the same lines (§6.5.1), so A-19.3's epoch comparison covers `policy` because it is inside the record [A-91.4]. Full replay (step 5) is the authority for every rotation (A-120.0) [A-174.3]. Eager mode does this in its pass over lines `0…position`; lazy mode MUST still read line 0 and scan every line for `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` (the scan may be a byte-level search for the type literal; its cost counts against the §14 hook budget [A-56.19]), or fall back to eager mode.
  1. [A-39.1, as drafted] "Rollback detection against the pin, the anchor logs and, when the bound witness policy (`witnessPolicy` record) or `IRONTRELLIS_WITNESS_POLICY` sets `requireOnOpen`, the bound remote witnesses, exactly as rc.1-final (`hook.ts` and WITNESS §1); this runs first and is never skipped." A-19.2's keys and per-position hashes apply to the witness comparison too: step (1) uses the file-derived keys of step 0 and per-position hashes taken from lines verified against the chain [A-19.2].
  2. Verify the file's lines `0…position` against the chain (§4.3; hashes only, no signatures, no transitions) up to `head.hash` of the signed root being used — either eagerly, or lazily line by line as a line is first read, against the `event` record of that position once step (3) has matched (the records are then trusted through the signed root), under the lazy line verification rule below, which replaces the comparison of stored `eventHash`/`recordHash` strings [A-33, as drafted] (cost unmeasured, see BUILD §6); a chain break is `INVALID_HISTORY {position}` [A-33].
  3. **Recompute the §7.1 root from the snapshot's records** (base plus journal) and require it to equal the owner-signed root **at exactly the snapshot's position**, accepted only under the file-derived keys [A-19.2]: the pin's `stateRoot` when `pin.head.position == position` and the pin satisfies §7.4's resume-root rule [A-20.1] (clauses (a)–(c) of §7.4: the signer's class under `P(pin.head.position)` at `pin.head.canonicalTime`; its membership and class under the append epoch at the file head; and `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT) = 1` under that policy. A pin signed by an `AGENT`, `NONE` or `LAPSED` key, or any `/2` pin while `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT) ≥ 2`, is not a root, and the reader falls back to `FULL_REPLAY` with the reason reported [A-92.3] [A-93.4] [A-101.0] [A-111.1] [A-120.0]), else a `STATE_CHECKPOINT` at `c = position + 1` (whose `stateRoot` is `rootAt(position)`) whose `controlAuthorization` MUST be verified (an in-file checkpoint is a resume root at any position: it passed §6.5.3 under its own epoch and is chain-bound [A-111.2]); a stored root field is never compared instead of a recomputation; a journal entry is never trusted on its own [A-19.5]. After the match, the snapshot's `ownerKeyEpoch` records MUST equal the file-derived epochs **as of the snapshot position** (`policy` included, since it is inside the record [A-91.4]) [A-19.3] [A-57.2]; otherwise `SNAPSHOT_STALE` and `FULL_REPLAY`. A reader holding an owner signer additionally requires the resumed root to verify under one of that signer's keys in force at the file's head [A-19.3].
  4. Rebuild every index from the records (§7.1 invariant), reading referenced events from the file.
  5. Replay the events after `position` with full validation.

If the snapshot is absent, stale, has no signed root at its position, or its recomputed root does not match an authenticated expected root, the reader discards it with `SNAPSHOT_STALE`, falls back to `FULL_REPLAY`, and rebuilds the snapshot [A-57.1]. Any mismatch anywhere in resume is fail-closed: `FULL_REPLAY`, never partial trust [A-19.3]. The recomputation costs about `2·recordCount` SHA-256 plus canonical encoding; it is budgeted in §14 and MUST NOT be skipped to meet a budget (BUILD §6).

**Lazy line verification (recomputes both hashes, fails closed).** [A-33, as drafted, modified by G2] (Extends A-19.3 and A-19.5 to reads after resume.)
1. Whenever a reader in `CHECKPOINT_RESUME` reads a line `q ≤ position` that it has not verified eagerly (consumption, receipt creation, `why`/`responsible`, `inspectAttemptHistory`, the export view's `events`, A-19.5 journal validation, or any other path), it MUST: parse the line; recompute `eventHash(e_q)` from the canonical form of the parsed event (§4.2) and `recordHash(q)` from the recomputed `eventHash` and the `event` record of `q − 1` (§4.3); and require the recomputed `eventHash`, the recomputed `recordHash`, the line's own `recordHash` field and the line's `previousHash` field all to equal the `event` record of `q` (and, for `previousHash`, the `recordHash` of `q − 1`). A stored string is never compared in place of a recomputation.
2. On any mismatch the operation that read the line is aborted with `INVALID_HISTORY {position: q}` before any answer computed from the line is returned; the snapshot is discarded and reported `SNAPSHOT_STALE`; the line is never skipped, repaired or re-read in a weaker mode; a subsequent `FULL_REPLAY` fails at `q` with the same code. `INVALID_HISTORY` is rc.1-final's chain-break store code (BUILD §4 T3); it gains `{position}` [G2].
3. BUILD T2 adds: an edited prefix line that keeps its original `recordHash` and `previousHash` fields is refused on its first lazy read, from each of the paths in point 1 that the builder implements.

**Witnesses, budgets and rollback.** [A-39.2–3, as drafted] SECURITY states: "A rollback to an older attested copy restores budget usage and erases later refusals and counterparty receipts. A budget is only as strong as the history's rollback detection. Bind a witness with `requireOnOpen` to any history whose budgets matter." CLI: `budget declare` warns when the history has no bound witness or its policy has `requireOnOpen: false`; `init --witness` sets `requireOnOpen: true` unless `--no-require-on-open` is given (judgement: the safe default).

**Snapshot resource limits.** [A-44.2, as modified by G2] The §3.1 limits apply to each state record and to each journal operation's encoded record value, never to the snapshot container. The container holds up to `HARD_RECORDS` entries after journal application. Before allocation the reader MUST check the file size against `snapshot.maxBytes` (default `2 × HARD_BYTES` = 2 GiB, configurable, stated in OPERATOR and DEVIATIONS so the Python port matches) and MUST parse in streaming fashion, counting entries against `HARD_RECORDS`; a violation is `SNAPSHOT_STALE` and `FULL_REPLAY`. Validation is never weakened to fit a resource budget. (Snapshot JSON is also subject to the strict grammar of §3.1 [A-34].) The other A-44 points are applied in §9.5 (rollover index, A-44.1) and §14 (benchmarks and OPERATOR statements, A-44.3–4).

**Reader identity and clock.** [A-19.4] [A-22.4] Hooks, the gateway and the local API SHOULD be configured with `historyId` (= `recordHash(0)`) and MUST refuse a file whose line 0 differs: `HISTORY_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`. A reader that finds `head.canonicalTime > now + maxClockSkewMs` (default 300,000) reports `CLOCK_AHEAD_OF_READER` and denies regardless of `failOpen`. SECURITY states: "history time is the writers' claim; one writer can pin it forward." SECURITY also states: "Replay bounds a session key's timestamp from above only by its session's `expiresAt` [A-22.2], by T1 under `ATTESTED` (statement expiries still ahead of the head, A-131.4) and by T3 when the genesis sets a horizon; a session-carried advance is permanent, and `CONTROL_EPOCH_ADVANCED` does not reverse it." [A-179.5] [A-180.1 (A-22.5)] The file-only listings "session time advance" and "owner event at head time" (`verify`, `inspect`; `TIME_LISTING_SKEW_MS = 300,000` is a fixed constant of the format, not reader configuration; the reader's `maxClockSkewMs` remains for `CLOCK_AHEAD_OF_READER` only) are §18 [A-180.1 (A-22.5)].

**Tests.** [A-19.6] [A-57.3] A snapshot with an added owner key plus a pin signed by that key is refused (`FULL_REPLAY` with the reason reported); a snapshot whose `ownerKeyEpoch` differs from the file's is `SNAPSHOT_STALE`; a corrupt snapshot with a correct pin → `SNAPSHOT_STALE` then a clean replay; a correct history with a wrong signed pin → `ATTESTED_STATE_ROOT_MISMATCH`. Builder-pinned under a policy (§6.5.9): an agent-signed, lapsed-signed or reclassified-agent pin refused as a resume root (`FULL_REPLAY` with the reason), a pin accepted when `HUMAN` under both policies, a pin refused under `byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT = 2` while an in-file 2-of-3 checkpoint seeds the resume, and the append-epoch cases (a last-event rotation lowering `byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT` from 2 to 1 makes the pin a root; raising it from 1 to 2 stops it) [A-101.2] [A-111.4] [A-120.3].

Neither mode authorizes anything from a summary: the snapshot is the reader's own cache, validated against owner-signed roots. Histories still begin at their genesis (no summary-seeded genesis; founder D7 keeps that outside).

## 8. Authorization structures (format 2)

All literals of HF1 §8 move to `/2` and all Amounts become strings. Structures are otherwise identical to rc.1-final, except where this section says so.

### 8.1 Decision procedure
`authorize(state, input)` evaluates, in this order: (1) the rc.1-final authority procedure (domain, policy version, causal time, actor lifecycle, sessions, grants, delegation, intersections, prohibitions, per-authority limits incl. `usage`), producing ALLOW with a proof, DENY with a code or INDETERMINATE with a code; (2) **only if ALLOW**, the budget procedure of §9.4; a budget failure turns the result into `DENY` with `code ∈ BUDGET_*`, `failureCodes = [code]`, `budgetId`, `controllingAuthorityIds = null`, `scopeAssurance` unchanged. Budgets never turn a DENY into ALLOW and are not consulted on INDETERMINATE (vectors `authority-denial-precedes-budgets`, `indeterminate-causal-time`). The authority procedure yields `DENY AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` as a per-authority candidate failure (spec02 §4.2 as amended, §8.1.1 (c)): a candidate whose graph holds a controlling authority whose `usage.admittedCumulativeAmount + request.amount` would exceed MAX (§3.3), or whose `usage.admittedTransactionCount ≥ 2^53 − 1` (§7.2 U53; tested without forming `count + 1`), fails with that code; another successful candidate wins as for every other per-authority limit. The step-10 nonce check and the spec02 §6.3 proof-size preflight run only on a successful candidate, so `AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` beats `NONCE_ALREADY_ADMITTED` and `OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` whenever no candidate succeeds [A-135.2] [A-156.1] [A-178.2]. §3.3's closure sentence and the Appendix A row-13 replay mapping (`TRANSITION_INVALID`) are unchanged [A-135.2].

#### 8.1.1 Authority-procedure restatements
[A-186.2] [A-148.4] §8.1 step (1) incorporates the rc.1-final authority procedure by reference; this document does not reproduce it. Its description is the inherited `02-authorization.md` (cited here as **spec02 §n**; its "§6.3 preflight" is spec02's proof-size preflight, not §6.3 of this document), and the procedure itself is the one frozen in **build1**, read with the restatements of this subsection [A-134.0] [A-148.4]. Where spec02's description and build1 differ on a point no vector pins, build1 is the procedure and the description is amended to it. Every accepted admission carries a proof that replay re-derives byte for byte (§8.3), so a change to the procedure's decisions is a decision-rule change and is never additive; the three such changes that were asked for are deferred to a format minor as E-27 (§9.5), E-29 ((h) below) and E-31 ((g) below), Appendix E [A-127.0] [A-133.4]. Acceptance for a port is: §8.3 byte-equality of every re-derived admission proof over the 86 frozen vectors, the nine roots and the regenerated suite; §10.1 re-derivation of every CORE/BUDGET refusal (`REFUSAL_NOT_REPRODUCED` otherwise); and the `authorization/v2r` decision vectors for the DENY and INDETERMINATE codes that no accepted event carries. A port that matches build1 on all three is conforming; a divergence is an engine finding, not a text question [A-148.4]. The restatements (a)–(i) are spec02 amendments and readings. None changes the replay verdict of any accepted history, any frozen vector, pinned root or code: the raw path has no replay inputs, and the overflow and nonce cases are unreachable in every accepted history [A-134.3] [A-135.4] [A-136.4] [A-137.3]. Appendix F carries ASM-1…ASM-5, the forms of (e)–(h) written for the checker and the Lean model; the `PR_eff` clauses that rely on them are §6.5.3 [A-186.2].

**(a) An Amount at an input slot is a type; a non-Amount is a capture failure.** [A-134.0] [A-134.1] [A-134.2] In format 2 an Amount is the §3.3 type, and an authorize input is captured under the same type rules, so a non-Amount at an Amount slot is a capture failure, decided before causal time and never a verdict about the request (in rc.1 the transport carried a BigInt marker, so `MAX + 1` reached spec02 §4 step 5 as a value and was `DENY / INVALID_AMOUNT`). spec02 §2.2 step 7 (deep capture) gains: *"Every Amount slot of the input (`request.amount`; grant `constraints.maxAmount`, `maxCumulativeAmount`; `usage[].admittedCumulativeAmount`) MUST hold a §3.3 Amount string; a JSON number, `null`, a `$continuity.bigint` marker, a string failing the grammar, longer than 78 characters or exceeding MAX is a limit error ⇒ `INDETERMINATE / INVALID_INPUT` at capture (never a throw: trap 22's `TypeError` on a marker is an engine defect, fixed to `INVALID_INPUT`). `amount: undefined` behaves as absent."* spec02 §4 step 5 and §7.1 step 6 end: *"Then `amount` (if own) is already a §3.3 Amount by capture; `INVALID_AMOUNT` is unreachable in format 2 and is retained in `DenialCode` for the legacy and format-1 shadow re-derivation of prefix refusals (§16.2 step 3), where the source rules apply."* Trap 22 reads: *"Invalid amount is a capture failure in format 2: a non-Amount at any Amount slot ⇒ `INDETERMINATE / INVALID_INPUT` before causal time; `INVALID_AMOUNT` is emitted only by the shadow context of a migrated prefix."* spec02 §2.4's note "bad values ⇒ DENY `INVALID_REQUEST`/`INVALID_AMOUNT`" loses `INVALID_AMOUNT`. Replay: none (build1 already does this; every native history was produced by it). Writer: the marker throw becomes `INVALID_INPUT`; no decision or record existed, so no history changes [A-134.3].

**(b) Capture precedence in the raw path.** [A-136.0] [A-136.1] [A-136.2] [A-136.3] The deep capture (spec02 §2.2, §7.1 step 1) applies the **generic** limits and the **primitive grammars**; the **named** corpus limits are applied by the three-valued validators at §7.1 step 7, after causal time. spec02 §7.1 steps 1 and 7 read: *"1. Outer/deep capture (§2.2): `operationVersion`, own-key and exact-shape rules; the generic limits (any list > 4,096 members, any record > 256 fields, nesting > 32, total > 16 MiB, ProtocolString > 4,096 bytes); the primitive grammars at every typed slot (ContentHash, U53 except `evaluationTime`, Signature, and Amount per (a)) ⇒ `INVALID_INPUT` / `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`. Identifier length (256 units/bytes), `actions`/`resources` size (256), `requiredIntersectionIds` size (32) and the 1,024-authority bound are not tested here."* Steps 2–6 are unchanged (step 2 keeps `permissions.length + prohibitions.length ≤ 1024`; step 4 keeps causal time). *"7. `rawCorpusFromCapture`: the three-valued validators; `LIMIT` (identifier > 256 units or bytes at a designated slot, `actions`/`resources` > 256, `requiredIntersectionIds` > 32) or a duplicate among valid roots/evidence/usage/revoked ⇒ `INDETERMINATE / INVALID_INPUT`; `INVALID` or a shared `authorityId` ⇒ `DENY / INVALID_DELEGATION`."* The rest of step 7 is unchanged. spec02 §2.2 step 7's limits sentence reads: *"The deep capture raises a limit error ⇒ `INVALID_INPUT` for any list > 4,096, records > 256 fields, nesting > 32, total > 16 MiB, and for a non-Amount at an Amount slot. The named bounds (Identifier 256 units/bytes, `actions`/`resources` 256, `requiredIntersectionIds` 32, 1,024 authorities) are tested at §7.1 step 2 (authorities) and step 7 (the rest) with the same code; in the replay path the equivalent bounds are event-schema rules and never reach `authorize`."* Trap 23's first clause gains "(at §7.1 step 7, after causal time)". The resulting order of codes on a malformed raw input is: `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` / `INVALID_INPUT` (generic shape, primitive grammar incl. Amount) → `INVALID_INPUT` (Phase-1 collections, 1,024 authorities, scope/request shape) → `CAUSAL_TIME_INVALID` → `POLICY_MISMATCH` (DENY) → `INVALID_REQUEST` (DENY) → `INVALID_INPUT` (named corpus limits) / `INVALID_DELEGATION` (DENY) → corpus depth `INVALID_INPUT` → algebra. The five "unsafe number" host cases remain outside the transport boundary: a JSON number at an Amount slot is `INVALID_INPUT` by (a); a non-integer or unsafe `evaluationTime` stays `CAUSAL_TIME_INVALID` (trap 8). Hence 257 actions with `evaluationTime = −1` is `CAUSAL_TIME_INVALID`, and `maxAmount = MAX + 1` with `evaluationTime = −1` is `INVALID_INPUT` [A-136.0] [A-136.3]. Replay and writer: none (raw path only) [A-136.4].

**(c) `AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` is candidate-local.** [A-135.0] [A-135.1] [A-156.1] [A-166] [A-178.2] `AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` is a per-authority limit of the candidate, exactly like `CUMULATIVE_AMOUNT_EXCEEDED`: the same sum tested against the type bound instead of the grant bound. It is decided inside spec02 §4.2, before winner selection, never after; a successful candidate that would overflow is a failed candidate and another successful candidate wins. This keeps ASM-1 (Appendix F) true — a post-winner check on the selected path's usage would let a revocation turn `DENY / AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` into `ALLOW` — and mirrors §9.4 step 5. spec02 §4.2 step 4 gains, after `cumulativeExceeded`, the set *"`overflow`: usage entry exists and either (amount defined and `usage.admittedCumulativeAmount + amount > MAX`, §3.3) or (`usage.admittedTransactionCount ≥ 2^53 − 1`, §7.2 U53, tested without forming `count + 1`); computed over every grant of every path in the graph, like the sets above; `failingAuthorityId` is the least overflowing id on either disjunct."* The count stays in the same set with the same code: a new `DenialCode` would be a 37th member of a closed decision and receipt enumeration (§18) for a case no accepted history can reach [A-156.0]. Step 5's order is the twelve codes *"`REVOKED, NOT_YET_VALID, EXPIRED, MISSING_INTERSECTION, ACTION_NOT_ALLOWED, RESOURCE_NOT_ALLOWED, AMOUNT_REQUIRED, AMOUNT_EXCEEDED, CUMULATIVE_AMOUNT_EXCEEDED, AMOUNT_OVERFLOW, TRANSACTION_COUNT_EXCEEDED, NO_AUTHORITY`"*; the spec02 §6.1 denial sort and `result.code = failures[0].code` apply unchanged. When `maxCumulativeAmount` is defined, `cumulativeExceeded` is true whenever the amount disjunct is, so that disjunct is reachable only for a grant without `maxCumulativeAmount`. The raw path applies the set to **supplied** usage on both disjuncts, as it does for the sets above (§8.1 incorporates one procedure; `scopeAssurance` labels the provenance of the scope, not a different algebra) [A-166]. **Which ledger each set reads** [A-200.1]: natively, `cumulativeExceeded` and `transactionCountExceeded` read the capped index `authorityUsage` (§7.2: it "still drives the decision and the proof's `usageSnapshot`"), and `overflow` reads the §7.2 `usage` record of every controlling authority of the candidate's graph on both disjuncts (a controlling authority with no `usage` record has admitted nothing and cannot overflow); on the supplied-input path there is one supplied `usage[]` list, validated at §7.1 step 7, and every set reads it. "The same sum" above means the same arithmetic form, not the same ledger; the "whenever" sentence holds because for a capped, unrevoked controlling grant the capped projection of `usage` equals `authorityUsage` (§7.2 [A-148.5]), a builder-tested property of every accepted history. An engine whose native `overflow` reads only `authorityUsage`, and so never tests an uncapped controlling authority, diverges from this text; the divergence is unreachable in an accepted history (§5.2) and is a builder fix within existing rules, pinned by the `authorization/v2r` count-overflow vectors (P.8) [A-200.2]. Answers pinned by vectors (`authorization/v2r`): a canonical successful candidate overflows and another would not — the other wins; both nonce and overflow — `AMOUNT_OVERFLOW`, the nonce step not being reached; the oracle's "post-winner" and "post-nonce" variants are retired and "candidate-local" is the one reading [A-135.3]. Replay: a format-2 history never holds `usage > MAX` (§3.3 closure) and migration refuses a source state that would (`SOURCE_STATE_LIMIT`, §18), so no accepted admission is re-derived differently; a CORE refusal with `AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` can exist only in a history written by an engine that placed the code elsewhere, and the builder reports any such test history [A-135.4]. §9.4 is untouched [A-156.1].

**(d) The step-10 nonce test.** [A-137.3] spec02 §4 step 10 gains: *"In format 2 the `nonce` record is created by `TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED` (§7.2, §8.6); the step-10 test is therefore: a `nonce` record for `(actorId, binding.nonce)` exists and names an `intentId` other than `binding.intentId` ⇒ `DENY / NONCE_ALREADY_ADMITTED`. The intent's own reservation never collides (an already-admitted intent is `INTENT_REPLAY` at step 8.12; a declaration under an occupied nonce is refused at declaration, Appendix A row 12). Legacy and format-1 prefixes keep the rc.1 rule (reservation at admission) in the shadow context."* This is what build1 and the `authorization/v2r` vectors do; replay: none.

**(e) A prohibition lives and dies with its anchor path.** [A-127.1] [A-137.1] An unrevoked `PROHIBITION` keeps its effect while its anchor path is live. It loses effect only when a grant in its ancestry is revoked, or when an actor on its anchor path (its own grantor, or the grantor or grantee of a record in `anc(A', σ)`, §6.5.1) becomes `TERMINATED`; in the second case every permission path through a grant tied to that actor loses effect at the same time. A GLOBAL prohibition (empty `anc`) has no anchor path and no lifecycle term: it loses effect only by revocation or outside its own window; its grantor is the global policy source identifier, not an agent record, so no termination names it [A-201.1]. This is what build1 does (`prohibitionIsActive` → `pathActiveThrough`; for a GLOBAL prohibition the activity test reads no lifecycle, as spec02 §4.4 states and as both independent ports of build1 implement it; the builder confirms it and, if build1 differs, build1 wins and A-201 is reissued [A-201.1]); the earlier literal "retains effect regardless of its grantor's lifecycle" was not, and changing the procedure to it would be non-additive [A-127.0]. "Effect" here means **intrinsic activity**, spec02 §4.4 `prohibitionIsActive`, which includes the prohibition's own `[notBefore, expiresAt)` and every anchor grant's window, revocation and (replay) lifecycle; the authorization outcome is a consequence, not the definition. The "revocation-only activity helper" is a diagnostic reading and is not adopted [A-137.1]. The gap this opens under a lowered threshold is closed on the additive side by the `AGENT_TERMINATED` row of `PR_eff` (§6.5.3), which excludes the termination of any actor on an unrevoked prohibition's anchor path [A-127.2] [A-203.1]; Appendix F restates this as ASM-2.

**(f) Intersections fail closed; a "terminated" member defined.** [A-131.3] A member of `requiredIntersectionIds` **fails the path** when the named authority record is missing or revoked, or when its path is not live under the procedure (in particular when its grantee, or any actor on its anchor path, is `TERMINATED`); a failing member is never dropped from the intersection. Either reading of "terminated" is conjunctive, so Appendix F invariant 31 is unaffected (ASM-3).

**(g) `allow`, the proof-size preflight and revoked prohibitions.** [A-133.2] [A-133.3] [A-133.4] The spec02 §6.3 preflight is an output-size rule applied **after** the algebra has found a successful candidate; it reads no authority record and refuses to *emit*, not to *authorize*. For the non-expansion property (Appendix F), `allow(σ, r, t)` is therefore true iff the §8.1 step-1 authority algebra (spec02 §4 steps 1–10, raw path §7.1 steps 1–9), for a request `r` at evaluation time `t` over the records §8.1 reads (`authority`, `agent`, `role`, `tenure`, `session`, `counterparty`, `budget`, `budgetUsage`, `usage`, `nonce`), yields a successful candidate and no step-10 nonce collision, **before** the preflight; equivalently, the final verdict is `ALLOW`, or is `INDETERMINATE / OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` produced by the preflight on a successful candidate. Every other `INDETERMINATE` and every `DENY` is `¬allow` [A-133.2]. ASM-1 (revocation is monotone) is then: for a fixed request and evaluation time, if `σ'` differs from `σ` only in that one `PERMISSION` record is marked `revoked`, and no unrevoked `PROHIBITION` has that permission in its ancestry, then `allow(σ', r, t) ⇒ allow(σ, r, t)`. Proof sketch, which the builder's property test follows and which the Lean theorem of A-199 is to follow (`Authority.G65.revised_allow_revocation_nonexpansion` over the procedure model, stated in `ASM1-THEOREM-FOR-ASTRA.md`; until it lands, ASM-1 is property-tested and not kernel-proved for the executable procedure model, and the 17 Oct edition's `asm1_before_output_limited` / `asm1_after_allows` refute only the superseded final-verdict reading of Appendix D) [A-199.4] [A-205.2]: the winner `W` of `σ'` contains no revoked grant (a path through one fails `REVOKED`); `W`'s graph, its applying prohibitions' activity (the ancestry clause) and its usage are identical in `σ`; so `W` succeeds in `σ` and a winner exists there. The preflight is outside ASM-1: a smaller winner may fit where a larger one did not, and a lowered-threshold or lapsed-key revocation or termination can therefore turn `INDETERMINATE / OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` into `ALLOW` on a smaller granted path — the statement is corrected, not the procedure (SECURITY, Appendix G) [A-133.1] [A-133.3]. Revoked prohibitions **count** toward the preflight bound: spec02 §4.8 lists every applying prohibition in `checkedProhibitionIds` "even if expired or revoked", the list is a proof field (§8.3) and the preflight bound is the field's bound (spec02 §0 `MAX_SET_MEMBERS`); counting only active ones while keeping them in the proof would emit a proof the state-record and capture limits refuse, and dropping them changes re-derived bytes. No change before rc.2. **E-31** (format minor, with E-27): `checkedProhibitionIds` lists only prohibitions active at `evaluationTime`, and a successful candidate whose proof fails the preflight is a candidate failure (new code position after `NO_AUTHORITY`, `OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` carried as the candidate's failure; the next successful candidate wins; `INDETERMINATE / OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` only when no successful candidate fits); under E-31 the final verdict itself is monotone and the carve-out above becomes vacuous [A-133.4]. A global size guard on `σ` as a `PR_eff` clause was considered and rejected (it would withdraw the founder's reduction power to prevent an event that creates no authority); the `PR_eff` rows are unchanged, and the founder may overrule [A-133.5]. The two `authorization/v2r` vectors that record current behaviour, the ASM-1 test scope and the checker default `asm1_scope=algebra` are §6.5.9 [A-133.7].

**(h) Counterparty retirement is non-expansion only.** [A-127.4] [A-137.2] The §8.1 authority procedure reads domain, policy version, causal time, actor lifecycle, sessions, grants, delegation, intersections, prohibitions and per-authority limits; it reads **no counterparty record**. A counterparty's status is read only at Appendix A rows 36, 37 and 40 (a receipt needs an `ACTIVE` counterparty). Retirement changes no §8.1 verdict and no live session; its effect is that no `COUNTERPARTY_RECEIPT_RECORDED` is accepted for it until a re-registration, which is why `COUNTERPARTY_RETIRED` is `PR_eff` (§6.5.3; ASM-4, Appendix F). "Retired ⇒ deny" was never a rule of the effective text; a `counterpartyId`-aware denial in the authority procedure changes re-derived admissions and is **E-29** (format minor, post-rc.2). The SECURITY consequence is Appendix G.

**(i) Readings confirmed.** [A-132] One reading each, where a model's choice could differ from the most natural one; items not listed are representation choices that need no ruling (LP-X-1, 2, 17, 19, 21, 22).

| reading | confirmed as |
|---|---|
| LP-X-3 | Empty and absent optional arrays are distinct (closed schemas; an empty `nextKeyDigests` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`, HF1 §6.1). Shape checks precede authorization (§5 order). |
| LP-X-4 | Admissibility resolves the signer before `cls` (§6.5.3). A statement whose `notBefore` has not passed yields `NONE`; a policy may not carry a statement for an `agentCred` subject (Appendix F invariant 18). |
| LP-X-5 | `K` and `D` are compared as sets; `P_w` is compared **as written** (§6.5.1: canonical bytes, optional-field presence preserved) in every field except `attestations` and `labels` (Appendix F invariant 12). Renewal is strict in both bounds (§6.5.5). |
| LP-X-6, 7, 8, 9 | L3 and L9 at `M_eff(e) ≥ 2`; L12 at `X ≤ 2^53 − 2` with necessity per current epoch; the invariant-42 corollary as restated (§6.5.6, Appendix F) [A-126]. |
| LP-X-10 | `PR_eff` reads `e.timestamp` (§6.5.3) [A-126.5]. |
| LP-X-11, 12 | (e) and (h) above: a prohibition dies with its anchor path and `PR_eff` excludes such terminations; retirement is non-expansion only [A-127]. |
| LP-X-13 | Rollover updates are never `PR_eff`; an unchanged rollover update needs the ordinary quorum (§6.5.3, E-27). |
| LP-X-14 | §9 never claimed `use ≤ current allowance` after a prospective cap lowering; the bound is `use ≤ max(prior use, allowance)` (proved). No rule change. |
| LP-X-15 | §9.5 as written is effective until E-27: saturate `cap + carry` before subtraction; `firstPeriod` cuts off only when `carryBase` is absent (§9.5). |
| LP-X-16 | §8.1 order: the rc.1-final authority procedure (step 1, incorporated by reference, with its own amount rules) decides before any budget arithmetic (step 2, only on `ALLOW`); a comparison that reports the authority stage first is correct. |
| LP-X-18 | The computability sentence of §7.4's rotation verifier is a scope claim about the rules' inputs; `acceptSF_state_free` is the name reserved for the theorem that states it (not in the 17 Oct Lean edition; ordered, A-208). Independent epoch replay is Appendix F's subset-theorem last sentence, checked over the sweep, not implied by the subset theorem. |
| LP-X-20 | "Earliest" is the minimum accepted observation position; a duplicate digest (`OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE`) is refused before a duplicate `observationId` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`), Appendix A row 42 (§10.3). |

The `INTERSECTION-BOUND.md` fuel argument (`(N+1)·1024 + 1`) is a fidelity argument about the Lean model, not a rule of this text; its fail-closed exhaustion is a model artefact that a bounded valid corpus never reaches, and the kernel theorem is proved in Astra's 17 Oct Lean edition as `AuthorityModel.Bounds.selectGraph_fuel_sufficient` (the fixed fuel `(N+1)·1024+1` drains the queue under the premises `corpusStatus c = .valid`, `validateCorpus c = .ok paths` and `main ∈ paths`, which every accepted state satisfies; with `Completeness.selectGraph_no_missing_exact` the selection is exact), which closes the A-137.4 hold of A-186.5 [A-137.4] [A-205.4].

### 8.2 Runtime authorization challenge
```
{ "version": "irontrellis-runtime-authorization/2", "domain", "request": {actorId, action, resource, claimedAt, amount?, counterpartyId?, termsCommitment?, metric?},
  "authoritative": true, "consequential": true, "evaluationTime", "policyVersion", "rootRecognitionPolicy": "declared-principal-root/0.2",
  "eventHistoryHash": <head before the admission>.hash, "eventHistoryPosition": <its position>,
  "runtimeSessionId", "credentialKeyId", "controlEpoch", "roleId", "roleTenureId", "intentId", "nonce" }
```
`request` keeps only the keys present in the request, in the order shown (canonical sorting applies on hashing anyway). `TRANSACTION_INTENT_ADMITTED.runtimeAuthorizationHash` is `hashCanonical` of it; `runtimeSignature` the session's signature over that hash; `expectedHistoryHead` the full head before the admission; the proof's `historyHead` equals it. (This challenge is not changed by A-54 [A-59.2]: it is decision-time proof material, byte-equal to the §8.3 proof, and exists before the admission envelope does; SECURITY keeps the residual sentence for admissions.)

**Writer contract: metric.** [A-11] [A-26.2] [A-56.4] `run()` accepts `metric?: Identifier` (absent = `"amount"`); it is a `run()` option fixed by the connector at construction or from operator configuration, never copied from the tool call's arguments; the declaration and the admission's `request.metric` carry the same value or both omit it. `run()` and the gateway MUST NOT take `metric` from agent-supplied arguments. SECURITY states: "a budget with a named metric binds only requests that state that metric; the requester chooses the metric unless the connector fixes it."

### 8.3 Authorization proof
`proofVersion: "irontrellis-authorization-proof/2"`, `domain` the format-2 domain, `policyVersion` the genesis literal; the remaining fields exactly as HF1 §8.3 (`recognizedRoot`, `permissionPath` normalized, `intersections`, `effectiveConstraints`, `controllingAuthorityIds`, `usageSnapshot`, `authorityEvidence`, `checkedProhibitionIds`, `consequential`, binding fields). The replay re-derives the proof from the pre-event state and the recorded request and requires byte equality of the canonical texts (rc.1-final rule; every Amount in the comparison is the §3.3 string [A-7]). The budget procedure is applied during that re-derivation: an admission that the budgets would refuse is `TRANSITION_INVALID` (vector `rejects-admission-over-budget`). **Metric rule:** `request.metric` (absent ≡ `"amount"`) MUST equal the declared intent's metric (absent ≡ `"amount"`), else `TRANSITION_INVALID` (vector `rejects-admission-metric-mismatch`); the budget checks and the charge (§9.6) use that one metric. Checks for admissions, in order: schema; the epoch fence (`SESSION_FENCED`) [A-58.5]; the rc.1-final admission transition rules (session, binding, proof byte-equality, `evaluationTime == timestamp`, nonce; `TRANSITION_INVALID`); the metric rule; the budget procedure; capacity.

### 8.4 Administrative authorization
`version: "irontrellis-administrative-authorization/2"`; fields unchanged (HF1 §8.4). Action literals unchanged, plus the three ported ones of §13. [A-18.1] `transitionEffectHash` of an administrative authorization is `hashCanonical({transitionEventType: event.type, …the type's effect fields})` exactly as rc.1-final `administrativeTransitionEffect` (authority.ts) and reference-0.3 `portableAdministrativeTransitionEffect`; HF1 §8.4's prose ("the event's data without `administrativeAuthorization`") is read as that function (the legacy and v1 vectors verify only under this reading). [A-18.2] For `OBLIGATION_STATUS_RECORDED`, `request.action = data.action`, matched against the obligation's transition policy (spec 01 §D.7); HF1 §8.4's `record-collection-disposition` is one value of it. The outer ▲ session is subject to the epoch fence (§6.2) [A-58.5].

### 8.5 Receipts (issuer layer)
`payload.schemaVersion: "irontrellis-receipt/2"`; `receiptArtifactCommitment = hashCanonical(["irontrellis-receipt-artifact-commitment/2", contentHash, signature])`. Payload shape unchanged (spec 03 §G.1) with Amount strings. No history bound on the receipt's cited histories.

### 8.6 Intent declaration (new in format 2)
`TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED.data` = HF1 fields + `metric?` + `declarationAuthorization = {runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch, hash: ContentHash, signature}` (all five required). Challenge [A-54: `eventId`, `eventType`, `timestamp` added]:
```
{ "version": "irontrellis-intent-declaration/2", "domain", "eventId", "eventType": "TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED", "timestamp",
  "intentId", "nonce", "actorId", "action", "resource", "roleId", "roleTenureId", "adapterProfile",
  "amount": Amount|null, "counterpartyId": |null, "termsCommitment": |null, "metric": |null,
  "eventHistoryHash": <head before>.hash, "eventHistoryPosition", "runtimeSessionId", "credentialKeyId", "controlEpoch" }
```
`eventId`, `eventType` and `timestamp` are the envelope's; a writer fixes the final envelope before signing [A-54]. Replay: the session binding is checked per §6.2 (fence first) and the session's agent MUST equal `actorId`; failure `DECLARATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`. A missing `declarationAuthorization` is a schema violation (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`); in a native history `declarationAuthorization: null` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`, and `null` is required inside a migrated prefix (§16.2) — the presence of `migratedFrom` decides, never an undefined comparison [A-35.1] [A-52.5]. **Nonce rule (format 2):** the declaration creates the `nonce` record (§7.2); a second declaration with the same `(actorId, nonce)` is `TRANSITION_INVALID` whatever its intentId (vector `rejects-duplicate-nonce-declaration`); the admission's `nonce` MUST equal the declaration's and the record MUST name the intent (rc.1 reserved at admission only because declarations were unsigned). Checks, in order: schema; session authorization (§6.2, `DECLARATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`); actor binding (`DECLARATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`); the nonce rule and the other rc.1 declaration preconditions (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); capacity.

### 8.7 Adapter identity
Unchanged derivations (`continuity-adapter-idempotency/0.2`, `continuity-adapter-submission/0.2`, `continuity-adapter-no-effect/0.2` literals are kept verbatim: they name the *adapter* edition, not the history), computed over format-2 structures (Amount strings, chain heads). [A-17.5] For a three-field domain, `idempotencyKey = hashCanonical(["continuity-adapter-idempotency/0.2", domain.protocol, domain.version, domain.deploymentId, intentId], sha256)` — exactly rc.1-final's format-1 branch (`adapter-editions.ts:245`); no `chainId`/`verifyingContract` placeholders. The builder's regenerated suite pins them.

### 8.8 Lifecycle authorization (types 14, 15, 16) [A-21] [A-51] [A-58]
In format 2 `TRANSACTION_INTENT_CONSUMED`, `TRANSACTION_OUTCOME_RECORDED` and `RECEIPT_RECORDED` are ◇ session-signed events; they remain non-control for capacity (P.5).

**Schema (native histories and every position outside a migrated prefix).** `data.recordAuthorization = {runtimeSessionId: Identifier, credentialKeyId: Identifier, controlEpoch: U53, hash: ContentHash, signature: Signature}`, nested like `declarationAuthorization`; no new top-level fields; the type's other fields are unchanged from HF1. Type 16's inherited `data.runtimeSessionId` and `data.controlEpoch` MUST equal the nested values. Absence of `recordAuthorization` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` (closed schema). Inside a migrated prefix these types carry **no** `recordAuthorization` (present → `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) [A-52.1].

**Challenge** (`irontrellis-lifecycle-authorization/2`; the envelope `timestamp` is bound from the start):
```
{ "version": "irontrellis-lifecycle-authorization/2", "domain", "eventId", "eventType", "timestamp",
  "effectHash": hashCanonical(<data without recordAuthorization>),
  "eventHistoryHash": <head before>.hash, "eventHistoryPosition", "runtimeSessionId", "credentialKeyId", "controlEpoch" }
```
`hash = hashCanonical(challenge)`.

**Signer binding.** Consumption (14): the session's agent is the intent's `actorId`. Outcome (15): the session's agent is the intent's `actorId`, or `attesterId` when `attesterId` names an admitted agent (`attesterId` stays free text, so rc.1 adapter ids such as `adapter:simulated` need no agent record). Receipt (16): the session's agent is `issuerAgentId`, and the receipt's inherited `runtimeSessionId` MUST equal the signing session (satisfied by construction after the A-61.3 schema check; it cannot fail and an implementation MAY omit it [A-74.3]).

**Code and order.** schema (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) → `SESSION_FENCED` → every §6.2 session check, `hash` and signature → signer binding → inherited rc.1 transition preconditions → bounds → capacity. Every failure after the fence up to and including the signer binding is `RECORD_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (shared with §11.2), except intent resolution (A-60), which is `TRANSITION_INVALID` [A-74.2]; the fence itself is `SESSION_FENCED` [A-61.1]. [A-61.1] The fence is evaluated once the session record is found by `runtimeSessionId` and the event's `credentialKeyId` equals the record's; a missing session or a `credentialKeyId` mismatch is `RECORD_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` before the fence is reached; a found, matching session whose epoch differs from the current epoch is `SESSION_FENCED` regardless of any later fault. The fence never reorders the schema stage [A-61.2]; a type-16 mismatch between the inherited `runtimeSessionId`/`controlEpoch` and the nested values is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` at the schema stage [A-61.3]. [A-60] The signer-binding stage begins by resolving the event's `intentId` in the `intent` records: an absent or not-admitted intent is `TRANSITION_INVALID` for all three types (type 16 included), evaluated after the signature and before the binding; so bad-signature + unknown-intent is `RECORD_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` and valid-signature + unknown-intent is `TRANSITION_INVALID`, as for §10.2's `rejects-effect-for-unknown-intent`.

**Bounds** (independent of signing, code `TRANSITION_INVALID`): at most 16 `outcomes` and 64 `observations` per intent; at most 64 assignments and 64 reviews per duty; at most 16 `receipt` records per intent (derivable from state; there is no per-issuer-session bound). Inside a migrated prefix the bounds are not applied; outside it they count migrated entries [A-52.1].

**Projection.** Rows of types 14–16 set flag bit 1 (§12.1), in native and migrated histories alike.

**Migration.** Prefix events are transcribed source events (§16.2) and are unsigned there [A-52.1]. Format-1 and legacy reads keep rc.1 behaviour [A-28].

**Tests.** Accept/reject per type, tampered binding/hash/signature, wrong actor, type-16 nested/inherited mismatch, two simultaneous failures pinning precedence, one bound case per list; roots and projection pinned in the regenerated suite.

## 9. Budgets

A budget is a stop rule over a *group* of requests. The group is a selector (actors, roles, actions, resources, metric), the limit is a cap per period, per operation or in count, and unused allowance may **roll over** between periods up to a cap. ("Rollover" is this document's word for carry of unused allowance; it is unrelated to the Q profile's "carry" of cumulative usage across checkpoints, which is the `NONE` case.)

### 9.1 Declaration, update, close (all ◆)
```
Subjects = { actorIds: [Identifier]{1..256} | null, roleIds: [Identifier]{1..256} | null, actions: [Identifier]{1..256} | null, resources: [Identifier]{1..256} | null }   // null = any; closed record
BUDGET_DECLARED.data = {
  budgetId: Identifier, subjects: Subjects,
  metric: Identifier | null,                              // matched against the request's metric (default "amount"); null = every metric [A-26.1]
  period: { kind: "NONE" } | { kind: "FIXED_WINDOW", lengthMs: U53 ≥ 1, originMs: U53, carryUnused: boolean, carryCap: Amount | null },
  amountCap: Amount | null, countCap: U53 | null, perOperationCap: Amount | null,
  notBefore: U53 | null, expiresAt: U53 | null }
BUDGET_UPDATED.data = { budgetId, subjects: Subjects, amountCap, countCap, perOperationCap, expiresAt }   // all six keys required; subjects restated in full
BUDGET_CLOSED.data  = { budgetId }
```
Schema (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`): at least one of `amountCap`, `countCap`, `perOperationCap` non-null, for declarations and for the state after an update (vectors `rejects-budget-with-no-cap`, `rejects-budget-update-without-cap`); list members distinct; `Subjects` is closed (`rejects-budget-with-unknown-subject-key`); `expiresAt > notBefore` when both present; `carryUnused: true` REQUIRES a non-null `carryCap` (`rejects-budget-carry-without-carry-cap`) and `carryCap` MUST be null when `carryUnused` is false; `lengthMs ≥ 1` (`rejects-budget-window-zero-length`). Transitions (`TRANSITION_INVALID`): a `budgetId` is declared at most once (`rejects-duplicate-budget-id`); `UPDATED`/`CLOSED` need an ACTIVE budget (`rejects-update-of-unknown-budget`); at most 256 budgets per history, counting CLOSED ones (the 257th declaration is `TRANSITION_INVALID`). An `expiresAt ≤ timestamp` is accepted and means immediate expiry. An update is prospective: usage is retained under the same `budgetId` (so changing `subjects` never resets a period), the new caps and subjects apply from the next evaluation, and the rollover earned so far is frozen (§9.5). A closed budget never reopens.

State: the `budget` record with `status`, `declaredAtPosition`, `firstPeriod = periodIndex(B, max(declaration event timestamp, notBefore ?? 0))`, taken as `0` when that is `null` (declared before the window origin) and always `0` for `NONE`, `carryBase` (§9.5), `updatedAtPosition`, `closedAtPosition`; and `budgetUsage` records.

### 9.2 Matching
A request `(actorId, action, resource, metric, amount)` evaluated at time `t` **matches** budget `B` iff `B.status = ACTIVE`, `B.notBefore ≤ t` (when set), `t < B.expiresAt` (when set), `actorIds` is null or contains `actorId`, `roleIds` is null or contains a role the actor **currently holds** (a `tenure` record with `status: CURRENT` and `agentId = actorId`; matching is by roles held, not by the role the request acts under, so an actor holding two roles is bound by both roles' budgets; this is how a budget follows a role across a handover — vectors `allow-role-budget-within`, `deny-role-budget-per-operation`; the builder's suite adds the handover case, T6), `actions`/`resources` null or containing the request's value, and `B.metric` is `null` or equals `request.metric` (default `"amount"`) [A-26.1]. A null-metric budget matches whatever `request.metric` is and charges `request.amount` as it stands; it counts as one budget for the set-size rule. Budgets declared after the request's evaluation head do not exist for it.

### 9.3 Periods
`periodIndex(B, t)` = `0` for `NONE`; for `FIXED_WINDOW`: `null` if `t < originMs`, else `floor((t − originMs) / lengthMs)`. Period boundaries are half-open `[origin + k·length, origin + (k+1)·length)`. The clock is the evaluation time (`evaluationTime` of the decision, which for admissions and refusals equals the event timestamp), never the wall clock of the reader. Under `timeSource: EVENT_TIMESTAMP` a writer can move time forward into later windows; that is inherent and stated in SECURITY.

### 9.4 Checks (SPEC ORACLE: `lib.mjs evaluateBudgets`, `remainingAmountOf`)
Let `M` be the matching budgets sorted by `budgetId` byte order. If `|M| > 16` → `DENY BUDGET_SET_TOO_LARGE` (budgetId = the 17th; the CLI warns at declaration when a request could match more than 16). Then for each `B` in order, with `k = periodIndex(B, t)`, `U = budgetUsage(B, k)` (zero when absent) and `a = request.amount` (0 when absent):
1. `k = null` → `DENY BUDGET_PERIOD_UNDEFINED`;
2. `perOperationCap ≠ null ∧ a > perOperationCap` → `DENY BUDGET_PER_OPERATION_EXCEEDED`;
3. `countCap ≠ null ∧ U.count + 1 > countCap` → `DENY BUDGET_COUNT_EXCEEDED`;
4. `amountCap ≠ null ∧ a > remaining(B, k)` → `DENY BUDGET_AMOUNT_EXCEEDED`, where `allowed(B, k) = min(MAX, amountCap + carryIn(B, k))` and `remaining(B, k) = max(0, allowed(B, k) − U.amount)` (`carryIn` per §9.5, computed over saturated effective allowance [A-50]);
5. `U.amount + a > MAX` → `DENY AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` (reachable only for budgets without `amountCap`; closure of §3.3).
The first failing budget determines `code` and `budgetId`. All comparisons are exact integer comparisons; the saturation in `allowed` keeps every value ≤ MAX (§3.3) so that `U.amount + a ≤ allowed ≤ MAX` after a charge.

### 9.5 Rollover (SPEC ORACLE: `lib.mjs carryIn`, as amended by A-50)
`carryIn(B, k) = 0` if `period.kind = NONE`, or `carryUnused = false`, or `amountCap = null`. Otherwise, with `cap = amountCap`, `C = carryCap`, `used(j) = budgetUsage(B, j).amount`: [A-50]
```
f(p0) = c0
f(j+1) = min(C, max(0, min(MAX, cap + f(j)) − used(j)))      and for an idle stretch (used = 0) of n periods: f(j+n) = min(C, f(j) + n·cap)
carryIn(B, k) = f(k)
```
"Unused allowance" means unused **effective** (saturated) allowance: the inner `min(MAX, cap + f(j))` is `allowed(B, j)`. The idle-stretch closed form stays valid for zero-usage stretches. Intermediate sums MUST be evaluated with sufficient precision (bounded Amounts do not bound intermediates). The start `(p0, c0)` [A-204.1]: when `carryBase ≠ null`, `p0 = carryBase.periodIndex` and `c0 = carryBase.carryIn` for every `k ≥ p0`, so `carryIn(B, carryBase.periodIndex) = carryBase.carryIn` exactly and the recurrence runs from `p0` under the caps in force; `(firstPeriod, 0)` is the start only when `carryBase = null`; for `k < p0` (and, with `carryBase = null`, for `k ≤ firstPeriod`) the value is 0. An engine that restarts from `(firstPeriod, 0)` at `k = carryBase.periodIndex` diverges from this rule and from the frozen vector `allow-within-carry`; no design text (the batch-14 item C11, A-146.4) ever licensed that reading [A-204.2]. Because `C` is required (§9.1), `f` is bounded by `C`, and the closed form for idle stretches makes the cost O(number of periods with usage between `p0` and `k`), never O(elapsed periods) (vector `headroom/v2/b-amount-far-future-idle-stretch`: period 1,000,000 in constant work). The decision procedure changes only at MAX-adjacent inputs no frozen vector reaches; the architect's `lib.mjs carryIn` is amended to match; the index property test of A-44.1 compares against this recurrence; the regenerated suite adds a saturated period followed by a zero-cap update and an admission by a fresh authority, with pinned `carryBase`, decision and root.

**`carryBase` (truly prospective updates).** On every accepted `BUDGET_UPDATED` of a `FIXED_WINDOW` budget with `carryUnused`, the engine stores `carryBase = {periodIndex: k₀, carryIn: carryIn(B_old, k₀)}` with `k₀ = periodIndex(B, event.timestamp)` computed **under the caps in force before the update** (`null` when `k₀ = null`, i.e. before the origin); `carryBase` freezes the value of the recurrence above [A-50]. The rollover already earned is therefore frozen; raising or lowering a cap changes only future rollover (vectors `allow-within-carry` / `deny-amount-over-carry` after the cap lowering 100 → 60, `headroom/v2/b-amount-before-lowering`, `b-amount-period-1`, `b-amount-future-period-carries-cap`). Periods with no usage roll their full cap forward, bounded by `C`.

**Rollover index (implementation, not a format change).** [A-44.1, as drafted; A-50] An engine SHOULD keep, as a derived index rebuildable from `budget` and `budgetUsage` records, for each `FIXED_WINDOW` budget with `carryUnused` the pair `(j*, f(j*))` for the last period `j*` with usage below the current evaluation, so that `carryIn(B, k)` is O(1) amortized. The index is not a format change; a property test MUST show `carryIn` from the index equals `lib.mjs carryIn` on random usage patterns, including after `BUDGET_UPDATED` resets `carryBase`. Under A-50 the test compares against the recurrence above (the architect's `lib.mjs carryIn` is amended to match; the builder reports any divergence).

**Reading in force until E-27.** [A-132] [A-116.0] This section as written is effective until E-27 (Appendix E): `cap + carry` is saturated before the subtraction (the inner `min(MAX, cap + f(j))` above), and `firstPeriod` cuts the recurrence off only when `carryBase` is absent (the start rule above). The engine fix proposed in round 3 — starting the recurrence at `max(carryBase.periodIndex, firstPeriod)`, so that an update before a budget's first period cannot accrue rollover over periods the budget never had — is deferred to a format minor, E-27: this section is a decision rule, every accepted admission carries a proof that replay re-derives byte for byte (§8.3), and changing the recurrence would change the re-derivation of any existing history with such an update [A-116.0]. Until then the additive side carries the consequence: an update of a budget that rolls over is never `PR_eff` and needs the ordinary quorum, and a lapsed or single human tightens such a group by declaring a new non-rollover budget over it instead (§6.5.3, Appendix G) [A-132]. §9 never claimed `use ≤ current allowance` after a prospective cap lowering; the bound is `use ≤ max(prior use, allowance)`, and no rule changes [A-132]. The checker's `carry_base=clamp-first-period` reading stays a diagnostic (§6.5.9).

### 9.6 Charging
Only `TRANSACTION_INTENT_ADMITTED` charges: for every budget matching the admitted request at the admission's `evaluationTime` (with the metric rule of §8.3), `budgetUsage(B, k)` gains `count + 1` and `amount + a`. Consumption, outcomes (including FAILED with a no-effect proof), effects and refusals never change usage. There are no refunds (the proven Q semantics; a refund policy would be a format change). [A-25.1] Budgets bind admitted intents only. Hook decisions and per-call gateway decisions without `recordCalls` are never charged. A budget whose selectors match only such calls never accumulates usage. (`budget declare` warns when no exactly-once job, `recordCalls` tool or `run()` caller can produce a matching admission; the hook's SessionStart context names the budgets the hook cannot charge [A-25.2]. Whether hooks charge count budgets by admitting each allowed call, or a later format minor adds a light `USAGE_CHARGED` event, is a founder decision; until decided, nothing changes [A-25.3].)

### 9.7 Headroom (pure view)
`headroom(state, budgetId, at)` → `null` for an unknown budget, else `{budgetId, active, periodIndex, usedAmount, usedCount, carryIn, remainingAmount: Amount|null, remainingCount: U53|null}` where `active` = status ACTIVE and within `notBefore/expiresAt` and `periodIndex ≠ null`; `remainingAmount = remaining(B, k)` (§9.4; null when no amountCap; when `periodIndex` is null, usage and rollover are 0 so it equals the cap); `remainingCount = max(0, countCap − usedCount)`. `at` is any U53: a view of the past (`at` before the head, or before the declaration) is computed as-of that time over the *current* record set — it is a view, not a replay (vector `b-amount-period-0-view`). Headroom writes nothing and is not an authorization. Adapter op `headroom` (§17). **Capability.** [A-43.2, as drafted] A runtime handle's `headroom` is limited to budgets whose §9.2 actor and role selectors would match *some* request by the handle's own actor at `at` (the `actions`/`resources`/metric selectors are not tested, since a headroom query names none [A-81]); other budget ids return `null` as if unknown. Owner and auditor handles are unrestricted. PUBLIC_MINIMAL does not apply to headroom (§15, A-43.3).

## 10. Refusal and effect records (◇)

### 10.1 `REQUEST_REFUSED`
```
data = { refusalId: Identifier, refusalSource: "CORE" | "BUDGET" | "CONNECTOR",
         request: {actorId, action, resource, claimedAt, amount?, counterpartyId?, termsCommitment?, metric?},
         evaluationTime: U53, expectedHistoryHead: <full head>, decision: "DENY" | "INDETERMINATE", code: Identifier,
         budgetId?: Identifier (present iff refusalSource = BUDGET), connector?: {connectorId: Identifier, refusalCode: Identifier, detailDigest: ContentHash} (present iff CONNECTOR),
         runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch, refusalAuthorization: {hash: ContentHash, signature} }
```
Challenge (`irontrellis-refusal-authorization/2`) [A-54, confirmed by A-59: `eventId`, `eventType`, `timestamp` added; `frozen-r2.3` reproduces the old challenge]: `{version, domain, eventId, eventType: "REQUEST_REFUSED", timestamp, refusalId, refusalSource, request, evaluationTime, eventHistoryHash: expectedHistoryHead.hash, eventHistoryPosition, decision, code, budgetId: |null, connector: |null, runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch}`; a writer fixes the final envelope before signing.

Checks, in order: schema (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`; `rejects-connector-refusal-without-connector`); session authorization (§6.2, fence first; `REFUSAL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`; `rejects-refusal-with-tampered-code`, `rejects-refusal-signed-by-stranger`); **actor binding**: for `CORE` and `BUDGET` the signing session's agent MUST equal `request.actorId`, for `CONNECTOR` it MUST equal `connector.connectorId` (the connector's own owner-admitted agent; `TRANSITION_INVALID`; `rejects-refusal-actor-mismatch`, `rejects-connector-refusal-by-other-session`); `evaluationTime` MUST equal the event `timestamp` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`; `rejects-refusal-evaluation-time-mismatch`); `expectedHistoryHead` MUST be the head immediately before this event (`TRANSITION_INVALID`; `rejects-refusal-with-stale-head`); for `CORE` and `BUDGET`, replay re-runs §8.1 on the pre-event state with `{request, evaluationTime, consequential: false}` and requires `decision`, `code` and `budgetId` (null for CORE) to be equal, else `REFUSAL_NOT_REPRODUCED` (`rejects-refusal-not-reproduced`, `rejects-refusal-that-would-be-allowed`); a `CONNECTOR` refusal is attested, not re-derived (its `code` MUST be `"CONNECTOR_REFUSED"`); `refusalId` unique among refusals (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); capacity (§5.2). State: a `refusal` record; no usage changes. Refusals are session-signed, so a party without an admitted session cannot fill a history with them; a deployment bounds them with `capacity.maxStateRecords`, and writers coalesce and rate-limit them per actor (§10.1.1). `REFUSAL_NOT_REPRODUCED` is never a replay rejection of a refusal the writer itself signed [A-10.5].

**CONNECTOR attribution is stated, not enforced.** [A-36.2–5, as drafted] For CONNECTOR refusals, `request.actorId` is not required to name an existing agent (so A-38.4 can record attempts by never-admitted actors); replay is unchanged. `verify` and `inspect` flag a CONNECTOR refusal whose `request.actorId` is not an agent of the history, and `verify` lists every agent that signed a CONNECTOR refusal or a CONNECTOR-measured effect. SECURITY, `verify` and `inspect` state: "CONNECTOR means: the named agent says so. Replay does not know which agents are connectors. A refusal recorded by a hook shows the hook's mapping of a tool call; it does not show that the agent signed anything." (Shared with A-38.) An owner-signed connector designation is recorded as a format-3 candidate (DECISIONS). Projection rows are unchanged (changing the row actor for CONNECTOR refusals would change pinned projection heads).

#### 10.1.1 Writer contract for refusals (not a replay rule) [A-11] [A-12] [A-23] [A-38] [A-42] [A-56.3]
1. **Source.** A `recordRefusal` call with `connector` is a CONNECTOR refusal (`code: "CONNECTOR_REFUSED"`, attested; `decision` DENY unless the caller states INDETERMINATE). Without `connector`, the writer takes `refusalSource` (CORE or BUDGET), `decision`, `code` and `budgetId` **from its own §8.1 re-derivation**; a caller-supplied `source` is optional and MUST agree, else `INVALID_INPUT`; `"CONNECTOR"` without `connector` is `INVALID_INPUT`. [A-12.1]
2. **Actor binding before signing.** The handle session's agent MUST equal `request.actorId` (CORE/BUDGET) or `connector.connectorId` (CONNECTOR); else `TRANSITION_REJECTED`. [A-12.2]
3. **Time and head.** Under the writer lock, one clock reading sets `evaluationTime` = event `timestamp`; `expectedHistoryHead` is the head appended to; re-derive at that time on that head, then sign. [A-12.3]
4. **Not reproduced.** A CORE/BUDGET re-derivation that yields ALLOW is `REFUSAL_NOT_REPRODUCED` before any append. [A-12.4] [A-10.3]
5. **Coalescing against the history.** Before recording, the writer looks up the actor's latest `refusal` record (an index rebuildable from the records) and skips the refusal when `(actorId, action, resource, code, budgetId ?? null, connector.refusalCode ?? null)` is identical and that record is younger than `refusals.coalesceMs` (default 60,000). A process-lifetime cache of the same key is only a fast path and reports the same reason. [A-23.1] [A-12.5 as superseded] [A-56.3]
6. **Rate limit.** A per-actor token bucket `refusals.maxPerMinute` (default 6) applies. [A-23.2]
7. **Reporting.** `run()` (and the hook/gateway drain) on DENY/INDETERMINATE with `refusals: "record"` tries to record the refusal and in every case returns the decision unchanged plus whether the refusal was recorded and, if not, why. The one enumeration of not-recorded reasons is: `COALESCED`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `SPOOLED`, `RECORD_DEADLINE`, `NOT_REPRODUCED_AT_DRAIN`, `SESSION_TIME_HORIZON` (the timestamp would fail rule T3, §6.5.6; the entry stays spooled until an owner event moves `ownerTime` [A-179.4 (A-22.6)]), a capacity code, `HISTORY_CONFLICT`, a signer failure, `WRITE_UNCONFIRMED`. `DEDUPLICATED` and `{deduplicated: true}` are withdrawn. Recording never turns a decision into an exception. [A-11] [A-56.3]
8. **Refusal id.** `refusalId` absent → the writer derives it as stated in §10.2 "Generated ids" [A-37.2, as drafted; refines A-12.6's "fresh Identifier"]; a duplicate id is `TRANSITION_REJECTED`. [A-12.6]
9. **Hook and gateway deny path.** [A-38, as drafted, modified by G2]
   (a) *Verdict first* [A-38.1]. Hooks and the gateway compute and emit the decision before any write. Recording a refusal (or an effect) MUST NOT delay the decision beyond `refusals.recordBudgetMs` (default 250). If the budget is exceeded, the refusal input is appended to a local append-only spool file beside the history (engine-private, deletable) and the result reports `{recorded: false, reason: "SPOOLED"}`; if the spool cannot be written, `{recorded: false, reason: "RECORD_DEADLINE"}`.
   (b) *Draining* [A-38.2, as drafted, modified by G2]. The next hook process, gateway call or owner write drains the spool. A drained entry is recorded as a **new** refusal at the head then current: re-derived at that head and time (A-12.3), signed by the then-current session. If re-derivation yields ALLOW it is dropped with reason `NOT_REPRODUCED_AT_DRAIN` (never `REFUSAL_NOT_REPRODUCED` thrown). A-23's coalescing and rate limit apply at drain time. [G2 (i)] A drain by a process that does not hold the actor's session (an owner write, another agent's hook) records the entry as a CONNECTOR refusal ((d) shape); the original attempt time goes to the side log behind `connector.detailDigest`, never into `request.claimedAt` (which is part of the re-derived request). [A-68] This holds for every drain, whoever performs it: `request.claimedAt` is the re-derived request's value, and the original attempt time goes to the writer's side log (behind `connector.detailDigest` for the CONNECTOR shape; the spool log, surfaced by `inspect`, for a drain by the actor's own session). The draft clause keeping the original time in `request.claimedAt` is withdrawn.
   (c) *Owner signer* [A-38.3]. Restates A-24.3: refusal and effect appends by hooks and the gateway are non-owner writes with no attestation and no witness statement; an approval-prompting owner signer (hardware touch, agent confirm) is never invoked by an append the agent triggered.
   (d) *Actors without a current session* [A-38.4] (fenced, terminated, expired or never admitted): the hook or gateway records a CONNECTOR refusal through its own owner-admitted session (`connector.connectorId` = its agent id, `connector.refusalCode` = the CORE or BUDGET code it derived, `request.actorId` = the actor as mapped, `detailDigest` per A-42, item 10). If the hook itself has no session, nothing is recorded, as in rc.1.
   (e) *SECURITY* [A-38.5]: the sentence of A-36.3 (§10.1), plus "a hook that cannot record in time still denies; recording is best-effort and bounded".
   (f) *Hook README* [A-38.6] states the host timeout assumption (rc.1: 30 s `PreToolUse`) and that `recordBudgetMs` plus resume MUST fit well inside it.
   The reasons `SPOOLED`, `RECORD_DEADLINE`, `NOT_REPRODUCED_AT_DRAIN` extend A-11's list, which remains the one enumeration (item 7) [G2 (ii)].
10. **Resources.** [A-42, as drafted] (1) Hooks and the gateway record a CORE or BUDGET refusal (re-derivable) only for rules whose resource template uses **no** tool-input variable. (2) For a rule whose template renders tool input, they record a CONNECTOR refusal (A-12.1, A-38.4 shape): `request.resource` = the rule's template text as configured (≤ 128 characters, unrendered; Unicode scalar values, A-89.2: a JavaScript implementation measures with code-point iteration, `[...s].length`, not `.length` [A-170.3]), `connector.refusalCode` = the CORE or BUDGET code derived on the rendered request, `connector.detailDigest` = `sha256` of the canonical rendered request; the rendered request itself goes only to a local, deletable side log (same directory as the A-38 spool), never into the history. (3) `init` and `hooks validate` warn for every rule that renders tool input into the resource. (4) SECURITY: "Refusal records are permanent and replicated: never map secrets or free text into `request.resource`; a rule that renders tool input is recorded by digest only."
11. **Non-owner appends.** [A-24.3] [A-56.5] Refusals, effects and counterparty wrappers recorded by hooks and the gateway are appended as non-owner writes: no new attestation, no witness statement; they neither count toward nor are refused by `maxUnwitnessedEvents`; the next owner write or checkpoint attests them. Non-control appends through the owner API are refused with `WITNESS_UNAVAILABLE {reason: MAX_UNWITNESSED}` from `maxUnwitnessedEvents − 16` on, so control events keep room [A-24.2].
12. **Read-only histories.** [A-41, as drafted] Enforcement callers (hooks, the gateway, `run()`, consequential `authorize`, the execution API and every other caller that acts on a decision) refuse to open a fenced, legacy or format-1 history; a hook treats the refusal as an error (deny unless `failOpen`); the opt-in `enforceFromReadOnlyHistory: true` applies to never-migrated files only. Full rule: §16.1. The CLI prints the one-line config change (G2).

### 10.2 `EFFECT_RECORDED`
```
data = { effectId: Identifier, intentId, measurer: {kind: "SESSION" | "CONNECTOR" | "COUNTERPARTY", id: Identifier}, metric: Identifier, unit: Identifier,
         amount: Amount, observedAt: U53, detailDigest: ContentHash | null, runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch, effectAuthorization: {hash, signature} }
```
Challenge (`irontrellis-effect-authorization/2`) [A-54: `eventId`, `eventType`, `timestamp` added]: `{version, domain, eventId, eventType: "EFFECT_RECORDED", timestamp, effectId, intentId, measurer, metric, unit, amount, observedAt, detailDigest, eventHistoryHash: <head before>.hash, eventHistoryPosition, runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch}`; a writer fixes the final envelope before signing.

Checks, in order: schema; session authorization (§6.2, fence first; `EFFECT_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`; `rejects-effect-with-tampered-amount`); **measurer binding** (`TRANSITION_INVALID`): for `SESSION` and `CONNECTOR`, `measurer.id` MUST equal the signing session's agent (a connector signs with its own session, as in §10.1); for `COUNTERPARTY`, `measurer.id` MUST be an ACTIVE counterparty (§11.1) — `rejects-effect-measurer-mismatch`; **envelope binding** for `COUNTERPARTY` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`) [A-36.1, as drafted, modified by G2]: `detailDigest` MUST be non-null and equal the `envelopeDigest` (§11.2) of an accepted `COUNTERPARTY_RECEIPT_RECORDED` at an earlier position with the same `intentId` and `envelope.counterpartyId = measurer.id`; `amount` MUST equal that envelope's `amount`; **actor binding** for `SESSION` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`) [A-37.1, as drafted]: the signing session's agent MUST equal the intent's `actorId` — CONNECTOR effects keep the measurer binding above (the signing session's own agent) and may concern any admitted intent; COUNTERPARTY effects follow the envelope binding; the intent MUST be admitted (§7.2 status set; `TRANSITION_INVALID`; `rejects-effect-for-unknown-intent`); `observedAt ≤ timestamp` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); at most 64 effects per intent (`EFFECT_LIMIT`; unchanged by A-37, which rejects a per-measurer 64/256 limit); `effectId` unique (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); capacity. State: an `effect` record. Effects are evidence; they never change budgets or authority usage.

**Envelope index.** [A-36.1, as drafted] The lookup index (envelope digests by `(intentId, counterpartyId)`) is derived from `event` records of type 40 (§12.2 row 40 puts `counterpartyId` in `actorId` and `intentId` in `subjectId`) by reading the referenced events from the file, so it is rebuildable under the §7.1 invariant and adds no state record.

**Metric.** [A-36, G2 modification] The §11.2 envelope carries no metric. There is no metric rule for any measurer kind: SESSION and CONNECTOR effects may report any metric, and `verify` lists effects whose metric differs from the intent's.

**Generated ids (API rule).** [A-37.2, as drafted] When the caller does not supply `intentId`, `effectId` or `refusalId`, the API derives it as `hashCanonical([<kind literal>, actorId, operationId])` where `operationId` is the caller's operation id (rc.1 `run()` behaviour) or, absent one, a fresh 128-bit random value; a caller-supplied raw id stays allowed and the caller owns its collision risk. Derived ids keep `run()` idempotent and are not predictable across actors. [A-69] `<kind literal>` is `"irontrellis-intent-id/2"`, `"irontrellis-effect-id/2"` or `"irontrellis-refusal-id/2"`, and `operationId` is a string; the TypeScript engine and the Python port derive the same ids.

**SECURITY.** [A-37.3, as drafted] "`intentId`, `effectId`, `refusalId` and `nonce` are one namespace shared by every actor; a predictable id can be taken first by another admitted session, which then fails the owner's operation with `TRANSITION_INVALID`."

**Builder tests.** [A-37.4, as drafted] A SESSION effect by another agent refused; a CONNECTOR effect by the gateway on another agent's intent accepted; two actors with the same operation id get different derived intent ids. No vector or pinned root changes: the pinned connector refusal names `spender`, an existing agent; the only pinned effect is SESSION-measured and recorded by the intent's own actor [A-36, A-37 vector statements, as drafted].

### 10.3 `RECONCILIATION_OBSERVED` (type 42) [A-71]
A reconciler (an admitted session) records one signed claim about one provider-stream event: whether it matches a decision in this history. Native, additive, format 2 only (A-71.0; code 41 unassigned). Only `source.eventDigest` of host content enters the history.
```
data = { observationId: Identifier, reconcilerAgentId: Identifier,
         source: {streamId: Identifier, eventRef: Identifier, eventDigest: ContentHash},
         call:   {actorId: Identifier, action?: Identifier, resource?: string (≤ 128 Unicode scalar values [A-170.3]), observedAt?: U53, amount?: Amount, unsupplied: [Identifier]},
         match:  {result: "MATCHED" | "UNMATCHED", position?: U53},
         expectedHistoryHead: Head, runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch, observationAuthorization: {hash: ContentHash, signature} }
```
Schema (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`): `call.unsupplied` is a byte-ordered, duplicate-free subset of `["action", "observedAt", "resource"]`; each of `call.action`, `call.observedAt`, `call.resource` is absent iff named in `unsupplied`; `call.amount?` is freely optional; `match.position` is present iff `result = "MATCHED"`; no `controlAuthorization`. `source.eventDigest` is the adapter's `sha256` over the provider event as retrieved, in a serialization the adapter documents and keeps stable per `streamId`; replay never recomputes it. [A-71.1] The `≤ 128` bound on `call.resource` counts Unicode scalar values (code points), measured in JavaScript by code-point iteration (`[...s].length`), not `.length` [A-89.2] [A-170.3].
