A missing `observationAuthorization`, a `hash` that is not a `ContentHash`, or a `signature` that is not a §3.3 `Signature` is a schema failure, `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`; a well-formed signature that does not verify, or a `hash` unequal to the challenge hash, is `OBSERVATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (A-71.9's "unsigned" is the former, "forged" the latter, as every §3.3 scalar is treated) [A-89.3]. `call.observedAt` has no bound: it is the provider time as the adapter reports it (possibly from a migrated ledger, A-71.6), not a measurement the writer makes, and the event `timestamp` binds the recording time; `observedAt ≤ timestamp` applies to effects and receipt envelopes, not to type 42 [A-89.4].

Challenge (`irontrellis-reconciliation-authorization/2`, A-54 form; `hash = hashCanonical(challenge)`; a writer fixes the final envelope before signing): `{version, domain, eventId, eventType: "RECONCILIATION_OBSERVED", timestamp, observationId, reconcilerAgentId, source, call, match, eventHistoryHash: expectedHistoryHead.hash, eventHistoryPosition, runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch}`; `source`, `call`, `match` exactly as written in `data`. [A-71.2]

Checks, in order [A-71.3]: schema; session authorization (§6.2, fence first `SESSION_FENCED`; every other §6.2 failure, `hash` and signature `OBSERVATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`); **reconciler binding**: `reconcilerAgentId` = the signing session's agent (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); `expectedHistoryHead` = the head immediately before (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); **match binding** (`MATCHED` only): `match.position < p`, the accepted event there is a `TRANSACTION_INTENT_ADMITTED` or `REQUEST_REFUSED` whose §12.2 actor equals `call.actorId` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`; nothing else of it is compared at replay); **duplicate / equivocation** [A-86.1]: let `D` be the set of accepted observations with the same `(source.streamId, source.eventRef)` (index derived from `observation` records, rebuildable; one index for the whole history, across reconcilers [A-89.5]). If any member of `D` has `source.eventDigest` equal to this event's → `OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE`. Otherwise, if `D` is non-empty, the event is accepted with `equivocates = O.position`, `O` the earliest member of `D`; if `D` is empty, `equivocates = null`. A finding is not a verdict: a provider that rewrites its log must not brick the history, and the equivocation is what `verify` reports. A second reconciler's identical report is therefore `OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE` and a different digest an equivocation; corroboration of one provider event by independent reconcilers is not a format-2 feature (E-18) [A-89.5]; `observationId` unique (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); capacity steps 1–4 (non-control, `RESERVE_CLASS`, growth `ΔS = +1`). State: an `observation` record and the `reconciliation-cursor` of `(reconcilerAgentId, streamId)` (§7.2). Projection row 42: `(reconcilerAgentId, call.actorId, call.amount or —)`, bit 1 set, bit 2 iff `call.amount` present [A-71.5]. Limitation: an `UNMATCHED` observation cannot be re-observed with the same digest after the decision lands later; the reconciler observes a stream event only once the local history is at or past the decision it expects (writer rule, A-71.6).

**Writer contract (not replay) [A-71.6].** `recordObservation(handle, input)` on a runtime handle whose session's agent is `reconcilerAgentId` (else `TRANSITION_REJECTED`). Under the writer lock at the head appended to: look up the match in the current state, set `expectedHistoryHead`, sign, durably append, then advance the reconciler's own checkpoint (the adapter's cursor file; the `reconciliation-cursor` record is derived by replay). A crash between append and checkpoint advance re-emits; the pre-check or replay answers `OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE`, which the writer treats as recorded-and-advance. [A-86.2] The derived `observationId` is a function of `(reconcilerAgentId, streamId, eventRef, eventDigest)`, so every crash re-emission by the same reconciler, equivocations included, is caught at the duplicate step; the `observationId` uniqueness step is reached only by a *supplied* `observationId` that collides. One result, one meaning: `OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE` from the pre-check or from replay means "an observation with this `(streamId, eventRef, eventDigest)` is accepted; advance the checkpoint". The API result MAY carry the accepted record's `position` as an informative field; it is not a code. `observationId` absent → `hashCanonical(["irontrellis-observation-id/2", reconcilerAgentId, streamId, eventRef, eventDigest])`. Non-owner append (A-24.3): no attestation, no witness statement, outside `maxUnwitnessedEvents`. No coalescing; `maxStateRecords` bounds observations. The adapter ledger (Astra C1 interim) is retired at rc.2; each entry is recorded as a new observation at the then-head, never back-dated, and the ledger's original timestamps go, if anywhere, into `call.observedAt` [A-90.2].

**Listings (`verify`/`inspect`) [A-71.7].** Equivocations grouped by `(streamId, eventRef)`; `MATCHED` observations whose present `call.action`/`resource`/`amount` differ from the referenced request; self-observations (`reconcilerAgentId = call.actorId`). SECURITY: "An observation is one admitted session's signed claim about one provider event. It proves neither that the provider supplied every event nor that any external effect occurred; an equivocation listing shows that a story changed, not which version is true. CONNECTOR refusals and observations share the limitation of A-36: the named agent says so." [A-90.1]

**Tests [A-71.9].** Vector group `conformance/v2/observation/` (after E5; one pinned root `accepts-observation-history`): accepts matched-admission, matched-refusal, unmatched, equivocation; rejects duplicate, matched-wrong-actor, matched-later-position, matched-non-decision-type, stale-head, tampered-digest, fenced-session, owner-signed, at-`maxEvents`. [A-86.4] `rejects-duplicate-after-equivocation` (O1 digest A at `p`; O2 digest B accepted at `p+1` with `equivocates = p`; O2 re-emitted at `p+2` with a fresh envelope, fresh `timestamp` and the same derived id → `OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE` at `p+2`) and `accepts-second-equivocation` (digest C after A and B → accepted, `equivocates = p`); the checker default is `obs_dup=any`. The group and the root landed after E5 (E-17 closed, A-171.3). Builder/Astra (C1): unsigned, forged, cross-stream, crash between append and checkpoint advance, reconciler ≠ handle session.

## 11. Counterparty receipts (second layer)

### 11.1 Registration (◆)
`COUNTERPARTY_REGISTERED.data = {counterpartyId, keys: [{keyId, scheme: "ed25519", credential: PublicKey32}]{1..16}}` (distinct keyIds; each key subject to the §3.3 declaration test, `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` [A-32.2]; key-role writer rules and `allowKeyRebind`: §6.3 [A-32.3]; re-registration of an ACTIVE id is `TRANSITION_INVALID`, vector `rejects-reregistering-active-counterparty`; registering a RETIRED id again is allowed, replaces its keys and **keeps** `lastReceiptDigest` and `receiptCount`, so the receipt chain continues). `COUNTERPARTY_RETIRED.data = {counterpartyId}` (an unknown or already RETIRED id is `TRANSITION_INVALID`).

### 11.2 `COUNTERPARTY_RECEIPT_RECORDED` (◇ wrapper around a counterparty-signed envelope)
```
envelope = { version: "irontrellis-counterparty-receipt/2", historyId: ContentHash (= recordHash(0) of this history), counterpartyId, keyId,
             logicalId: Identifier (= intentId), intentDigest: ContentHash (= the intent's declarationAuthorization.hash),
             payloadDigest: ContentHash, amount: Amount, outcome: "DELIVERED" | "REFUSED" | "UNKNOWN", observedAt: U53,
             previousReceiptDigest: ContentHash | null, signature: Signature }
data = { intentId, envelope, runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch, recordAuthorization: {hash, signature} }
```
`envelopeSigningHash = hashCanonical(envelope without signature)`; `envelopeDigest = hashCanonical(envelope)` (with signature). Wrapper challenge (`irontrellis-counterparty-record-authorization/2`) [A-54: `eventId`, `eventType`, `timestamp` added]: `{version, domain, eventId, eventType: "COUNTERPARTY_RECEIPT_RECORDED", timestamp, intentId, envelopeDigest, eventHistoryHash: <head before>.hash, eventHistoryPosition, runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch}`; a writer fixes the final envelope before signing. (The counterparty-signed envelope is not changed by A-54.)

Checks, in order: schema; the counterparty is ACTIVE and has `keyId` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`, `rejects-receipt-from-unregistered-counterparty`); the envelope signature verifies under that key over `envelopeSigningHash` under the §6.3 profile (`COUNTERPARTY_SIGNATURE_INVALID`; `rejects-receipt-with-bad-counterparty-signature`, `rejects-receipt-with-altered-envelope`); the wrapper session authorization (§6.2, fence first; `RECORD_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `rejects-receipt-wrapper-stale`); `envelope.historyId = recordHash(0)` of this history (`TRANSITION_INVALID`, `rejects-receipt-for-other-history`); `envelope.logicalId = intentId`, the intent is admitted (§7.2) and `envelope.intentDigest = intent.declarationDigest` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`, `rejects-receipt-intent-digest-mismatch`); `envelope.observedAt ≤ timestamp` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`) [A-45.1, as drafted]; `previousReceiptDigest` equals the counterparty's `lastReceiptDigest` (`TRANSITION_INVALID`, `rejects-receipt-chain-break`); capacity. State: the counterparty's `lastReceiptDigest`/`receiptCount` move. `amount`, `payloadDigest` and `outcome` are **attested by the counterparty only**: Core records them and binds them to the intent, it does not compare `amount` with the admitted amount (an auditor can, since `intentDigest` binds the declaration; a COUNTERPARTY effect of §10.2 binds to this envelope by `envelopeDigest` [A-36]). The issuer-side `RECEIPT_RECORDED` is session-signed per §8.8 and independent; the two layers are joined by `intentId`.

**Writer rule: `observedAt`.** [A-45.2, as drafted] A writer MUST NOT advance its own `timestamp` to satisfy the `observedAt` check (that would re-open S-3 / A-22); an envelope whose `observedAt` is ahead of the writer's time is refused at the API with `INVALID_INPUT {reason: OBSERVED_AT_AHEAD}` and the counterparty re-issues.

**Receipt chain.** [A-45.3, as drafted] "The receipt chain is one sequence per counterparty and history. A counterparty signs sequentially and, after a lost or refused envelope, re-issues from the recorded `lastReceiptDigest`; two envelopes signed in parallel against the same `previousReceiptDigest` cannot both be recorded (by design, MJ-5). Prefix intents of a migrated history have `declarationDigest: null` and cannot receive counterparty receipts."

**SDK.** [A-45.4, as drafted] `counterpartyState(counterpartyId)` → `{counterpartyId, status, lastReceiptDigest, receiptCount, keyIds}` or `null` (minor, non-format). No vector or pinned root changes (both pinned envelopes have `observedAt = timestamp = 108`).

## 12. Proof projection

### 12.1 Row
Every accepted event `e_p` has a 160-byte row:

| offset | bytes | content |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | `position` u64 BE |
| 8 | 8 | `timestamp` u64 BE |
| 16 | 2 | type code u16 BE (the § 5 table index) |
| 18 | 2 | flags u16 BE: bit 0 control event (◆ types of §5), bit 1 session-signed (◇ types, **including 14–16** and 42 [A-71.5], set per type in native and migrated histories alike [A-51.6]), bit 2 amount present; ▲ types and the genesis set neither bit 0 nor bit 1; other bits zero |
| 20 | 32 | `sha256(utf8(actorId))`, or 0^32 |
| 52 | 32 | `sha256(utf8(subjectId))`, or 0^32 |
| 84 | 32 | amount as a 32-byte big-endian unsigned integer, or 0^32 |
| 116 | 32 | `eventHash(e_p)` |
| 148 | 12 | zero |

**Bit 2.** [A-178.1] Bit 2 is set iff the §12.2-selected amount slot holds a §3.3 Amount: a present, non-null string (a present `"0"` sets the bit). An absent optional amount (types 12, 38 and 42, and the HF1-inherited optional slots of types 10 and 13), an explicit `null` at a nullable slot (types 32/33 `amountCap`), or a type with no amount slot clears the bit and zeroes bytes 84–115. Types 39 and 40 always carry an amount and always set the bit. `additionalControlAuthorizations` (§6.1) changes no row [A-95.7].

### 12.2 Field selection per type
`(actor, subject, amount)`: 0 `(—, canonicalLineageId, —)`; 1 `(—, principalId, —)`; 2 `(agentId, principalId, —)`; 3 `(—, roleId, —)`; 4 `(predecessorAgentId, ruleId, —)`; 5, 6 `(agentId, roleTenureId, —)`; 7 `(toAgentId, toRoleTenureId, —)`; 8 `(agentId, sessionId, —)`; 9 `(agentId, controllerId, —)`; 10 `(granteeId for PERMISSION else subjectActorId or —, authorityId, constraints.maxAmount or —)`; 11 `(revokerId, authorityId, —)`; 12 `(actorId, intentId, amount)`; 13 `(proof.request.actorId, intentId, proof.request.amount)`; 14, 15, 21 `(—, intentId, —)`; 16 `(issuerAgentId, intentId, —)`; 17 `(actorId, record.obligationId, —)`; 18 `(toAgentId, obligationId, —)`; 19 `(actorId, obligationId, —)`; 20 `(agentId, roleTenureId, —)`; 22 `(actorId, record.dutyId, —)`; 23 `(toAgentId, dutyId, —)`; 24, 30, 31 `(actorId, dutyId, —)`; 29 `(actorId, descriptor.dutyId, —)`; 25 `(—, anchor, —)`; 26 `(—, ownerKeys[0].keyId, —)`; 27 `(—, witnesses[0].verifierKey, —)`; 28 `(—, verifierKeys[0], —)`; 32, 33 `(—, budgetId, amountCap)`; 34 `(—, budgetId, —)`; 35 `(—, checkpointId, —)`; 36, 37 `(—, counterpartyId, —)`; 38 `(request.actorId, refusalId, request.amount)`; 39 `(measurer.id, intentId, amount)`; 40 `(envelope.counterpartyId, intentId, envelope.amount)`; 42 `(reconcilerAgentId, call.actorId, call.amount)` [A-71.5].

### 12.3 Chain
`projectionHead(−1) = 0^32`; `projectionHead(p) = sha256(utf8("irontrellis-projection/2") ‖ 0x00 ‖ projectionHead(p−1) ‖ row(p))`. [A-47.2, as drafted; errata E-1] The preimage is exactly 24 + 1 + 32 + 160 = **217 bytes**. Carried in checkpoints (§7.3), attestations (§7.4) and the export view (§15). Rows carry **identity, order and amounts** (who, what subject, how much, which event); they do not carry actions, resources, metrics, decisions or budget ids, so a budget or authority proof reads the event and the state records, while a row lets a prover check ordering and amounts without parsing JSON. Speed: **unmeasured**. The design claim is limited to this: per-event *integrity hashing* is bounded (one SHA-256 of the canonical event, one 93-byte SHA-256 for the chain, one 217-byte SHA-256 for the projection, at most two signature verifications); per-event *transition re-derivation* (authority, budgets over ≤ 16 budgets with O(used periods) rollover, state-record updates with O(log n) Merkle work for in-place changes and O(n − j + log n) for an insertion at sorted index `j` [A-4.2]) is additional and unmeasured.

## 13. Ported duty-policy machinery (founder D5 and answer 1)

Ported from Continuity `v0.3.0-preview.10.2` (the founder-staged source; commit id not recorded in the staged copy; its `packages/core-0.2/src` + `packages/core-0.3/src` tree digest is in `conformance/reference-0.3-FREEZE-LIST.txt` and the files ship in this package under `reference-0.3/`). The frozen source is the normative reference for the schemas and transition rules named below; this section lists every piece and the format-2 adaptations so the builder does not have to discover them. The left-out sweep (`LEFT-OUT-SWEEP.md` B1–B17) is the checklist.

### 13.0 Rules record
`DUTY_POLICY_RULES` (`reference-0.3/packages/core-0.2/src/core/duty-policy.ts:12-96`) is carried verbatim (its `writerProfile` field keeps the string `"continuity-segmented-local/1"` because it is inside the hashed record). [A-15] `rulesHash` is a **fixed literal**, `0xf7fb2b7de27abb77c6c3d03af6f61dce11afda5a510af20c8036085cd10a5f94`, the keccak-256 canonical hash of `DUTY_POLICY_RULES` as Continuity preview.10.2 computed it (the legacy hash algorithm). Format 2 carries it unchanged in every descriptor, finding and event and never recomputes it with SHA-256 (the SHA-256 value, `0xd6e56c55f31c51b94067c9446415587b3cc963e3a4f9fb3d20173d73dd451ce7`, MUST NOT appear). The builder MUST verify the literal once at build time with the legacy reader's keccak-256 over the canonical text of the rules record ported verbatim. Every other §13 hash — `descriptorHash`, `dutyRecordHash`, `evidenceIndexHash`, `findingHash`, `authorityProofHash`, `genesisHash` (= `recordHash(0)`) and the evidence index's `contentHash` (= `eventHash`) — is SHA-256. Limits: 4 dispositions and 2 contests per duty; criteria `SOURCE_REVIEWED, HISTORY_REVIEWED, FINDING_RECORDED, CONTROL_REVIEWED`; states `SATISFIED | UNAVAILABLE | UNRESOLVED`; reason ≤ 256 UTF-8 bytes; nextStep ≤ 512.

### 13.1 `ATTEMPT_DUTY_POLICY_ACTIVATED` ▲ (type 29)
`data = {actorId, descriptor, descriptorHash, activationAuthorityId, administrativeAuthorization}`, `descriptor = {version: "continuity-attempt-disposition/1", rulesHash, criteriaProfile: "local-investigation/1", domain, genesisHash, baseAdapterPolicyHash, dutyId, dutyCreationEventId, dutyRecordHash, sourceIntentId, sourceAdmissionEventId, durableRoleId, principalId, incidentSourceDigest: {algorithm: "sha256", value}, acceptedAttesterRoleId, dispositionLimit: 4, contestLimit: 2}`. Format-2 adaptations: `genesisHash = recordHash(0)` (the chain hash of the genesis, the format-2 counterpart of `eventHistoryHashes[0]`); `domain` is the format-2 domain; `dutyRecordHash = hashCanonical(duty.record)` with Amount strings; `administrativeAuthorization` uses `/2` literals (§8.4) and the action `ACTIVATE_DUTY_POLICY`, resource `duty-policy:<descriptorHash>`. Checks (`duty-policy.ts:131-207`, `portable-replay.ts:2074-2088`), in order: schema, including the descriptor's literal fields `version`, `rulesHash`, `criteriaProfile`, `dispositionLimit: 4`, `contestLimit: 2` and `incidentSourceDigest.algorithm` (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) [A-129.2]; the genesis adapter policy is E5 or E6 (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); the duty exists, its source intent is admitted under `REMOTE_SERVICE_REPORT_ADAPTER_ID`, `durableRoleId` is the source role and the attester role belongs to the same principal (`derivePortableDutyPolicyDescriptor`) (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); the descriptor equals the re-derived descriptor byte-for-byte and `descriptorHash` is its hash (`TRANSITION_INVALID`) [A-129.2]; no policy is active for the duty yet; the actor is the current occupant of the duty role and is not terminated; `activationAuthorityId` names an unrevoked root PERMISSION grant from the source principal to the actor with exactly `actions: ["ACTIVATE_DUTY_POLICY"]`, `resources: ["duty-policy:<descriptorHash>"]`, `maxDelegationDepth: 0`, an `expiresAt` after the event time, no quantitative constraints, and the administrative proof's recognized root and single-element permission path are that grant (`portableDutyPolicyActivationProofMatches`); the administrative authorization verifies (§8.4) [A-58.5]; every other unnamed step keeps the §5 fallback `TRANSITION_INVALID` [A-129.2]. The outer session fence (`SESSION_FENCED`) is tested **first of the type's checks**, before the first item of this list and after the envelope checks, T1 and T3 [A-58.5] [A-82] [A-172.1] [A-202.1]. State: `duty.policy` is set with `dispositionCount 0, contestCount 0`, `priorHead` = the short head before the event. Capacity: §13.5 (growth: `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY` at `maxEvents` [A-2]).

### 13.2 `ATTEMPT_DUTY_DISPOSITION_RECORDED` ▲ (30) and `ATTEMPT_DUTY_CONTEST_RECORDED` ▲ (31)
`data = {version: "continuity-attempt-disposition/1", rulesHash, dutyId, actorId, dispositionAuthorityId, finding: {challenge, runtimeSignature}, administrativeAuthorization}`. The finding challenge (`continuity-duty-finding/1`, `duty-disposition.ts:14-21`): `{version, purpose: DISPOSITION|CONTEST, domain, rulesHash, descriptorHash, activationEventId, dutyId, sourceAdmissionEventId, transitionEventId, transitionEventType, evaluationTime, priorHead, latestAssignmentEventId, previousDispositionEventId: |null, evidenceIndexHash, operation, attesterId, runtimeSessionId, credentialKeyId, controlEpoch, roleId, roleTenureId, attestationAuthorityId, dispositionAuthorityId, authorityProofHash}`; `operation` is `{disposition: COMPLETED_UNDER_POLICY|ESCALATED, checklist: {<criterion>: {state, reason}}×4, reportDigest: {algorithm, value}, nextStep: string|null}` for a disposition and `{targetDispositionEventId, reason, reportDigest}` for a contest. The **evidence index** (`continuity-duty-evidence-index/1`, `duty-disposition.ts:31-43`) is `{version, domain, dutyId, dutyCreationEventId, activationEventId, sourceAdmissionEventId, latestAssignmentEventId, recordCount, counts, records: [{eventId, eventType, contentHash}]}` over, in position order: the duty creation event, the activation event, every `TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED/ADMITTED/CONSUMED`, `TRANSACTION_OUTCOME_RECORDED`, `RECEIPT_RECORDED`, `OUTCOME_OBSERVATION_RECORDED` of the source intent, and every `ATTEMPT_DUTY_ASSIGNED`, `ATTEMPT_DUTY_REVIEW_CLOSED`, `ATTEMPT_DUTY_CONTEST_RECORDED` of the duty (dispositions excluded); `contentHash = eventHash` as `0x` hex (format-2 adaptation of `hashCanonical(event)`; the two coincide). Format-2 adaptations: `priorHead` is the §4.4 head; `runtimeSignature` is a §6.3 signature of the attester session's scheme (64 or 65 bytes), not `signature65` only; `authorityProofHash = hashCanonical` of the attester's administrative policy proof with `/2` literals; the actions are `ATTEST_DUTY_FINDING` (finding, resource `duty:<descriptorHash>`) and `RECORD_DUTY_DISPOSITION` (outer transition, same resource) — there is no `RECORD_DUTY_CONTEST` action. Checks (`duty-disposition.ts:44-97`, `portable-replay.ts:2090-2102`), in order: schema, including the `version` and `rulesHash` literals (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID`; A-15: `rulesHash` is a fixed literal and the SHA-256 value MUST NOT appear) [A-129.1]; a policy is active for the duty (`TRANSITION_INVALID`); eligibility (`operationEligible`; `TRANSITION_INVALID`): disposition — `dispositionCount < 4`, `COMPLETED_UNDER_POLICY` requires all four criteria `SATISFIED`, `contestCount = 0` and `nextStep = null`, `ESCALATED` requires a non-empty `nextStep` and (a criterion not `SATISFIED` or `contestCount > 0`); contest — `contestCount < 2` and `targetDispositionEventId` is an accepted disposition of this duty; the finding challenge equals the re-derived one byte-for-byte (attester = current occupant of `acceptedAttesterRoleId`, attester session eligible at `evaluationTime = timestamp`, `evidenceIndexHash` = hash of the index at the pre-event state, `previousDispositionEventId` = the last accepted disposition) and `runtimeSignature` verifies under the attester session (the attester session keeps this section's codes, not `SESSION_FENCED` [A-58.5]); the outer actor is the duty's current assignee and the current occupant of `durableRoleId`; the outer administrative authorization verifies under `RECORD_DUTY_DISPOSITION` (§8.4) [A-58.5]; every other unnamed step keeps the §5 fallback `TRANSITION_INVALID` [A-129.1]. The outer session fence (`SESSION_FENCED`) is tested **first of the type's checks**, before the first item of this list and after the envelope checks, T1 and T3; the attester session keeps this section's codes [A-58.5] [A-82] [A-172.1] [A-202.1]. State: `duty.policy.dispositionCount`/`contestCount` +1, `duty.dispositions` gains `{eventId, position, type, findingHash}`.

### 13.3 Duty view (`continuity-attempt-duty-view/1`)
`inspectDutyPolicy(state, dutyId)` → `null` when no policy, else `{version, dutyId, dutyDisposition: OPEN|COMPLETED_UNDER_POLICY|ESCALATED|NEEDS_REVIEW|CONTESTED, outstanding, externalOutcome: "NOT_PROVEN", policy: <the duty.policy record in the frozen activation shape: the §7.2 `duty.policy` fields with `position` written as `eventPosition` and `priorHead` written as the full §4.4 head before the activation (`{hash, position, canonicalTime}`, `canonicalTime = e_{position−1}.timestamp`, read from the history; the state record keeps the short head); every other field as §7.2 [A-128.1]>, observedHead: <head>, currentAssigneeId, latestAssignmentEventId, lastRecordedDisposition: null | {eventId, eventPosition, timestamp, disposition, findingHash, evidenceIndexHash, reportDigest}, evidenceScope: {kind: "COMPLETE_CAPTURED_HISTORY", head}, reasons: [one of NO_DISPOSITION_RECORDED, ACCEPTED_CONTEST, RELEVANT_EVIDENCE_CHANGED, ATTENTION_REQUESTED, ALL_LOCAL_CRITERIA_SATISFIED]}` exactly as `duty-policy.ts:209-217` and `duty-disposition.ts:120-129`: no disposition → `OPEN` (or `CONTESTED` if a contest exists), `outstanding: true`; else `CONTESTED` if `contestCount > 0`, else `NEEDS_REVIEW` if the current evidence-index hash differs from the last finding's `evidenceIndexHash`, else the last disposition; `outstanding = dutyDisposition ≠ COMPLETED_UNDER_POLICY`. Adapter op `duty-view` (§17).

### 13.4 Writer contract (not a replay rule)
The two-phase finding producer (`administration/index.ts:397-413`): `prepareDutyFinding(state, input)` → `{challenge, signingHash}` after checking both authorities with a placeholder signature; `attachDutyFinding(state, input, runtimeSignature)` re-derives and verifies the finding, then returns the outer administrative input; the outer signature follows (`prepareAdministrativeEvent` / `attachAdministrativeSignature`). **Fresh eligibility** (`validatePortableDutyFreshEligibility`, `duty-disposition.ts:106-119`) MUST be re-run by the writer after each signature returns and again under the writer lock immediately before the append, at a fresh time ≥ the signed time with the head unchanged, for both the attester session and the outer session, requiring the authority routes (recognized root, permission path, intersections) of both proofs to be unchanged; the signed challenges are never altered. The one-signature route refuses findings with `DUTY_WRITER_REQUIRED`.

[A-8] The local API keeps the frozen preview.10.2 shapes: `openLocalDutyPolicy(options, findingSigner?)` with `findingSigner = {session, signHash}` (default: the runtime session). `describe({duty, incidentSourceDigest: {algorithm: "sha256", value}, attesterRole})` → `{descriptor, descriptorHash, activationAction: "ACTIVATE_DUTY_POLICY", activationResource: "duty-policy:<descriptorHash>", head, grantsAuthority: false}` (read-only). `activate({id, descriptor, activationAuthority})`: event id `duty-policy:<id>`; returns `{eventId, head, alreadyRecorded, view}`; an identical repeat → `alreadyRecorded: true`; different content under the same id → `OPERATION_CONFLICT` (the comparison ignores signature bytes, below [A-48]). `prepareDisposition`/`prepareContest` are the two-phase producers above; `dispose`/`contest` use ids `duty-disposition:<id>`/`duty-contest:<id>` with the same idempotency and result shape. The actor is always the handle session's agent (no `actorId` input). `inspect(duty)` on the handle returns the frozen wrapper `{scope: "CAPTURED_HISTORY_ONLY", executionCapability: false, head, policy: <§13.3 view> | null}` and `TRANSITION_REJECTED` for an unknown duty; the state API `inspectDutyPolicyState` and the adapter op `duty-view` return the §13.3 view or `null`. Codes: `DUTY_WRITER_REQUIRED`, `OPERATION_CONFLICT`, `TRANSITION_REJECTED`, capacity codes per §5.2.

**Idempotent re-recording ignores signature bytes.** [A-48.1, as drafted] For every API path that returns `alreadyRecorded` (duty activation, disposition, contest, and any other idempotent re-record), "identical" compares the event's id, type and `data` **without** its signature fields (`controlAuthorization.signature`, `administrativeAuthorization` signature bytes, finding signatures, `recordAuthorization.signature`) — equivalently, the challenge or `effectHash` content; a repeat whose only difference is a fresh signature from a non-deterministic signer (KMS ECDSA under EIP-191, FROST) returns `alreadyRecorded: true` and writes nothing. FROST nonce safety: §6.4 [A-48.2]. Builder test [A-48.3, as drafted]: a duty activation repeated with a different valid signature over the same content returns `alreadyRecorded`.

### 13.5 Capacity
At activation the capacity policy reserves `4 + 2` slots for the duty's dispositions and contests (`dutyReserved`, `core-0.3/src/capacity.ts:22-42`), counted with `lifecycleReserved` in `S(p)` (§5.2.1); the reservation is never released on completion. A disposition or contest beyond the quota is refused by eligibility (§13.2) before capacity. An activation at `maxEvents` is `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY`; dispositions and contests within the quota are admitted [A-10.4].

### 13.6 Queries and inspections
When at least one duty has a policy: RESPONSIBLE answers gain `attemptDuties: [{dutyId, sourceIntentId, sourceAdmissionEventId, originalActorId, durableRoleId, creationActorId, initialAssigneeId, currentAssigneeId, deadline, currentView, externalOutcome: "NOT_PROVEN"}]` sorted by `dutyId` with the evidence references of `portable-query-responsibility.ts:115-128`; SURVIVES duty projections carry `currentView` in place of `status` (`portable-query-survives.ts:158-169`); the query scope carries `recognizedExtensions: [{version: "continuity-attempt-disposition/1", rulesHash}]` (`portable-queries.ts:50-52`); the public disclosure includes the duty field paths only when the extension is active (`PORTABLE_DUTY_QUERY_FIELD_PATHS`, `portable-query-codec.ts:285-306`); `inspectAttemptHistory` returns `duty.disposition = <view>` for duties with a policy. Unextended histories keep their rc.1-final answers byte-for-byte.

### 13.7 Oracle and tests
`packages/reference-0.3` (BUILD §4) is the frozen preview.10.2 engine; the builder generates the duty-policy vectors from the ported engine and checks them differentially against it on legacy-format twins (T5), ports the 50 oracle test cases (T5) and the eight-step investigation walkthrough as a test (T7, LEFT-OUT-SWEEP B16).

**Continuation history profile and history store:** both are *demonstrated equivalents* under the founder's rule (LEFT-OUT-SWEEP §C and §D, tests T1–T4), with two pieces ported: the migration fence and resumable migration (§16.2).

## 14. Performance budgets (acceptance criteria; see BUILD-INSTRUCTIONS §6)

| path | budget | status |
|---|---|---|
| append (owner write incl. two signatures, pin, snapshot update; the root recomputed per append as part of the pin attestation [A-4.3]) | ≤ 5 ms median at 10,000 events, excluding signer latency | measured 36.6 ms median at 10k events by the builder; miss deferred to E5 (aligned-subtree cache), KNOWN-ISSUES if still missed [A-4.3, rulings] |
| hook per-call check in `CHECKPOINT_RESUME` incl. the §7.5 root recomputation (about 2·recordCount SHA-256) and the `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` scan [A-56.19] | ≤ 20 ms median at 10,000 events (≈ 12,000 records) with ≤ 256 events after the snapshot; never a full replay when a valid snapshot exists; the recomputation is never skipped | unmeasured target |
| full replay | ≤ 0.30 ms per event (signature-dominated; rc.1-final measures 0.22) | measured baseline, target unchanged |
| state root from scratch | ≤ 5 ms at 12,000 records; incremental update `O(log n)` per **in-place** changed record (insertions fall under the append budget) [A-4.2] [A-56.6] | unmeasured target |
| checkpoint emission | one owner signature + one state root | — |

A missed budget is reported in `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`; it is never met by weakening validation. A writer MUST NOT omit, defer or approximate the root of any attestation or checkpoint to meet a budget [A-4.3].

**Reported benchmarks (not acceptance budgets).** [A-44.3, as drafted] BUILD §6 adds two reported measurements: "rollover evaluation after 100,000 used periods, with and without the index" (§9.5) and "hook per-call check at 10^6 records (test-only cap override)". §14's budgets are unchanged; a miss or a bad number goes to KNOWN-ISSUES (A-4.3), never to weakened validation.

**OPERATOR.** [A-44.4, as drafted] OPERATOR states: per-call resume cost grows linearly with the record count; refusal floods are bounded by A-23; a corrupted snapshot costs one full replay and a rebuild per corruption (an attacker with write access to the history directory is the out-of-scope administrator of SECURITY).

## 15. Export view (`irontrellis-export/2`)
`exportView(store, {fromPosition, toPosition, include: {fromRecords?: boolean, toRecords?: boolean, inclusion?: [{kind, key}], keyEvents?: boolean, attestations?: boolean, referencedEvents?: boolean}})` [A-27.3] [A-53.2] returns a closed record; additive top-level fields (`fromAuthenticated`, `historyId`) are minor while the §17 comparison list is unchanged [A-56.20]. [A-65.1] This is the one wire shape: `include` carries boolean selectors (`inclusion` a selector list); evidence is returned top-level, each field present iff selected and never `null`; every position-keyed array is sorted ascending by position without duplicates; a verifier rejects a duplicate position, an unsorted array, an explicit `null` evidence field or an unknown key with `INVALID_INPUT`; `fromAuthenticated` is independent of transition success:
```
{ version: "irontrellis-export/2", domain, genesisEventHash: ContentHash (= eventHash(e₀)), historyId: ContentHash (= recordHash(0)) [A-47.1],
  from: {position, head: Head, stateRoot: rootAt(from), projectionHead, recordCount},
  to:   {position, head, stateRoot: rootAt(to), projectionHead, recordCount},
  eventCount: to − from + 1, checkpointIds: [Identifier] (STATE_CHECKPOINT events within from..to, in order),
  events: [{position, event, eventHash, recordHash, projectionRow: "0x"+320 hex}] (positions from..to inclusive),
  checkpoints: [STATE_CHECKPOINT data within the range],
  fromRecords?: [{kind, key, record}] (the full record set at `from`, when include.fromRecords),
  toRecords?:   [{kind, key, record}] (the full record set at `to`, when include.toRecords),
  inclusion?: [{at: "to", kind, key, leafIndex, path: [ContentHash]}] (audit paths against to.stateRoot for the requested records),
  keyEvents?: [{position, event, headBefore: short head}] (every OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED event at a position ≤ to, when include.keyEvents) [A-27.3],
  attestations?: {from: HeadAttestation|null, to: HeadAttestation|null, fromCheckpoint: {position: from+1, event, eventHash, recordHash}|null} (the owner attestations held for from.head and to.head; fromCheckpoint carries the STATE_CHECKPOINT at from+1 when it lies outside the range, chained onto from.head by its previousHash; when include.attestations) [A-27.3] [A-65.2],
  referencedEvents?: [{position, event}] (every event at a position < from whose body a transition or view in the range may read, when include.referencedEvents) [A-53.2],
  fromAuthenticated: boolean [A-27.3] }
```

**What the view states.** [A-27.1] The view states a transition between two roots. Its starting state is authenticated only when `from.position = 0`, or by an owner-signed root at `from` (a `STATE_CHECKPOINT` at `from + 1`, or a `/2` head attestation of `from.head`) verified under owner keys **and policies** established from the genesis through every `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` event at positions ≤ `to` — every `keyEvents` entry and the authenticating checkpoint at `from + 1` verified under the §6.5.3 quorum with the pre-event policy derived from the genesis and the preceding key events (key events through `acceptSF`, §7.4 [A-120.1]); an `attestations.from` `/2` attestation authenticating `from` is an asserted root and MUST satisfy §7.4's resume-root rule with "file head" read as the view's last epoch (its append epoch [A-120.0]), including `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT) = 1` under that policy [A-101.1] [A-111.1]; full replay is never substituted by an asserted root that an unqualified key signed [A-93.4] —; the epoch used is the one in force at the signed position (`from + 1` for a checkpoint, `from.head.position` for an attestation), with the key events bound to the chain through `fromRecords` or `include.keyEvents` [A-58.10]. The policy in force at `from` is read from `fromRecords`' `ownerKeyEpoch` records or re-derived from the genesis and `keyEvents`; nothing is added to the view [A-95.5] [A-91.4]. A view without an authenticated `from` carries `fromAuthenticated: false`. [A-75.2] `fromAuthenticated` is always present; an exporter sets `true` only when the view itself carries the evidence (`from = 0`, or `attestations` selected and `fromCheckpoint` or `attestations.from` present and verified under the bound key events and, for `attestations.from`, the resume rule above; a held attestation that is not verified never sets it [A-185.4]); an identity range without `attestations` is `false`. [A-75.3] The shape carries no `B(from)` and no checkpoint after `to`: a verifier starting at `from > 0` applies the record-count capacity tests from `from.recordCount` and reports the byte tests as not judged (report-level); `to` is owner-authenticated through `attestations.to`, which an exporter obtains by attesting at `to`; `from.fileBytes` and `attestations.toCheckpoint` are E-16. Exporters SHOULD choose `from` and `to` at `c − 1` of a checkpoint or emit one. The prover proves `R_from → R_to`; the relying party anchors `R_from`; the ZK index gains no new identity [A-27.4].

**Verification.** [A-53.1] [A-27.2] `events` is inclusive (`from … to`, `eventCount = to − from + 1`, disclosure only). The verifier rebuilds the state at `from` from `fromRecords` (§7.1 invariant) and checks its root equals `from.stateRoot`; checks that the `event` records of `fromRecords` re-chain from `genesisEventHash` to `from.head.hash`; checks the event at `from` against the already-applied `event` record and `from.head` as boundary evidence; then applies exactly positions `from + 1 … to` with full validation and checks `to.stateRoot` and `to.projectionHead`. `from == to` is an identity transition; for `from = 0` the genesis is applied once. Invalid ranges and missing boundary records are `INVALID_INPUT`. **Referenced events:** `referencedEvents` carries `[{position, event}]` for every event at a position `< from` [A-75.1] whose body a §7.2 transition or §13.2 view in the range may read (admission, consumption, outcome, receipt and finding bodies referenced by position from `fromRecords`). Each MUST hash to the authenticated `event` record at its position and chain correctly; they are evidence, never applied. A verifier lacking a needed one returns `EXPORT_DEPENDENCIES_MISSING` and MUST NOT claim transition validity. [A-65.3] The closure is every body at a position `< from` that the effective text (SPEC plus every addendum) reads while applying `from + 1 … to` or evaluating the §13.2 view: the lifecycle references from `fromRecords` (the admission bodies among them are what `authorityUsage` is re-derived from, as the §7.2 `usage` row states; a `from > 0` verifier therefore needs nothing new under `referencedEvents` for it, and the `from = 0` ZK profile reads every body anyway [A-148.5]), the type-40 body cited by a COUNTERPARTY effect's `detailDigest` (A-36.1), when `from + 1` is inside a migrated prefix, every prefix event at positions `1 … from − 1`, which together with the boundary event in `events` supply the source prefix bodies `1 … from` (the shadow head is a sequential chain over the un-transcribed events), and, under an `ATTESTED` epoch, the body `e_{q−1}` of every `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` at `q ≤ from`, so that a conforming exporter supplies it [A-131.1]. [A-75.1] `referencedEvents` is strictly `< from`: the boundary body is carried once, in `events`, and a verifier refuses an entry at a position `≥ from` with `INVALID_INPUT`. A verifier never infers a body from an uncommitted index; exporters MAY include a disclosed superset, and every supplied body is validated against its `event` record. [A-65.4] For `from = 0`, a verifier given `fromRecords` starts from the rebuilt post-genesis state and applies `1 … to`; without them it applies the genesis from `events[0]` once and then `1 … to`; both are conforming. With the per-intent receipt bound (§8.8) no per-session lookup is needed. [A-53.2]

**T2 on the key-event path; what the verifier list is.** [A-131.1] [A-93.5] [A-148.2] The export key-event verifier evaluates T2 (§6.5.6) for a key event at `q` only when `e_{q−1}` is available to it: in `events` (`q − 1 ≥ from`) or in `referencedEvents`; otherwise it **omits T2 and reports it as not judged** (report-level, as the byte tests above), never `EXPORT_DEPENDENCIES_MISSING`. `keyEvents[].headBefore` stays a short head: A-65.1 is a closed shape, and a new key inside it would be refused by every existing verifier with `INVALID_INPUT`, so a full head there is E-30 (format minor); the witness and resume paths hold a full head and apply T2 in full; full replay is the authority [A-131.1]. The human-attestation evidence bundle is not an export dependency: `EXPORT_DEPENDENCIES_MISSING` never names it (§6.5.5) [A-93.5]. The normative content of the export verifier is the Verification paragraph above (rebuild from `fromRecords`, re-chain, apply `from + 1 … to`, check `to.stateRoot` and `to.projectionHead`) together with the A-65.1 shape refusals (a duplicate or unsorted position, an explicit `null` evidence field or an unknown key → `INVALID_INPUT`) and the §17 comparison list; `eventCount`, `checkpointIds` and `checkpoints` are disclosure fields. A ZK profile MAY refuse a disclosure field that disagrees with the applied range as a rule of that profile (`SPEC-IRONTRELLIS-ZK-ABI-v2-r1.md`); that is not an obligation on every verifier [A-148.2].

[A-43, G2 modification] This is the only interface a prover or an auditor consumes; the gateway process may read the view for its own verification and never exposes it as a tool; witnesses consume `/1` head statements (§7.4). Nothing in them pins engine internals. Vector `export/v2/cp-1-to-cp-3` pins both ends (regenerated at E5, P.3); adapter op `export` (§17). Regenerated suite: round-trips for `from == to`, `from = 0`, the cp-1/cp-3 range, and a duty range whose latest finding precedes `from` [A-53.3].

**`historyId`.** [A-47.1, as drafted] The export view record gains `historyId: ContentHash (= recordHash(0))` beside `genesisEventHash`. Additive; the adapter runner compares only `version, domain, genesisEventHash, from, to, eventCount, checkpointIds` (§17), so the export vector is unchanged; the Python port carries the field.

**Capability.** [A-43.1, as drafted] `exportView` is an owner or auditor capability: runtime handles, the hook's agent-facing context and gateway tools MUST NOT expose it; the API exposes it on owner and read-only (auditor) handles only. (Runtime-handle `headroom`: §9.7, A-43.2.)

**Privacy.** [A-43.3, as drafted] SECURITY states: "An inclusion proof discloses sibling leaf hashes; a low-entropy neighbouring record (for example an adjacent `budgetUsage` period) can be recovered by guessing. Projection rows reveal actor identities by dictionary and amounts in clear; they are not privacy-preserving. PUBLIC_MINIMAL limits query answers only; it does not apply to the export view, headroom or rows." [A-43.4, as drafted] Salted or blinded leaves change the §7.1 leaf preimage and every pinned root: recorded as a format-3 candidate (DECISIONS), not adopted.

## 16. Reading older formats and migration

### 16.1 Read
Legacy (`continuity-event/0.2`) and format-1 histories open **read only**: replay, every query, `verify`, the attestation checks of their own versions and the export view (with `stateRoot`/`projectionHead` `null`). Any write to them is `FORMAT_READ_ONLY {reason: LEGACY | FORMAT_1 | MIGRATED}` [A-9]. [A-28] They are read, verified and migrated exactly as the frozen rc.1-final build and the `reference-0.3` oracle accept them; event types 29–31 present in legacy files are accepted in legacy reads iff the oracle accepts them; §5's refusal list for legacy reads is informative; the 34 legacy and 90 v1 vectors remain the conformance definition (`replay/v2/still-reads-*`). [A-9] [A-30] Twins for BUILD §4 come from the frozen rc.1-final build, the architect generator's method (§16.3), or an unexported test-only hook. **Enforcement callers refuse read-only histories.** [A-41, as drafted; founder-approved] (1) Hooks, the gateway, `run()`, consequential `authorize`, the execution API and every other caller that acts on a decision (**enforcement callers**) MUST refuse to open a history that is fenced (`FORMAT_READ_ONLY {reason: MIGRATED, target}`), legacy or format 1 (`FORMAT_READ_ONLY {reason: LEGACY | FORMAT_1}`), and a hook treats the refusal as an error (deny unless `failOpen`). `verify`, `inspect`, non-consequential queries, the export view and `migrate` keep reading them. (2) **Opt-in** for never-migrated files only: `enforceFromReadOnlyHistory: true` in hook or gateway config lets an enforcement caller read a legacy or format-1 history that has **no** `.migrated` marker, logging a warning on every open; there is no opt-in for a fenced history (its target exists). (3) `historyId` pinning of readers is A-19.4 (unchanged, §7.5): configured `historyId` SHOULD be set and MUST be enforced when set; the CLI's `migrate` output and `init` print the `historyId` to put in hook and gateway configs. The CLI prints the one-line config change for the opt-in (G2). Read-only legacy does not undercut Continuity Core: Continuity's own engine (frozen in-repo as `reference-0.2`/`reference-0.3` and kept as a product by the founder, answer 3) keeps writing legacy files that the ZK baseline proves; IronTrellis verifies and reads them and never modifies a source (§16.2), so a ZK user keeps a provable file. [A-6] [A-31] [A-58.7] The Python package, until its format-2 port lands, refuses a format-2 file on its first line (`StoreError: unknown store format 'irontrellis-portable-file-store/2'`) and rejects replaying its events at position 0 with `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` (the rc.1 engines' store-level code).

### 16.2 Migrate (ordered algorithm)
`migrateHistory(source, target, options)` / `irontrellis migrate`:
1. **Plan.** Refuse if `target` or `<target>.migration-plan.json` exists with different contents; refuse an unpinned format-1 source unless `acceptUnpinnedSource` (rc.1 rule); refuse a format-1 source that itself carries `migratedFrom` (a file migrated from legacy by rc.1, which would need two shadow levels) with `MIGRATION_UNSUPPORTED {reason: SOURCE_ALREADY_MIGRATED}` — it stays readable and verifiable read-only (DECISIONS, call 13); refuse a format-1 source whose events include an accepted `OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED` (`MIGRATION_UNSUPPORTED {reason: SOURCE_KEY_ROTATED}`) or `WITNESSES_BOUND`/`WITNESSES_UNBOUND` (`{reason: SOURCE_WITNESS_BOUND}`), which likewise stays readable and verifiable read-only [A-63.1]; refuse a source any of whose owner, session or counterparty keys fails the §3.3 declaration test (`{reason: SOURCE_KEY_INVALID}`; read-only with the A-32.3 report; on replay a prefix key failing the test is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) [A-79.1]. Write the plan `{version: "irontrellis-migration-plan/1", source, sourceFormat, sourceHead (own algorithm), target, ownerKey, createdAt}` atomically; the migrator draws `migrationId` (step 4) at plan time and writes it into the plan file [A-40.3, as drafted].
2. **Transcribe** each source event `e_p` in order into a format-2 event with the same `id`, `type`, `timestamp` and `data` except: (a) every Amount field is converted from its marker record to a §3.3 string — **the Amount field paths of a source event, exhaustively** (closed schemas of HF1 §6 and spec 02 §6.3 admit no others): `AUTHORITY_GRANTED.data.grant.constraints.{maxAmount, maxCumulativeAmount}`; `TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED.data.amount`; in `TRANSACTION_INTENT_ADMITTED.data.authorizationProof`: `request.amount`, `effectiveConstraints.{maxAmount, maxCumulativeAmount}`, `permissionPath[i].constraints.{maxAmount, maxCumulativeAmount}`, `intersections[j].effectiveConstraints.{maxAmount, maxCumulativeAmount}`, `intersections[j].path[i].constraints.{maxAmount, maxCumulativeAmount}`, `usageSnapshot[k].admittedCumulativeAmount`; no other event type of formats 0.2 and 1 carries an Amount (`RECEIPT_RECORDED` carries hashes only; receipt *artifacts* are outside the history and keep their own version per §8.5). The same list, in the other direction, is the inversion of step 3; a marker record at any other path in a source is `MIGRATION_UNSUPPORTED {reason: UNEXPECTED_AMOUNT_FIELD}`; (b) [A-56.13] [A-17.2] [A-52.4] in the genesis, format-2 literals replace the source literals in `versions`, `domain` and `policyVersion` (always `irontrellis-local-owner/2`), `ownerKeys` becomes the migrating key set and `migratedFrom` is added; `nextKeyDigests`, `capacity`, `checkpointPolicy`, `projection` and `sessionTimeHorizonMs` [A-179.1 (A-22.6)] are added only when the migration options give them (`migrate` with `sessionTimeHorizonMs` set prints that sessions surviving the prefix cannot write until the first post-migration time-advancing owner event, §6.5.8 [A-179.4 (A-22.6)]); the migration options MUST NOT set `ownerPolicy` (a migrated genesis carries none, §6.5.2 rule 9; `migrate` prints that a policy is set by the first native rotation) [A-91.5] [A-170.5]; `adapterPolicyHash`, `canonicalLineageId`, `rootRecognitionPolicy`, `globalPolicySourceId`, `timeSource` and `finality` are copied as written; nothing else in the genesis is rewritten; the source genesis is kept as written in `migratedFrom.genesis`, so the position-0 shadow hash does not depend on the rewrite; a genesis violating these rewrite rules fails with the same code as the `migratedFrom.historyHash` mismatch; (c) every control event gets a new `controlAuthorization` signed by the new owner key over the §6.1 challenge with the format-2 chain; a **format-1** source's own `/1` authorization is kept verbatim as `data.sourceControlAuthorization`, which MUST be present on every prefix control event of a format-1 source (absent → `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`), is allowed only at positions ≤ `migratedFrom.position` (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID` after it, and in any native history [A-35.1]), is excluded from the `/2` `effectHash` like `controlAuthorization` itself, and is verified in the shadow context (`CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID {position}` [A-40.1]); legacy control events had none and get no such field [A-52.1]; (d) `TRANSACTION_INTENT_DECLARED` events in the prefix carry `declarationAuthorization: null` (MUST be `null` at positions ≤ `migratedFrom.position`, `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` after it and in a native history [A-35.1] [A-52.1]); (e) all other runtime-signed structures (admissions, receipts, administrative authorizations, finding signatures) are copied verbatim: their signatures bound the *source's* challenges and heads; types 14–16 in the prefix carry **no** `recordAuthorization` (present → `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`) [A-52.1]; (f) [A-17.1] a legacy `RUNTIME_SESSION_ADMITTED` in the prefix gains `credentialScheme: "eip191-personal-sign-keccak256"` (format 2 requires the scheme; rc.1 did the same); a prefix `RUNTIME_SESSION_ADMITTED.expiresAt` is absent or a U53 copied unchanged, never `null` (A-62.1) [A-73]. Amount fields in legacy and format-1 canonical events are always marker records (`{"$continuity.bigint": …}`; integers never occur there), so (a) is a bijection on the listed paths. A source-derived state record that cannot satisfy §3.1 canonical limits fails migration before target publication with `MIGRATION_UNSUPPORTED {reason: SOURCE_STATE_LIMIT}`; never truncation [A-52.3].
3. **Shadow context.** For positions ≤ `migratedFrom.position`, replay validates the verbatim runtime signatures and the `sourceControlAuthorization` [A-40.1] against the source's hashing (`shadowHashes`: the source's own per-position history hashes, recomputed by the reader using the source algorithm — keccak prefix wrapper for legacy, sha256 prefix wrapper for format 1 — over the **un-transcribed events**, which the reader reconstructs by the inversion: undo (a) (strings → markers), undo (c) (remove the `/2` `controlAuthorization`; for format 1 restore `sourceControlAuthorization` as `controlAuthorization`), undo (d) (remove `declarationAuthorization: null`), undo (f) (remove the added `credentialScheme`) [A-17.1] [A-56.14], and for position 0 use `migratedFrom.genesis` with `genesisEventId`/`genesisTimestamp` as the source genesis event; the `shadow` record (§7.2) commits to the recomputed hashes) exactly as HF1 §11; prefix events MUST pass the source format's schema, signatures and transitions as the oracle applies them [A-52.1] [A-28]; after `migratedFrom.position` the ordinary format-2 rules apply. The inversion is total because (a), (c), (d) and (f) are each reversible and the source genesis is stored as written. Neither `migratedFrom.sourceAttestation` nor `migratedFrom.migrationId` takes part in the step-3 inversion (the source genesis is stored as written in `migratedFrom.genesis`) [A-40.4, as drafted]. [A-62.1–2] Inside the prefix a `RUNTIME_SESSION_ADMITTED` event keeps exactly the source grammar for `expiresAt` (absent, or a U53 copied unchanged; explicit `null` is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`); transcription and inversion preserve field presence, and the derived `session` record normalizes an absent source expiry to `null`. [A-52.2] A migrated session with `expiresAt: null` (record value) is valid for prefix verification only; at every position after the prefix it is treated as expired for every purpose, including the check that blocks a fresh admission (§6.2) [A-62.3]; post-prefix signing needs a fresh finite-expiry session (OPERATOR says so; `migrate` prints the affected session ids). [A-52.1] Inside the prefix the §8.8 bounds are not applied; outside it every native rule applies and the bounds count migrated entries. [A-52.5] These prefix exceptions are closed and MUST be coded against `migratedFrom`'s presence (`inPrefix(p)`, P.5), never against an undefined comparison. [A-130.1] A history that ends before `migratedFrom.position` is rejected when its replay completes with `TRANSITION_INVALID` (reason `migrated prefix incomplete`) at **position `eventCount`**, the first missing position; the store's `INVALID_HISTORY {position}` on open names the same position.
4. **Genesis.** `migratedFrom = {format: "continuity-event/0.2" | "irontrellis-event/1", historyHash (the source head hash in the source algorithm), position, recordHash (the source's last store-line recordHash as written in the source file; a migration from an event list without lines recomputes it with the source store's line algorithm; not checkable from the target alone — a stated limit [A-17.4]), genesis (the source genesis data as written), genesisEventId, genesisTimestamp, versions (the source `versions` block as written), sourceAttestation, migrationId}` [A-40.2–3, as drafted; founder-approved]:
   - **`sourceAttestation`**: for a format-1 source, the source pin's `irontrellis-head-attestation/1` (the attestation object as written, `{version, domain?, head: {hash, position, canonicalTime}, keyId, scheme, signature}`; HF1 §9 is the authority for whether `domain` is present [A-80]) of `migratedFrom.historyHash` at `migratedFrom.position`, copied as written and verified on replay under `migratedFrom.genesis.ownerKeys` (`EVENT_DATA_INVALID` when it fails: signature over the object minus `keyId`/`scheme`/`signature` exactly as the rc.1-final verifier; `domain`, when present, equals `migratedFrom.genesis.domain`; `head.hash`/`head.position` equal `migratedFrom.historyHash`/`.position`; `head.canonicalTime` equals the source head's canonical time as rc.1-final defines it, checked once the prefix is replayed, `{position: migratedFrom.position}` [A-80]); `null` for a legacy source or when the plan used `acceptUnpinnedSource`. `verify` and the export report state, for `null`: "legacy prefix: control events attested by the migrating key only" or "format-1 prefix: source unpinned (`acceptUnpinnedSource`)". [A-63.2] Verification under `migratedFrom.genesis.ownerKeys` is complete because a format-1 source containing a rotation or a witness binding is refused at step 1 (A-63.1) and such a type inside a prefix is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` on replay (coded against `migratedFrom`, A-52.5). Lifting this is a later format-2 addendum (A-63.3: epoch-scoped shadow per HF1 §7.1, `sourceAttestation` under the epoch at `migratedFrom.position`, and a prefix rotation never opening a target epoch); nothing of it is pinned at G2.5.
   - **`migrationId`**: 32 random bytes as a ContentHash-shaped `0x` + 64 hex string, drawn by the migrator at plan time and written into the plan file (step 1), so that two migrations of copies of one source never share a `historyId`.

   Both are closed-schema additions to the genesis `migratedFrom` (A-55.2); neither takes part in the step-3 inversion [A-40.4].
5. **Write** the target file and its pin (attested by the new owner key), then the snapshot.
6. **Fence.** Write `<source>.migrated = {version: "irontrellis-migrated/1", target, sourceHead, targetHead, migratedAt}` atomically beside the source. [A-184.1] `target` is written as the path of the target **relative to the directory containing the source file**, with `/` separators (a leading run of `..` is allowed; no other `.`/`..` segments); when no relative path exists (another Windows drive), the absolute path with `/` separators (stated limit). A reader accepts either form, resolves a relative `target` against the source's directory, and compares paths only after canonicalising both sides (symlinks resolved, `realpath`); a marker or lock-holder path is never compared as a string. The same canonicalisation applies to every path comparison of §16.2 (`SOURCE_FENCED`, `HISTORY_EXISTS`, `--status`, the fence check) and to writer-lock holder paths. The plan file (engine-private, never published) keeps absolute paths. Every later open of a history whose `.migrated` marker exists is read-only with `FORMAT_READ_ONLY {reason: "MIGRATED", target}`, and an enforcement caller refuses to open it at all, with no opt-in (§16.1) [A-41, as drafted]; the source bytes are never modified (they remain the archive and the ZK-provable file). A marker whose `sourceHead` does not match the file is `MIGRATION_MARKER_INVALID` and the open fails.
7. **Resume and status.** Every step is idempotent: a re-run with the same plan verifies existing outputs instead of refusing them, resumes at the first missing output, and finishes by writing the fence; `irontrellis migrate --status <plan>` reports `{phase: PLANNED | WRITTEN | PINNED | FENCED, verified: boolean}` from the actual files, not from a journal. Builder tests T3 and T4 (kill at each step).
8. **Projection and records of the prefix:** rows (§12) and state records (§7.2) are computed over the transcribed events exactly as for native format-2 events (the intent `metric` defaults to `"amount"`, `declarationDigest` is `null` for prefix intents; rows of prefix types 14–16 set flag bit 1 [A-51.6]; the `shadow` record is present from position 0 [A-17.3]).

The rules of HF1 §11 (verbatim prefix, no rotation or witness binding inside the prefix) apply to both source formats [A-63] [A-78.2: reading (a), confirmed]; the prefix is always single-signer under `/1` and `/2`: `additionalControlAuthorizations` inside a prefix is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` at C4, coded against `migratedFrom` (A-52.5) [A-91.5]; the G2.5 format-1→2 twin uses an unrotated source. Architect-pinned vectors for migration are not in `conformance/v2` (the shadow context needs the engine); the builder's regenerated suite pins a legacy → 2 and a format-1 → 2 migration (final head and root), cross-checked at G2.5; the regenerated suite adds unsigned source lifecycle events in the prefix, a missing-expiry session used after the prefix, a source list above the §8.8 bounds, native `null` authorization, and the first event after the prefix [A-52]; a native declaration with `declarationAuthorization: null`, a native control event carrying `sourceControlAuthorization`, and a migrated history with a prefix declaration lacking `null` and a post-prefix declaration carrying `null` [A-35.3, as drafted]; a format-1 source with an edited post-signature control event (refused by the shadow-context verification of step 2 (c), A-40.1) and a format-1 prefix with a forged `sourceAttestation` (refused at step 4, A-40.2) [A-40.5, as drafted]. The unfrozen regenerated twin roots change under A-40.1, A-52 and A-55.3; the builder's regenerated legacy→2 and format-1→2 migration roots change (they are pinned at G2.5, §16.2; not frozen) [A-40.5, as drafted], including under A-40.2–3 (G2 A-40: "regenerated twin roots change, which G2.5 pins anyway").

### 16.3 Transcription for test twins
The conformance generator transcribes an engine-built format-1 history to format 2 (amount strings, new literals, re-signed control events and admissions, dropped consumptions). This is a test technique, not a product path.

## 17. Adapter line protocol (conformance)
Unchanged contract (`conformance/legacy/ADAPTER.md`) with these format-2 additions: a `replay` result carries `stateRoot` = `rootAt(head.position)` (the root **after** the last event, including that event's own `event` record — for a prefix ending in a `STATE_CHECKPOINT` this differs from the checkpoint's `data.stateRoot`, which is `rootAt(position−1)`) and `projectionHead` (both `null` unless the history is format 2 and accepted); `authorize` results carry `budgetId` (`null` unless a budget decided); new op `headroom` `{op, events, budgetId, at}` → the §9.7 record or `null`; new op `export` `{op, events, fromPosition, toPosition, include}` → the §15 record **without** `events`, `checkpoints`, `fromRecords`, `toRecords`, `inclusion`, `keyEvents`, `attestations` and `referencedEvents` (the runner compares `version, domain, genesisEventHash, from, to, eventCount, checkpointIds`; unchanged by A-27/A-53 [A-53.3] and by the additive `historyId` [A-47.1]); new op `duty-view` `{op, events, dutyId}` → the §13.3 record or `null`; `verify-attestation` results carry `stateRootValid` and `projectionValid` (both `true` for non-format-2 histories). Amounts in format-2 vectors are decimal strings in `input` and `expect`, parsed without marker revival [A-7] under the strict grammar of §3.1, which applies to adapter inputs and outputs for format-2 vectors [A-34, as drafted].

## 18. Error codes

**Replay** (`code` of a rejected replay; one meaning per code [A-10]): `INVALID_INPUT`, `GENESIS_REQUIRED`, `GENESIS_DUPLICATE`, `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_SCHEMA`, `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`, `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_TYPE`, `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`, `EVENT_ID_DUPLICATE`, `EVENT_TIME_REGRESSION`, `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (with `{position}` in the shadow context [A-40.1]), `TRANSITION_INVALID`, `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY` (a format-2 replay code; rc.1 had it as a writer code only), **new in format 2:** `CAPACITY_EXCEEDED`, `CAPACITY_RESERVED` [A-0] [A-10.1], `SESSION_FENCED` [A-29], `DECLARATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `REFUSAL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `REFUSAL_NOT_REPRODUCED`, `EFFECT_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `EFFECT_LIMIT`, `COUNTERPARTY_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, `RECORD_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` (counterparty wrapper and lifecycle records [A-51.4]), `STATE_ROOT_MISMATCH`, `PROJECTION_HEAD_MISMATCH`, `OBSERVATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE` (type 42, §10.3 [A-71.8]). `AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` does not exist [A-51.4]. Appendix A gives the check order per event type. The set is unchanged by §6.5 and by the time rules T1–T3: every quorum, presence, distinctness and floor failure is `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, every shape failure is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`, and every other refusal they introduce is `TRANSITION_INVALID` (Appendix F invariant 22); the set stays at 26 [A-92.6] [A-125.3]. A free-text reason message accompanying a code is informative; conformance is the code and the position (Appendix A). Enumerated `reason` literals written in braces in this section are normative identifiers [A-191.4].

**Decision (DENY):** the 30 rc.1-final codes, **new:** `BUDGET_PER_OPERATION_EXCEEDED`, `BUDGET_COUNT_EXCEEDED`, `BUDGET_AMOUNT_EXCEEDED`, `BUDGET_PERIOD_UNDEFINED`, `BUDGET_SET_TOO_LARGE`, `AMOUNT_OVERFLOW` (`DenialCode` gains these six [A-10.2]). Decision (INDETERMINATE): the 8 rc.1-final codes, unchanged. Refusal records of source CONNECTOR use `code: "CONNECTOR_REFUSED"`.

**Store/API** (`IronTrellisErrorCode`): rc.1-final codes plus `FORMAT_READ_ONLY` (reasons `LEGACY`, `FORMAT_1`, `MIGRATED`; also returned when an enforcement caller refuses to open a read-only history [A-41]), `MIGRATION_MARKER_INVALID`, `MIGRATION_UNSUPPORTED` (reasons `SOURCE_ALREADY_MIGRATED`, `UNEXPECTED_AMOUNT_FIELD`, `SOURCE_STATE_LIMIT` [A-52.3], `SOURCE_KEY_ROTATED`, `SOURCE_WITNESS_BOUND` [A-63.1] [A-76], `SOURCE_KEY_INVALID` [A-79.1]), `SNAPSHOT_STALE` (informational: a snapshot was discarded and rebuilt; also a resource-limit violation [A-44.2]), `CHECKPOINT_MISMATCH` (the API refuses to write a checkpoint whose root disagrees with its own state — a self-check that must never fire), `DUTY_WRITER_REQUIRED`, `OPERATION_CONFLICT` (rc.1, reused for duty operations), `TRANSITION_REJECTED` [A-8] [A-12], `INVALID_INPUT` (API misuse, incl. the `format` option [A-9], export ranges [A-53.1], a strict-grammar violation in an adapter or API input [A-34.1], the key-role refusals of §6.3 [A-32.3] and `{reason: OBSERVED_AT_AHEAD}` [A-45.2]), `INVALID_HISTORY {position}` (chain break, rc.1 code widened [A-33]; also a failed lazy line verification [A-33.2] and a strict-grammar violation in a store line [A-34.1]), `HISTORY_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` [A-19.4], `ATTESTED_STATE_ROOT_MISMATCH {reason: PIN | ANCHOR_LOG}` [A-20.3], `EXPORT_DEPENDENCIES_MISSING` [A-53.2], `WITNESS_UNAVAILABLE {reason: MAX_UNWITNESSED}` [A-24.2], `HISTORY_CONFLICT`, `WRITE_UNCONFIRMED` [A-11], `CLOCK_INVALID` (rc.1; not raised for power-reducing control events [A-22.3]), writer pre-checks with the replay meaning: `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY`, `CAPACITY_EXCEEDED`, `CAPACITY_RESERVED` [A-56.8], `OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE` [A-71.8], and `REFUSAL_NOT_REPRODUCED` thrown before any append [A-10.3]. Store-level in the rc.1 engines: `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` at position 0 [A-58.7]. Under an owner policy (§6.5) the store/API set gains: `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` as a writer pre-check with the replay meaning (the §6.5.3 rule applied at `submitControlEvent` or at a single-phase append, §6.5.8), carrying informative `{required, present}`, and `HISTORY_CONFLICT` on a head moved between `prepareControlEvent` and `submitControlEvent` (existing code) [A-92.6]; `INVALID_INPUT {reason: POLICY_RESET}` from `rotateOwnerKey`, `commitNextKey` and the CLI when the pre-event policy is not the default and no `ownerPolicy` is supplied without `resetPolicy: true` (§6.5.8) [A-96.4]; the time-rule writer pre-checks `TRANSITION_REJECTED {reason: ATTESTATION_EXPIRY, expiresAt}` (T1), `TRANSITION_REJECTED {reason: ATTESTATION_HORIZON, horizon}` (T2) and `TRANSITION_REJECTED {reason: SESSION_TIME_HORIZON, ownerTime, horizonMs}` (T3) of §6.5.8 — the API never throws the replay code `TRANSITION_INVALID`, and a builder that shipped it in these pre-checks renames and records it in DEVIATIONS [A-172.2] [A-179.4 (A-22.6)]; `MIGRATION_UNSUPPORTED` and the migration options are unchanged (a policy is never set by migration, §6.5.2 rule 9). `CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason: NO_SIGNER}` covers an unreachable `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` as §6.5.8 defines it over the counting set and T2 (report-level, below) [A-95.6] [A-119.3]. **Witness** (`irontrellis-witness/1`, §7.4): `WITNESS_POLICY_UNKNOWN` (an older witness, behind, not wrong) [A-100.1]; `WITNESS_SIGNER_UNQUALIFIED` (a head statement whose signer is unqualified under the epoch policy the witness holds) [A-111.3]; the `verifyRotationEvent` result carries `stateChecked: false` [A-120.1] [A-125.3]. (Informative.) The shape in which the gateway surfaces upstream refusals, effects and receipts inside a tool result (`structuredContent.irontrellis`) is a convention of the gateway package, `irontrellis-tool-result/1`, documented there with its own changelog, not a Core shape; the `code` values inside it MUST be this section's codes verbatim and the receipts the §8.5 payloads unchanged [A-121.1].

**Report-level (not errors):** `CLOCK_BEHIND_HEAD {skewMs}`, `CLOCK_AHEAD_OF_READER` [A-22]; resume outcome `FULL_REPLAY` with its reason (including a `/2` pin refused as a resume root under §7.4 [A-101.0] [A-111.1]); not-recorded refusal reasons `COALESCED`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `SPOOLED`, `RECORD_DEADLINE`, `NOT_REPRODUCED_AT_DRAIN`, `SESSION_TIME_HORIZON` (§6.5.8, §10.1.1 item 7 [A-179.4 (A-22.6)]), a capacity code, `HISTORY_CONFLICT`, a signer failure, `WRITE_UNCONFIRMED` [A-56.3]; `verify` listings of effects whose metric differs from the intent's [A-36]; `verify`/`inspect` listings of credential reuse and of legacy or format-1 keys failing the §3.3 declaration test [A-32.3], of CONNECTOR refusals whose `request.actorId` is not an agent, and (`verify`) of every agent that signed a CONNECTOR refusal or CONNECTOR-measured effect [A-36.2]; the `verify`/export statements for a `null` `migratedFrom.sourceAttestation` [A-40.2]; `CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason}` for a failed automatic checkpoint, `{reason: RESERVE}` (once per open, a writer-process rule; a file-based advisory marking every suppressed point is a conforming checker [A-84]) for a skipped one (report-level, as listed in the draft's summary) [A-46], and `{reason: NO_SIGNER}` for an automatic checkpoint or handover seal the writer's signers cannot sign to `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` (§7.3, §6.5.8) [A-95.6] [A-119.3] [A-174.2]; the export verifier's "byte limits not judged" statement for `from > 0` [A-75.3] and its "T2 not judged" statement for a key event whose `e_{q−1}` the view does not carry (§15) [A-131.1]; the §10.3 observation listings [A-71.7]; `rollback_check` findings carry no code [A-84]. The label `SNAPSHOT_ROOT_MISMATCH` is not adopted [A-57.1].

**Report-level listings under an owner policy and the time rules (`verify`, `inspect`; §6.5.8 collects the §6.5 items).** [A-95.6] [A-105.3] [A-115.3] [A-125.3] The counting and non-counting entries of every ◆ event with the class of each (`LAPSED` included), and every agent key that can count toward no type [A-95.6] [A-105.3]; attester credential reuse, attestation intervals, keys without a statement under `ATTESTED`, owner keys of a scheme the attestation profile cannot name (§6.5.5), types unreachable under the attested set of epoch `n` (§6.5.2), and evidence-bundle presence (a second, optional report, never a condition of acceptance, §6.5.5) [A-95.6] [A-105.3] [A-93.5]; given `--as-of <U53>` (optional; default the head's `canonicalTime`), every statement of the head epoch with `expiresAt ≤ asOf + window` (window default 30 days, `2,592,000,000` ms) and the head epoch's counts of `HUMAN`, `LAPSED`, `AGENT` and `NONE` keys at `asOf` — replay never consults `asOf` [A-98.7] [A-109.2]; "policy reset at p", every rotation whose resulting policy is the default while the pre-event policy was not (effective comparison, §6.5.8) [A-96.4] [A-112]; every ◆ event whose timestamp exceeds `τ(p)` by more than `--time-window` (default 30 days) and every ◆ event that crossed an attestation expiry [A-109.3] [A-115.3]; the `PR_eff` verdict per `PR_types` event, rollover budgets updated by the ordinary quorum (§6.5.3), carried statements and carried expired statements per rotation (§6.5.5), dead carried commitments (§6.5.7) [A-115.3] [A-125.3]; every pin and anchor-log entry whose signer is `AGENT`, `NONE` or `LAPSED` at its `canonicalTime` (§7.4) [A-111.3]; every `AGENT`-classed key whose label (§6.5.2 `labels`) names a committee, and every policy with no `AGENT` entry in which a label names a committee (a committee co-signing key is `AGENT`-class by ceremony rule, companion document) [A-140.1]; every policy whose `byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT` exceeds the number of non-`AGENT` credentials of its epoch (never a refusal; extending §6.5.2 rule 10 to `STATE_CHECKPOINT` is E-34) [A-151.2]. A spooled refusal count under T3 is an `inspect` item (§6.5.8) [A-179.4 (A-22.6)].

**Time listings (file-only; read no clock; change no vector or root).** [A-180.1 (A-22.5)] `verify` and `inspect` list every ◇ or ▲ event at `p` with `¬inPrefix(p)` and `e_p.timestamp − e_{p−1}.timestamp > TIME_LISTING_SKEW_MS` as **"session time advance"** `{position, type, sessionId, advanceMs}` (`sessionId` = the binding's session; the outer session for ▲). They also list every ◆ event with `e_p.timestamp = e_{p−1}.timestamp` whose predecessor is a non-control event as **"owner event at head time"** `{position, type}`. `TIME_LISTING_SKEW_MS = 300,000` is a fixed constant of the format, not reader configuration (the reader's `maxClockSkewMs` remains for `CLOCK_AHEAD_OF_READER` only). Both listings are file-only, read no clock, and change no vector or root [A-180.1 (A-22.5)].

**Anchor-time bracketing (`verify`, when anchor evidence is supplied; report-level).** [A-181.1] [A-181.2] [A-181.4] Let `A` be the verified anchors in `CONSENSUS_VERIFIED` mode supplied to `verify`, each with head position `h_i = statement.head.position` and block time `T_i = report.finality.l1BlockTime` (chain evidence from the anchoring profile's verify report, never a clock reading and never read in replay; the report and its `finality.l1BlockTime` field are the companion document's, A-181.3). For every event at `p ≥ 1`: `T(p) = min{T_i : h_i ≥ p}` (the earliest consensus-verified block time among anchors whose head is at or past `p`; monotone sequences are not assumed). `verify` lists `e_p` as **"event time ahead of its anchor"** `{position, type, sessionId: Identifier|null, anchor: {statementDigest, headPosition, l1Level, l1BlockTime}, aheadMs}` iff `e_p.timestamp > T(p) + δ`, naming the anchor that attains the minimum, with `δ = TIME_LISTING_SKEW_MS = 300,000` ms, fixed (one rule for ◆, ◇ and ▲; `δ_r` as a reader option is withdrawn) [A-181.1] [A-181.4]. Positions with no such anchor are reported as **unbracketed** (`bracketedThrough: max h_i` or `null`), never as passing. Only anchors verified in `CONSENSUS_VERIFIED` mode bracket; `TRUSTED_NODE` anchors are listed separately as "trusted-node evidence (not bracketing)" and never make a tail bracketed [A-181.2]. Chain time is evidence about when a prefix became public; it is never used in replay and never changes a verdict (§7.4 [A-94.1]). The listing joins the time listings above; it is report-level and uses no refusal code. An event written between an anchored head and that anchor's inclusion legitimately carries a time earlier than the inclusion time, so no "behind its anchor" listing exists [A-181.4]. A TEST-class anchor (the class is derived from `(chainId, profileId)` by the companion document's table, A-183.1) brackets only under the label "bracketed (test chain)", never as production bracketing; a TEST anchor verified in `TRUSTED_NODE` mode is trusted-node evidence like any other [A-183.2] [OR-01].

## 19. Versioning
[A-55] Format 2 is unreleased. Its effective definition is this document together with the frozen suite (the 86 of `conformance/v2-g3`, A-95.9) [A-191.3] plus the builder's E5 regenerated suite (`conformance/v2-regenerated/MANIFEST.json` as hashed in the repository-root `MANIFEST.json` [A-192.2]; the E5 regeneration landed, and the current manifest and vector ledger, including MANIFEST revision 2 of the regenerated suite, is P.8 [A-171.3] [A-184.2] [OR-01]). While unreleased, a closed-schema addition, a decision-procedure change at inputs no frozen vector reaches, or a projection-flag assignment for a type no frozen vector contains is a format-2 addendum when ruled by the architect (A-21, A-22.1, A-26.1, A-40.2–3, A-50, A-51, A-54 were made under this frame, as were the closed-schema additions `ownerPolicy`, `additionalControlAuthorizations` and `sessionTimeHorizonMs` of §6.5 and §5.1, each guarded so that no frozen vector, pinned root or A-71 vector changes [A-91.0] [A-92.0] [A-179.1 (A-22.6)]). After release, the following applies as written: a change to any hashed preimage, any closed schema, the state-record table, the projection table or the decision procedure is format 3. Additive, non-hashed, non-semantic changes (new query ops, new export `include` options, new CLI commands, additive top-level export fields [A-56.20]) are minor versions of the packages, not of the format. The genesis `versions` block is the only place a reader learns the format; readers MUST refuse unknown literals rather than guess. The packages ship as `1.0.0-rc.2` (founder answer 8). The G3 deliverables are this document (the consolidated text A-55.3 names `SPEC-IRONTRELLIS-CORE-v2-CONSOLIDATED.md`; this file is that text under the job's name) and a conformance manifest listing the addenda and vector sha256s; `ADDENDA-INDEX-BY-SECTION.md` is its map.

**Numbering of addenda.** [A-186.1] Addenda are numbered `A-n` in issue order and inserted, never renumbered (P.4). A-138 and A-139: reserved by the ADDENDA-G6.6 header for G6.5, which issued A-133…A-137 only; no ruling bears either number. References to "A-170" before ADDENDA-AS01 are the allocation line only; A-170 is that file's first ruling [A-186.1].

## Appendix A — Replay error codes and check order per event type

**A.1 The replay code set (26 codes [A-71.8]).** `INVALID_INPUT`, `GENESIS_REQUIRED`, `GENESIS_DUPLICATE`, `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_SCHEMA`, `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`, `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_TYPE`, `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`, `EVENT_ID_DUPLICATE`, `EVENT_TIME_REGRESSION`, `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `SESSION_FENCED`, `DECLARATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `REFUSAL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `REFUSAL_NOT_REPRODUCED`, `EFFECT_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `EFFECT_LIMIT`, `COUNTERPARTY_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, `RECORD_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `TRANSITION_INVALID`, `STATE_ROOT_MISMATCH`, `PROJECTION_HEAD_MISMATCH`, `CAPACITY_CONTROL_ONLY`, `CAPACITY_EXCEEDED`, `CAPACITY_RESERVED`, `OBSERVATION_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`, `OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE`. The set is unchanged by §6.5 and by the time rules T1–T3 (§18) [A-92.6] [A-125.3].

**A.2 Common prefix (every event, in order).** Before replay, every format-2 JSON text is parsed under the strict grammar of §3.1 [A-34.1, as drafted]: a violation in a store line is the store code `INVALID_HISTORY {position}` (in the snapshot, `SNAPSHOT_STALE` and `FULL_REPLAY`); in an adapter or API input, `INVALID_INPUT`. C1 envelope/capture → `INVALID_INPUT`. C2 position 0 must be `DEPLOYMENT_INITIALIZED` → `GENESIS_REQUIRED`; at position 0, unknown `eventSchemaVersion` → `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_SCHEMA`, non-exact `versions` or disagreement with line 0 → `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`. C3 type known in format 2 → `UNSUPPORTED_EVENT_TYPE`. C4 closed schema, Amount strings, `$continuity.` keys, prefix/native field rules (`declarationAuthorization`, `sourceControlAuthorization`, `recordAuthorization`, `expiresAt`), and, coded against `migratedFrom` [A-52.5], inside any migrated prefix types 26–28 [A-63.1] and 32–42, inside a format-1 prefix also 29–31, and any prefix **counterparty** key (rows 36, 37) failing the §3.3 test [A-79.1] (prefix session (8) and rotation (26) keys are tested at their rows' stage, after control authorization [A-79.2]; the dual-fault code is `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID`) [A-90.4] → `EVENT_DATA_INVALID` [A-78.1].
