## Appendix F — Invariants and liveness

[A-186.2] [A-114] [A-124] This appendix is new in r5. It is **normative for the independent checker and the Lean model** — the checker evaluates every full form marked for it over its state and reports a violation, and the Lean model proves the statements marked for it — and **informative for writers**: no sentence here is a replay rule, replay is defined by §6.5, §7.4–§7.5, §15 and Appendix A, and nothing here loosens or tightens them. Each property is a *claim about those rules*. An accepted history that violates a property marked *intended* is a defect in the rules (to be ruled on, never silently repaired by reading the property as a rule); a history that violates a property marked *known not to hold* is nothing new. The catalogue replaces the withdrawn A-104 in full and is amended as the later rulings say; the withdrawn text is listed in Appendix D only [A-114] [A-186.3]. Where this appendix cites a rule, it names the r5 section that carries it; the terms it shares with §6.5 (`σ(p)`, `τ(p)`, `K`, `D`, `P_w`, `P`, `t_set`, `cred`, `digest`, `creds`, `keyIds`, `revealed`, `isAtt`, `foreign`, `attCreds`, `H`, `hc`, `started`, `introduced`, `MAXT`, `rollsOver`, `anc`) are the same objects as in §6.5.1; F.1 restates them in the model's notation so that the appendix is self-contained, and where a definition lives in §6.5 the model points at it rather than repeating it.

**F.1 The model.** [A-114.0] [A-124.0] Every term is defined here or in §6.5.1; nothing is left to the reader.

*Histories and states.* [A-114.0] [A-124.0] A history is a finite sequence of accepted events `e_0 … e_n`, `e_0` the genesis (type 0). Event kinds: ◆ (owner-signed control, the 22 types of P.5), ◇ (session-signed), ▲ (administrative, runtime-signed). `σ(p)` is the state before `e_p` (all §7.2 records); `σ(p+1)` after it. `τ(p) = e_{p−1}.timestamp` for `p ≥ 1`. Timestamps are non-decreasing (`e_p.timestamp ≥ τ(p)`), U53 milliseconds. `Accepted(e, p)` means replay accepts `e` at `p` over `σ(p)` in the Appendix A order; a *reachable state* is `σ(n+1)` for some accepted history. Quantifiers range over accepted histories unless stated.

*Epochs.* [A-114.0] [A-175.2] An owner epoch record is `{fromPosition, keys, nextKeyDigests?, policy?}` with ghost `t_set` (the opening event's timestamp). The shorthand `nextKeyDigests?`, and every "present" in this appendix that speaks of `nextKeyDigests`, means **non-null**: the `ownerKeyEpoch` record keeps `nextKeyDigests: [ContentHash]|null` (§7.2, §6.5.1) [A-175.2]. Epoch 0 is set by the genesis; every accepted type 26 at `r` opens `{fromPosition: r + 1, keys: data.ownerKeys, nextKeyDigests: data.nextKeyDigests as written, policy: data.ownerPolicy as written}`. The epoch in force at `p` has the greatest `fromPosition ≤ p`. `K(p)`, `D(p) = nextKeyDigests ?? []` (as a set), `P_w(p)` (as written), `P(p) = P_w(p) ?? {threshold: 1}`, `t_set(p)`. For liveness, the *append epoch* at head position `h` is the epoch in force at `h + 1`; its values are written `K⁺, D⁺, P⁺` (so a rotation at `h` has `K⁺ = its data.ownerKeys`).

*Keys.* [A-114.0] A key is `{keyId, scheme, credential}`; `cred(k) = (scheme, lowercase(credential))`; `digest(k)` = HF1 §7.2 `ownerKeyDigest`, a function of `cred(k)`, injective (CR-2). `creds(K)`, `keyIds(K)` as expected (§6.5.1). `revealed(e) = {k ∈ e.data.ownerKeys : k.keyId ∉ keyIds(K(p))}`. `keepsEveryKey(e)`: §6.5.4 (count equality, each entry equals a member of `K(p)` in all three fields). *Committed epoch*: `nextKeyDigests` present (non-null).

*Policies.* [A-114.0] [A-124.0] `ownerPolicy` per §6.5.2; absent fields are absent. `M_eff(e) = P(p).threshold` when `e.type ∈ PR_types ∧ byType[e.type] present ∧ byType[e.type] < threshold ∧ ¬PR_eff(e, σ(p))`, otherwise `P(p).byType[e.type] ?? P(p).threshold` (§6.5.3); `PR_types` has the seven names of §6.5.3; `LOW_types = PR_types ∪ {STATE_CHECKPOINT}`; `M_rot(P) = P.byType["OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED"] ?? P.threshold`; `byType` keys are the §5 type literals. `isAtt(P) := P.humanFloor = "ATTESTED"`; absent `humanFloor` has `DECLARED` semantics and differs from `"DECLARED"` as written. `PR_eff(e, σ(p))` is the exact predicate of §6.5.3 (a function of `(e.data, e.timestamp, σ(p))`). `rollsOver`, `anc`, `introduced`: §6.5.1. `G(e, p)`: §6.5.4. `isCheckpoint(e) := e.type = 35`.

*Attestations.* [A-114.0] [A-124.0] `validAt(a, t) := a.notBefore ≤ t < a.expiresAt`; `stmt(P, c)` the unique statement of `P.attestations` with `subject` credential `c` (if any); `attestedAny(P, c) := stmt(P, c)` exists; `attestedValidAt(P, c, t) := stmt exists ∧ validAt(stmt, t)`; `started(P, c, t) := stmt(P, c) exists ∧ stmt.notBefore ≤ t`; `subjects(P)` = the set of subject credentials; `carried(a', p) := a' ∈ P(p).attestations` as written; `H(p) := max{a.expiresAt : a ∈ P(p).attestations}` (the attestation horizon, §6.5.1); `renewal(a', a, p, t) := subject creds equal ∧ max(a.notBefore, t_set(p)) < a'.notBefore ≤ t ∧ a'.expiresAt > a.expiresAt` (§6.5.5 (6)). Class order `NONE < LAPSED < HUMAN` (with `AGENT` incomparable).

*Records.* [A-124.0] `foreign(σ)` and `attCreds(P)`: §6.5.1. `allow(σ, r, t)` and `live(σ, t)`: F.5 below, over the records named there; ASM-1…5 (F.4) are hypotheses of invariant 31's full form.

*Signatures and admissibility.* [A-114.0] `S(e) = [controlAuthorization] ++ (additionalControlAuthorizations ?? [])`; `NKA(e) = nextKeyAuthorizations ?? []` (outside `S(e)`). `adm(e) = K(p)`, except `adm(e) = K(p) ∪ {k ∈ revealed(e) : digest(k) ∈ D(p)}` when `e.type = 26 ∧ P(p).recovery = "COMMITTED_NEXT_KEYS"` (§6.5.4). An entry resolves to the unique key of `adm(e)` with its `keyId` (none: it fails §6.5.3 step 3). `isRecovery(e)`: §6.5.4. `isPolicyRot(e) := e.type = 26 ∧ keepsEveryKey(e) ∧ e.data.ownerPolicy ≠ P_w(p)` as written. `isRestoration(e)`: §6.5.5 (1)–(6). `X(e) := PR_eff(e, σ(p)) ∨ isRestoration(e) ∨ isCheckpoint(e)`.

*Classes and counting.* [A-114.0] [A-124.0] `cls(P, K, D, t, c) ∈ {HUMAN, AGENT, LAPSED, NONE}`: §6.5.3, total over every credential. For a signer entry `s` resolved to key `k`: `cls(s) = cls(P(p), K(p), D(p), e.timestamp, cred(k))`. `C(e) ⊆ S(e)` and the floor: the counting rule of §6.5.3. `hc(e) := |{s ∈ C(e) : cls(s) ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED}}|`. Challenge: §6.1 `/2` over `previousHead` (the head at `p − 1`) and `effectHash` (= `data` minus `controlAuthorization`, `additionalControlAuthorizations`, `nextKeyAuthorizations`, `sourceControlAuthorization`); every entry of `S(e)` and `NKA(e)` verifies over the same `hashCanonical(challenge)`.

*Holders (liveness only).* [A-114.0] `Hld` is a set of credentials whose private material some willing party holds; H-1: for `c ∈ Hld` and any challenge the holder produces a verifying signature; H-2 (L10 only): no signature verifies under `c ∉ Hld`. Holders are credentials, never persons.

*Verifiers.* [A-124.0] `acceptSF(e, epoch, previousHead, genesis)` is the state-free subset of Appendix A row 26 defined in §7.4.

**F.2 Safety invariants.** [A-114.1] [A-124.1] Status letters, informative: **U** unchanged since the first catalogue (G6.1), **C** changed by a later ruling, **N** new. Each is *intended* unless marked *known not to hold*.

1. **(One-signature floor) U.** [A-114.1] Every accepted ◆ `e` has `controlAuthorization` naming a key of `adm(e)` whose signature verifies over `e`'s challenge; `|S(e)| ≥ 1`.
2. **(Threshold floor) U.** [A-114.1] In every epoch `P.threshold ≥ 1` and every `byType[T] ≥ 1`; hence `M_eff(e) ≥ 1`.
3. **(Same challenge) U.** [A-114.1] Every entry of `S(e)` and of `NKA(e)` verifies over the single `hashCanonical(challenge(e))`, which binds `previousHead` and `effectHash`; `effectHash` covers `ownerPolicy`, `ownerKeys` and `nextKeyDigests` and excludes exactly the four signature fields.
4. **(Admissibility) U.** [A-114.1] Every entry of `S(e)` resolves in `adm(e)` as defined in F.1.
5. **(No self-admission) C.** [A-114.1] For every `s ∈ C(e)` with resolved key `k`: `cred(k) ∈ creds(K(p))`, or (`isRecovery(e) ∧ k ∈ revealed(e) ∧ digest(k) ∈ D(p)`). No key first introduced by `e` counts otherwise; `isRecovery` is structural (§6.5.4), so this is not circular.
6. **(Distinctness) C.** [A-114.1] The `keyId`s of `S(e)` are pairwise distinct and the credentials of their resolved keys are pairwise distinct. `NKA(e)` is a separate list: a recovery signer appearing in both is not a repeat.
7. **(Quorum) U.** [A-114.1] `|C(e)| ≥ M_eff(e)`.
8. **(Agent never alone) U.** [A-114.1] Every accepted ◆ `e` has `s ∈ C(e)` with `cls(s) ∉ {AGENT, NONE}`.
9. **(Agents never on key or policy changes) U.** [A-114.1] `e.type = 26 ⇒ ∀ s ∈ C(e): cls(s) ∉ {AGENT, NONE}`.
10. **(Lapsed keys reduce, restore or checkpoint only) C.** [A-124.1] `∀ s ∈ C(e): cls(s) = LAPSED ⇒ PR_eff(e, σ(p)) ∨ isRestoration(e) ∨ isCheckpoint(e)` (unchanged in form; `PR_eff` includes `BUDGET_DECLARED` and excludes rollover updates, §6.5.3).
11. **(Human present on every non-exempt event) C.** [A-114.1] `isAtt(P(p)) ∧ ¬X(e) ⇒ ∃ s ∈ C(e): cls(s) = HUMAN`.
12. **(Restoration preserves) C.** [A-124.1] `isRestoration(e) ⇒`: `K(p+1) = K(p)` (keyIds and credentials); `nextKeyDigests` presence equal and `D(p+1) = D(p)`; `P_w(p+1)` equals `P_w(p)` as written in every field except `attestations` and `labels`; `subjects(P(p+1)) = subjects(P(p))`; every `a' ∈ P(p+1).attestations` is carried or a `renewal(a', stmt(P(p), subject(a')), p, e.timestamp)`; `|{c ∈ creds(K) : attestedValidAt(P(p+1), c, e.timestamp)}| ≥ M_rot(P(p+1))`; and for every `c ∈ subjects(P(p))`: `cls(P(p+1), K, D, e.timestamp, c) ≥ cls(P(p), K, D, e.timestamp, c)` (no subject's class drops; a renewed subject is `HUMAN`).
13. **(Policy-time no lock-out) C.** [A-124.1] In every epoch with `P_w` present, set at `p`: `|{cred(k) : k ∈ K, ¬agentCred(k)}| ≥ M_rot(P) ≥ P.threshold ≥ 1`; for every `T ∈ PR_types` with `byType[T]` present, `byType[T] ≤ |{cred(k) : k ∈ K, ¬agentCred(k)}|`; `byType[T] < threshold` only for `T ∈ LOW_types = PR_types ∪ {STATE_CHECKPOINT}` (§6.5.2 rule 11); under `ATTESTED` additionally `|{c ∈ creds(K) : attestedValidAt(P, c, t_set)}| ≥ M_rot(P)`, `byType[T] ≤ |{c ∈ creds(K) : started(P, c, t_set)}|` for `T ∈ PR_types` (§6.5.2 policy-time rule (b)), and `|P.attesters| ≥ 2`; and `attCreds(P) ∩ foreign(σ(p)) = ∅`, `attCreds(P) ∩ creds(K) = ∅`, and no digest of `attCreds(P)` is in `D` (§6.5.2 rule 2, §6.5.4).
14. **(Class is a function of credential) C.** [A-124.1] In an epoch with `P_w` present `creds(K)` are pairwise distinct; `cls` is a total function of `(P, K, D, t, c)` over every credential, never of a `keyId` and never of the event; admissibility (`adm(e)`) is decided before `cls` is applied; under `DECLARED` semantics `cls` never yields `LAPSED` or `NONE`.
15. **(Commitment conservation) C.** [A-114.1] For every accepted `e` with `e.type = 26` out of a committed epoch: either `revealed(e) ≠ ∅`, every `k ∈ revealed(e)` has `digest(k) ∈ D(p)`, and, when `G(e, p)`, every `k ∈ revealed(e)` has exactly one entry of `NKA(e)` with its `keyId` (verifying); or `isPolicyRot(e) ∧ set(e.data.nextKeyDigests) = D(p)`. Corollary (every reading, every history): no accepted event whose `S(e)` and `NKA(e)` name only keys of `K(p)` changes or drops `D(p)`.
16. **(Keys change only by key rotations) U.** [A-114.1] `isPolicyRot(e) ⇒ K(p+1) = K(p)` in keyIds and credentials.
17. **(Pre-event authority) C.** [A-114.1] The verdict on `e` at `p` is a function of `(K, D, P_w, t_set)(p)`, `σ(p)`, `τ(p)`, the head hash at `p − 1` and `e` itself. `e`'s own `data` enters authorization only through: the challenge (`effectHash`); `adm(e)` (recovery extension, which still requires `D(p)`); the structural predicates `isRestoration(e)`, `isRecovery(e)`, `PR_eff(e, σ(p))`, `isCheckpoint(e)`, which are computed from `e.data`, the `keyId`s of `S(e)`, the epoch record and `σ(p)` before any signature is verified or counted. `e`'s `ownerPolicy`, `ownerKeys` and `nextKeyDigests` take effect at positions `≥ p + 1`.
18. **(Attestation binding) C.** [A-124.1] Every statement `a` in `P_w(p+1)` set at `p`: `audience = genesis.domain`; `attesterKeyId` names an attester of the same policy, whose signature verifies; subject in `K(p+1)` or with digest in `D(p+1)`; attester credential `∉ creds(K)`, attester digest `∉ D`, attester credential `∉ foreign(σ(p))`; `notBefore < expiresAt`; `expiresAt > e.timestamp` **unless `carried(a, p)`** (every type-26 event, §6.5.2 rule 3's waiver); subject credential not `agentCred`; at most one per subject credential; `historyId` absent at `p = 0`, and for `p > 0` either `carried(a, p)` or `historyId = recordHash(0)`.
19. **(Resume roots) C.** [A-124.1] No `/2` head attestation whose signer fails §7.4 (a), (b) or (c), with (b) and (c) judged under the **append epoch** of the file head, is used as a resume root or as an export root; in-file `STATE_CHECKPOINT`s are resume roots at any position. The rule is about the assurance of the signer; the root value of either object is verified against the resumed state.
20. **(Additivity) U.** [A-114.1] For every history in which no event carries `ownerPolicy` or `additionalControlAuthorizations` (hence no epoch carries `policy`, `humanFloor`, `attestations` or `recovery`): `Accepted`, every state root, projection head and record equal their values under r4 + G5 (this document with §6.5, and every rule it guards, removed). Mechanically: every rule of §6.5 and every rule it guards in §7.4–§7.5, §15 and Appendix A is guarded by `ownerPolicy` in the event, `P_w(p)` present, `additionalControlAuthorizations` present, `isAtt(P(p))` or `P(p).recovery`, each of which is false throughout such a history. (T3, §6.5.6, is guarded by a non-null genesis `sessionTimeHorizonMs` and is outside this invariant's hypothesis; a history whose genesis leaves it null replays exactly as before [A-179.3 (A-22.6)].)
21. **(Prefix) U.** [A-114.1] No event with `inPrefix(p)` carries `ownerPolicy` or `additionalControlAuthorizations`; a migrated genesis carries no `ownerPolicy`.
22. **(Codes) C.** [A-114.1] Every replay refusal introduced by §6.5 and the rules it guards (G6, G6.1, G6.2 and their amendments) is `EVENT_DATA_INVALID`, `CONTROL_AUTHORIZATION_INVALID` or `TRANSITION_INVALID`; the §18 replay set stays at 26. Store/API and witness codes are outside this invariant.
23. **(Distinct credentials are distinct humans)** *Known not to hold.* U. [A-114.1] Core sees keys.
24. **(Declared class is evidence)** *Known not to hold.* U. [A-114.1] A label is a label; §6.5.7 only closes the case where Core has seen the credential as a session or counterparty.
25. **(Attester honesty)** *Known not to hold.* U. [A-114.1] Collusion is indistinguishable from consent.
26. **(Expiry is real time)** *Known not to hold.* C. [A-114.1] `HUMAN`/`LAPSED` is decided on `e.timestamp`. What a non-owner writer can do is bounded by invariant 33 (and by T3 where the genesis sets a horizon, §6.5.6 [A-179.3 (A-22.6)]); what an owner-side single authorizer can do is item 37; Core has no clock (E-23).
27. **(Recovery without `recovery`)** *Known not to hold.* U. [A-114.1] By design (L10).
28. **(Protection under `M_rot = 1`)** *Known not to hold.* U. [A-114.1]
29. **(Transport, presence, custody of remote signers)** *Known not to hold.* U. [A-114.1]
30. **(Witness liveness)** *Known not to hold.* U. [A-114.1]
31. **(Non-expansion) C.** [A-124.1] [A-126.5] For every accepted `e` with `PR_eff(e, σ(p))`, F.5 holds (`allow` and `live` monotone non-increasing **for every `t ≥ e.timestamp`**; `K`, `D`, `P_w`, bound witnesses and bound policy unchanged), **under ASM-1…5** (F.4). Reduced form for Lean: `PR_eff(e, σ) ⇒ e.type ≠ 26` and `PR_eff` is a function of `(e.data, e.timestamp, σ(p))` (the `AGENT_UNAPPOINTED` row reads `t`) [A-126.5]. Concrete form for Lean: every row of the §6.5.3 `PR_eff` table over a concrete record model with the `BUDGET_DECLARED` row and the prohibition ancestry and grantor clauses, the grantor clause read over the anchor path [A-127.3], rollover included. Status at the 17 Oct edition [A-205.2]: OwnerPolicy's `NonExpansion.lean` proves the rows in their G6.3 form (grantor-only termination clause, A-116.1; `revoke_nonExpansion`, `terminate_nonExpansion`) over a model in which ASM-1…5 hold by definition; the r5 `AGENT_TERMINATED` row (A-127.2) is proved for the executable procedure model by `Authority.G65.algebra_nonexpansion` and `Authority.G65.revised_allow_nonexpansion` over `Authority.G64.terminationGuard` (Termination family); the `AUTHORITY_REVOKED` row's procedure-level theorem is ordered by A-199 and the OwnerPolicy re-proof with the anchor-path clause by A-208. No theorem named `NE.ceTerm_prohibition_grantor` or `asm24_concrete` exists in that edition. Full form for the checker over its decision oracle, both readings, with `allow` read before the proof-size preflight (F.5, §6.5.9).
32. **(Reduction needs no agent; lowered thresholds reduce only) C.** [A-124.1] In every epoch with `P_w` present and every `T ∈ PR_types`: `byType[T] ?? threshold ≤ |{cred(k) : k ∈ K, ¬agentCred(k)}|`, and under `ATTESTED` `≤ |{c ∈ creds(K) : started(P, c, t_set)}|`; hence **for every `t ≥ t_set`, `|{c ∈ creds(K) : cls(P, K, D, t, c) ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED}}| ≥ byType[T] ?? threshold`** (a started statement is valid or expired, never `NONE`). For every accepted ◆ `e` with `¬PR_eff(e, σ(p))`: `M_eff(e) ≥ P(p).threshold` unless `e.type = STATE_CHECKPOINT`.
33. **(Only owners cross an expiry) C.** [A-124.1] For every accepted `e` at `p` with `isAtt(P(p))` and every `a ∈ P(p).attestations`: `τ(p) < a.expiresAt ≤ e.timestamp ⇒ e` is a ◆ event (T1, §6.5.6). Activations are not bounded: a ◇/▲ event may carry time past a `notBefore` (stated, §6.5.6).
34. **(Owner, committed, attester and foreign credentials are disjoint under a policy) C.** [A-124.1] In every epoch with `P_w` present, set at `p`: `creds(K) ∩ foreign(σ(p)) = ∅`; every `d ∈ introduced(e, p)` (every `d ∈ D` at the genesis) is the digest of no credential in `foreign(σ(p)) ∪ attCreds(P(p)) ∪ attCreds(P_w(p+1))`; `attCreds(P_w(p+1)) ∩ foreign(σ(p)) = ∅`; and while `P_w(p)` is present no accepted `RUNTIME_SESSION_ADMITTED` or `COUNTERPARTY_REGISTERED` at `p` carries a credential in `creds(K(p)) ∪ attCreds(P(p))` or whose digest is in `D(p)`. Corollary (induction over the epochs with `P_w`): `creds(K(p)) ∩ foreign(σ(p)) = ∅` at every `p` in such an epoch. The attester clauses are read over the attesters **in force and resulting**, never over every attester ever named (§6.5.7). A carried digest naming a foreign credential may exist in `D`; it is never revealed while `P_w` is present (§6.5.4).
35. **(Asserted-root threshold) C.** [A-124.1] A `/2` head attestation seeds a resume or authenticates an export `from` only if `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT) = 1` under the policy of the **append epoch** at the file head (the view's last epoch); otherwise only in-file checkpoints, which carried `M_eff(35)` counting signatures, do (§7.4 (c)). The guarantee is about the signer's assurance.
36. **(Structural predicates before signatures) C.** [A-124.1] `M_eff(e)`, `PR_eff(e, σ(p))`, `isRestoration(e)` (reading `ownerKeys` against members of `K(p)` before the key test), `isRecovery(e)` (computing `digest` of possibly malformed revealed credentials, a string function), `isCheckpoint(e)`, `adm(e)` and `cls` are computable from `e.data`, the `keyId`s of `S(e)`, `e.timestamp`, the epoch record and `σ(p)` without verifying any signature; `hc(e)` and T2 are computed after control authorization from `C(e)`; replay's verdict and code do not depend on the order in which signatures are verified.
37. **(Owner-side time)** *Known not to hold.* C. [A-124.1] Under `DECLARED`, a key that authorizes alone chooses `e.timestamp` with no upper bound; the effect is the expiry of sessions, budgets and dated grants (a reduction) and the loss of real-time meaning. Under `ATTESTED`, a single human-class signer is bounded by T2 (invariant 42); **two** human-class counting signers choose any timestamp, including `2^53 − 1`, after which no `ATTESTED` policy can be set or restored. Reason: two human-class keys are a quorum under Appendix G G.2 (1), and Core has no clock.
38. **(Key-set liveness)** *Known not to hold.* N. [A-114.1] Out of a committed epoch a quorum holding no committed key can never add, remove or replace a key (§6.5.4, Appendix G G.5). Reason: HF1's commitment guarantee; E-24 would relax it.
39. **(Custody of committed keys)** *Known not to hold.* N. [A-114.1] `M_rot` counts keys; one custodian of `M_rot` committed keys recovers alone (Appendix G G.2 (4), G.5).
40. **(Attester withdrawal; cross-history statements)** *Known not to hold.* N. [A-114.1] An attester cannot withdraw a statement (only the quorum embeds and removes; E-19); statements embedded at position 0 bind `audience` only and are reusable by another history with the same `domain` (§6.5.2 rule 3).
41. **(Single-key roots vouch for skipped state)** *Known not to hold* where `M_eff(35) = 1`. N. [A-114.1] A `/2` pin is a single-key object (§6.5.3); when the deployment lets one key sign a checkpoint, one key likewise seeds a resume (invariant 35 is the whole guarantee).
42. **(Single human-class signer bounded by the horizon) N, intended.** [A-124.1] [A-126.4] For every accepted ◆ `e` at `p ≥ 1` with `isAtt(P(p))` and `hc(e) ≤ 1`: `e.timestamp = τ(p) ∨ e.timestamp ≤ min(H(p), 2^53 − 2)` (T2, §6.5.6). Corollary: no accepted history reaches a head time of `2^53 − 1` under `ATTESTED` without an event with `hc ≥ 2`; and for every reachable `ATTESTED` state with head time `t < 2^53 − 1`, a fresh statement with `expiresAt > t` exists in U53, so §6.5.2 rule 3 is satisfiable at a restoration. A **renewal** in the sense of §6.5.5 additionally needs `a'.expiresAt > a.expiresAt` and is therefore impossible for a statement whose `expiresAt` is already `2^53 − 1`; such a statement needs none: it can only be carried, and its subject cannot lapse before head time `2^53 − 1`, which no history reaches without `hc ≥ 2` (item 37) [A-126.4].
43. **(Counterparty keys replaced by re-registration)** *Known not to hold.* N. [A-124.1] `foreign(σ)` is a function of the records; a counterparty key replaced by a re-registration is in no record and may later be committed or revealed. Reason: a record field for former keys is a format change for every counterparty record (E-28, guarded, post-rc.2). Ceremony MUST (Appendix G G.2 (9)).

**F.3 Liveness properties.** [A-114.2] [A-124.2] "Authorizable" means: there exists a well-formed event at the current head `h` that `Accepted` admits. Every property L1–L12 is written over `M_eff(e)` of the constructed event and assumes all of the common hypotheses **H0** [A-124.2]:
- (a) *epoch*: `K⁺, D⁺, P⁺` are the append epoch's (the epoch in force at `h + 1`);
- (b) *holders*: the named keys' credentials are in `Hld` and H-1 holds; the holders are willing;
- (c) *time*: the event's timestamp `t` satisfies `τ ≤ t ≤ 2^53 − 1`, `τ = e_h.timestamp`; "at any timestamp" or "any legal time" means for every such `t`;
- (d) *capacity*: the absolute limits of §5.2 admit one more control event and, for a type with growth, one more record;
- (e) *preconditions*: the type's own r4/HF1 preconditions hold in `σ(h+1)` (a revocation has an unrevoked grant, a fence has an `ACTIVE` agent at `fromEpoch`, an update has an `ACTIVE` budget, etc.); for a `PR_eff` event the row's own clauses (§6.5.3) hold as well: for a fence `toEpoch > fromEpoch` and no session of the agent at `toEpoch`; for a termination, no unrevoked prohibition with the agent on its anchor path [A-127.2] [A-203.1]; for a revocation, no unrevoked prohibition under the grant [A-124.2] [A-203.1];
- (f) *well-formed*: the event carries `additionalControlAuthorizations` iff `M_eff(e) ≥ 2`, distinct `keyId`s and credentials, and the closed schemas;
- (g) *rotations*: every revealed key passes the §3.3 declaration test, carries a credential no epoch declared and no session or counterparty of `σ` uses, with pairwise distinct credentials; when a commitment is required or chosen, a fresh `D'` of digests of keys no epoch declared and not in the new set exists; the resulting policy is valid under §6.5.2 (rules 1–11, the attestation rules and the policy-time rules) and §6.5.7 at `t`; a committed key is revealed under the `keyId` its pre-attestation names when that statement is to be carried; the resulting `ownerKeys` carry no credential of `foreign(σ)`; introduced digests pass the commitment rule of §6.5.4; attesters pass §6.5.2 rule 2 [A-124.2];
- (h) *policy change*: `P' ≠ P_w⁺` as written.

- **L1 (Ordinary events, `DECLARED`).** [A-114.2] [A-124.2] If credentials of `≥ M_eff(e)` distinct keys of `K⁺` are held with at least one not `agentCred`, an event of ◆ type `T ≠ 26` is authorizable.
- **L2 (Ordinary events, `ATTESTED`).** [A-114.2] [A-124.2] If credentials of `≥ M_eff(e)` distinct keys of `K⁺` with `cls ∈ {HUMAN, AGENT}` at `t` are held, at least one `HUMAN`, an event of ◆ type `T ≠ 26` with `¬X(e)` is authorizable at `t`; with `X(e)`, `LAPSED` keys count as well (L3).
- **L3 (Reduction, `ATTESTED`, lapse included).** [A-126.1] If credentials of `≥ M_eff(e)` distinct keys of `K⁺` with `cls ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED, AGENT}` at `t` are held, at least one `HUMAN` or `LAPSED`, and the event satisfies `PR_eff(e, σ)` (or is a checkpoint), it is authorizable at `t`, where `t` is any legal time if **`M_eff(e) ≥ 2` and at least two of the counting signers are human-class (`hc(e) ≥ 2`)**, and otherwise `t = τ` or `τ ≤ t ≤ min(H, 2^53 − 2)` (T2). At `M_eff(e) = 1` the array is forbidden (§6.5.3 step 2), so `hc(e) ≤ 1` and T2 binds whatever the number of human-class holders. In particular it is authorizable at `t = τ` after every statement has expired. Under Appendix G G.2 (2) one such holder suffices; by invariant 32, for every `t ≥ t_set` at least `byType[T] ?? threshold` human-class credentials exist in `K⁺`, so no agent is ever needed (theorem, not a remark).
- **L4 (Reduction, `DECLARED`).** [A-114.2] [A-124.2] As L1 for a `PR_eff` event; one non-agent holder suffices under Appendix G G.2 (2); no agent is needed (invariant 32).
- **L5 (Restoration).** [A-124.2] Under `isAtt(P⁺)`, if credentials of `≥ M_rot(P⁺)` distinct keys of `K⁺` with `attestedAny(P⁺, c)` are held, and for `≥ M_rot(P⁺)` of them an attester named in `P⁺` issues renewals (`notBefore ∈ (max(stmt.notBefore, t_set⁺), t]`, `expiresAt > max(stmt.expiresAt, t)`, `historyId = recordHash(0)`), then a restoration rotation carrying every other statement as written is authorizable at `t` subject to T2 (with `M_rot ≥ 2` the holders are `≥ 2` human-class and T2 does not bind). No hypothesis on `byType` is needed (§6.5.2 policy-time rule (b) is preserved by carrying and by renewal).
- **L6 (Policy change).** [A-124.2] If credentials of `≥ M_rot(P⁺)` distinct keys of `K⁺` with `cls = HUMAN` at `t` (not `agentCred` under `DECLARED`) are held, a policy rotation to any `P'` satisfying H0(g)–(h) with the same keys is authorizable, out of a committed or uncommitted epoch. **Repetition:** after it, L6 applies again to the new append epoch with its own antecedent (holders, classes and `M_rot(P')` under `P'`); no claim is made that the same holders keep a quorum under `P'`.
- **L7 (Key change).** [A-114.2] If holders as in L6 exist and, out of a committed epoch, at least one credential with digest in `D⁺` is held, a key rotation revealing it (with its proof of possession) is authorizable; out of an uncommitted epoch no committed key is needed. Without a held committed key out of a committed epoch, no key rotation is authorizable (item 38; this clause is a theorem, like L10).
- **L8 (Recovery).** [A-124.2] If `P⁺.recovery` is set, credentials of `h` keys of `K⁺` with `cls = HUMAN` at `t` and of `r ≥ 1` pairwise distinct credentials with digest in `D⁺` are held, `h + r ≥ M_rot(P⁺)`, each revealed key passes H0(g) (in particular its credential is not in `foreign(σ)` and, where its pre-attestation is to be carried, it is revealed under that statement's `keyId`), and under `ATTESTED` each of the `r` has `attestedValidAt(P⁺, c, t)`, a recovery rotation is authorizable at `t` subject to T2. With `h = 0`, `r ≥ max(1, M_rot)` is required (Appendix G H1b under `M_rot ≥ 2` needs `M_rot` escrowed keys). A `LAPSED` revealed key never counts.
- **L9 (Reduction survives quorum loss).** [A-126.2] If `h < M_rot` human-class credentials are held, L3/L4 hold for every `PR_eff` event with `M_eff(e) ≤ h` (agent-free), and with `a` willing `AGENT` co-signers for every `PR_eff` event with `M_eff(e) ≤ h + a` provided `h ≥ 1`; **L3's time clause is read at the constructed event's `M_eff(e)`: with `M_eff(e) = 1`, or with one human-class counting signer, T2 binds `t` regardless of `h`.**
- **L10 (Non-liveness, theorem).** [A-124.2] Under H-2: if, under `DECLARED` semantics, no held credential of `K⁺` is non-agent, or, under `ATTESTED`, no held credential of `K⁺` has `attestedAny(P⁺, c)` (so that no held credential is `HUMAN` or `LAPSED` **at any `t ≥ τ`**), and either `P⁺.recovery` is absent or no credential with digest in `D⁺` is held, then no ◆ event is ever authorizable again; ◇/▲ events continue until their session's `expiresAt`, a fence, capacity, the bound of T1 (§6.5.6) or, when the genesis sets a horizon, the bound of T3 [A-179.3 (A-22.6)].
- **L11 (Checkpoint under lapse).** [A-124.2] Under `ATTESTED`, if credentials of `≥ M_eff(e)` keys of `K⁺` with `cls ∈ {HUMAN, LAPSED, AGENT}` are held, at least one `HUMAN` or `LAPSED`, a `STATE_CHECKPOINT` is authorizable at `t = τ` and at every `t` T2 admits.
- **L12 (Time advance).** [A-126.3] Under `ATTESTED`, for a position `p` let `X(p)` be the earliest `a.expiresAt > τ(p)` over `P(p).attestations` (undefined when there is none). **Necessity, per current epoch:** a ◇/▲ event at `p` with `timestamp ≥ X(p)` is never accepted (invariant 33 with T1); session time crosses `X(p)` only after some ◆ event with `timestamp ≥ X(p)` is accepted, or after a type-26 event (itself ◆, at any time T2 admits) replaces `P(p)` so that the statement expiring at `X(p)` is renewed or dropped. No claim is made with `X` fixed from an earlier epoch: a restoration before `X` that renews the statement moves the barrier. **Sufficiency:** a ◆ event is authorizable at `X(p)` whenever **`X(p) ≤ 2^53 − 2`** and the holders of L3 (a `PR_eff` event with its precondition) or of L11 exist, since then `X(p) ≤ min(H, 2^53 − 2)`; at `X(p) = 2^53 − 1` it needs `hc ≥ 2` (invariant 42), so a statement that encodes "never expires" as the U53 maximum is crossed by no single signer. Under Appendix G G.2 (8) one `HUMAN` or `LAPSED` holder suffices for every `X(p) ≤ 2^53 − 2`.

**F.4 Authority and budget semantics that invariant 31 relies on (ASM-1…5).** [A-116.3] §8.1 incorporates the rc.1-final authority procedure by reference; the effective text does not define it, and §8.1.1 describes it as frozen in build1. Invariant 31's full form is therefore a theorem **relative to** the following assumptions, each a property of that procedure over the records it reads. The checker's oracle implements them (both of its readings satisfy them with the `PR_eff` rows of §6.5.3); the builder MUST add one property test per assumption against the engine's authority procedure (random authority forests, random requests; the test is a builder test, not a conformance vector, §6.5.9) and report any failure as an engine-versus-spec question to the architect. §8.1.1 (e)–(h) state the same facts as spec02 amendments; this subsection states them in the form the checker and the Lean model use.
- **ASM-1 (revocation is monotone).** [A-133.3] For a fixed request and evaluation time, if `σ'` differs from `σ` only in that one `PERMISSION` record is marked `revoked`, and no unrevoked `PROHIBITION` has that permission in its ancestry, then `allow(σ', r, t) ⇒ allow(σ, r, t)` with `allow` as in F.5. Proof sketch, which the builder's property test follows and which the Lean theorem of A-199 is to follow (`Authority.G65.revised_allow_revocation_nonexpansion` over the procedure model, stated in `ASM1-THEOREM-FOR-ASTRA.md`; until it lands, ASM-1 is property-tested and not kernel-proved for the executable procedure model, and the 17 Oct edition's `asm1_before_output_limited` / `asm1_after_allows` refute only the superseded final-verdict reading of Appendix D) [A-199.4] [A-205.2]: the winner `W` of `σ'` contains no revoked grant (a path through one fails `REVOKED`); `W`'s graph, its applying prohibitions' activity (the ancestry clause) and its usage are identical in `σ`; so `W` succeeds in `σ` and a winner exists there. The proof-size preflight (spec02 §6.3) is outside ASM-1: a smaller winner may fit where a larger one did not, and that is the whole of the `asm1-output-bound` observation (§8.1.1 (g)). The ASM-1 test compares the pre-preflight candidate result and asserts separately that the final decision differs from it only by `OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (§6.5.9).
- **ASM-2 (prohibitions persist with their path).** [A-127.1] [A-137.1] An unrevoked `PROHIBITION` keeps its effect while its anchor path is live. It loses effect only when a grant in its ancestry is revoked, or when an actor on its anchor path (its own grantor, or the grantor or grantee of a record in `anc(A', σ)`) becomes `TERMINATED`; in the second case every permission path through a grant tied to that actor loses effect at the same time. A GLOBAL prohibition (empty `anc`) has no anchor path and no lifecycle term: it loses effect only by revocation or outside its own window (its grantor is the global policy source identifier, not an agent record) [A-201.1]. "Effect" means **intrinsic activity**, spec02 §4.4 `prohibitionIsActive`, which includes the prohibition's own `[notBefore, expiresAt)` and every anchor grant's window, revocation and (replay) lifecycle; the authorization outcome is a consequence, not the definition; the "revocation-only activity helper" is a diagnostic reading and is not adopted [A-137.1]. The builder's ASM-2 property test is stated to this sentence; its direct-grantor cases are conforming behaviour, not failures. The `AGENT_TERMINATED` row of `PR_eff` (§6.5.3) excludes the termination of any actor on an unrevoked prohibition's anchor path [A-203.1], so a `PR_eff` termination never ends a prohibition and the row is non-expanding under this assumption by the same argument as the `AUTHORITY_REVOKED` row's ancestry clause (the termination only removes paths).
- **ASM-3 (intersections fail closed).** [A-131.3] A member of `requiredIntersectionIds` **fails the path** when the named authority record is missing or revoked, or when its path is not live under the procedure (in particular when its grantee, or any actor on its anchor path, is `TERMINATED`); a failing member is never dropped from the intersection. Either reading of "terminated" is conjunctive, so invariant 31 is unaffected.
- **ASM-4 (lifecycle is conjunctive).** [A-116.3] [A-127.4] [A-137.2] A `TERMINATED` actor, an `ENDED` tenure and a session whose `controlEpoch` differs from its agent's each only cause denial; the status of an agent that controls other agents never enables an `ALLOW` for them when it changes to `TERMINATED`; and a `RETIRED` counterparty, which `authorize` never reads: retirement changes no §8.1 verdict and no `live` session; its effect is that no `COUNTERPARTY_RECEIPT_RECORDED` (Appendix A row 40) is accepted for it until a re-registration. `COUNTERPARTY_RETIRED` is `PR_eff` because it only removes (receipts) and is trivially non-expanding for `allow` and `live` [A-127.4]. A strict "retired ⇒ deny" was never a rule of the effective text and is E-29 (§8.1.1 (h)); the monotonic reading is the ruling and is reaffirmed [A-137.2].
- **ASM-5 (budgets deny only).** [A-116.3] §8.1 as written: a budget procedure never turns `DENY` or `INDETERMINATE` into `ALLOW`; it is applied only after `ALLOW`.
With ASM-1…5 and the `PR_eff` rows of §6.5.3, the full form of invariant 31 is provable for every row over a concrete record model extended by the `BUDGET_DECLARED` row (budgets deny only) and the prohibition ancestry and grantor clauses [A-116.3], the grantor clause read over the anchor path [A-127.3]. Status at the 17 Oct edition [A-205.2]: OwnerPolicy's `NonExpansion.lean` proves this with the G6.3 grantor-only clause and with ASM-1…5 as its definition of the procedure; the anchor-path form of the termination row is `Authority.G65.algebra_nonexpansion` over `Authority.G64.terminationGuard`; the OwnerPolicy re-proof with the anchor-path clause, and the ASM-1 theorem over the procedure model (A-199), are ordered (A-208). ASM-1…5 are theorems of the procedure model only where this subsection and item 31 say so.

**F.5 Non-expansion (the property `PR_eff` events are meant to satisfy).** [A-116.4] [A-133.2] Let `allow(σ, r, t)` be true iff the §8.1 step-1 authority algebra (spec02 §4 steps 1–10, raw path §7.1 steps 1–9), for a request `r` at evaluation time `t` over the records §8.1 reads (`authority`, `agent`, `role`, `tenure`, `session`, `counterparty`, `budget`, `budgetUsage`, `usage`, `nonce`), yields a successful candidate and no step-10 nonce collision, **before the proof-size preflight** (spec02 §6.3); equivalently, the final verdict is `ALLOW`, or is `INDETERMINATE / OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` produced by the preflight on a successful candidate. Every other `INDETERMINATE` and every `DENY` is `¬allow` [A-133.2]. Let `live(σ, t)` be the set of `session` records with `t < expiresAt`, `controlEpoch` equal to the agent's current epoch and the agent `ACTIVE`. For every accepted `e` at `p` with `PR_eff(e, σ(p))`, **for every `r` and every `t ≥ e.timestamp`**: `allow(σ(p+1), r, t) ⇒ allow(σ(p), r, t)` and `live(σ(p+1), t) ⊆ live(σ(p), t)`; `K`, `D`, `P_w`, bound witnesses and the bound witness policy are unchanged. Times before `e.timestamp` are excluded (LEAN3-X-13, `NE.ce31_carry_past`): no decision is ever evaluated at a time earlier than the head, since `evaluationTime` equals the event timestamp and timestamps are non-decreasing; a replay re-derives earlier decisions over the earlier state. **Outside the claim:** obligation, duty and succession records and the transitions they admit. A termination under a `SUCCESSION_RULE_DECLARED` may make `ROLE_TRANSFERRED` (◆, owner-authorized) and `OBLIGATION_PERFORMANCE_ASSIGNED` (▲) admissible for the successor; neither changes `allow` or `live`; a successor gains §8.1 authority only through later owner-authorized events [A-116.4]. The checker evaluates this property over its decision oracle on every accepted `PR_eff` event of the sweep, with `allow` read before the preflight (`asm1_scope=algebra`, §6.5.9); what the owner can therefore always reduce, with substitutes, is Appendix G G.4.

**F.6 The subset theorem (`Accepted ⇒ acceptSF`).** [A-120.2] With `acceptSF(e, epoch, previousHead, genesis)` the state-free subset of Appendix A row 26 defined in §7.4 (row 26 with the owner-key rule's `foreign` test, the commitment rule's `foreign` clause and the attester rule's `foreign` clause omitted; the `attCreds(P(p))` clause of the commitment rule kept [A-131.2]): `Accepted(e, p) ⇒ acceptSF(e, epoch(p), head(p − 1), genesis)`. A verifier using `acceptSF` never refuses a rotation full replay accepted; it accepts a rotation full replay refuses only through the omitted foreign-credential rules, which it does not claim to check. On an accepted history the epochs derived by any `acceptSF` verifier equal the epochs of full replay. The theorem holds a fortiori with the `attCreds(P(p))` clause kept (fewer rules deleted) [A-131.2]. The last sentence (independent epoch replay) is checked over the sweep; it is a separate claim, not a consequence of the implication (§8.1.1 LP-X-18).

**F.7 The N3 theorem (removal of a committee key).** [A-151.3] The exact form, for Lean, of the claim that an owner quorum "can always rotate out" a key held by an anchoring committee (the anchoring profiles document, A-140.4, which is outside Core).
- **(a) Removal.** Under H0(a)–(h) for a type-26 event at head `h`, append epoch `(K⁺, D⁺, P⁺)`, committee key `c ∈ K⁺`: if credentials of `≥ M_rot(P⁺)` distinct keys of `K⁺ \ {c}` with `cls = HUMAN` at `t` are held (under `DECLARED`: not `agentCred`; under `ATTESTED`: a statement with `notBefore ≤ t < expiresAt`), and, **out of a committed epoch, a credential with digest in `D⁺` is held and revealed with its proof of possession** (L7; item 38: out of a committed epoch no quorum without a committed key can remove a key), then a rotation to `K' = (K⁺ \ {c}) ∪ revealed` with a policy `P'` valid for `K'` (§6.5.2 rules 1–11 including rules 6 and 10 and every `byType[T] ≤ N'`; the attestation rules; the policy-time rules; §6.5.7 at `t`) is authorizable. Out of an uncommitted epoch no committed key is needed. `c`'s signature, present or absent, is non-counting (invariant 9) and never required. This is L6/L7 applied to `K'`, not a new liveness property. The probe's rotation (`P' = {threshold: 1}`, lowering `byType` to fit `N' = 2`) is an instance.
- **(b) `LAPSED` contributes nothing to (a)** (§6.5.3: a non-restoration type 26 counts `HUMAN` only).
- **(c) Restoration is a separate capability** (L5): under `ATTESTED`, `≥ M_rot` holders with `attestedAny` plus `≥ M_rot` renewals yield a restoration, which keeps every key including `c` (invariant 12, §6.5.5); (a) then applies at the renewed classes. The companion document's claim is read as (a) under its premises and never as a claim for `LAPSED` holders or outside H0.
Code and stage: none; the writer pre-check and `verify` report. The `verify` listing of policies whose `byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT` exceeds the number of non-`AGENT` credentials is §18 and the SHOULD it rests on is Appendix G G.2 (8) and, for the bound by non-`AGENT` credentials, the anchoring companion document's A-140.6 as amended by A-151.2 (its §2.7) [A-151.2] [OR-01].

**F.8 Known-not-to-hold list (consolidated).** [A-124.3] Items 23–30, 37, 38, 39, 40, 41 and 43 above. Intended: 1–22, 31–36 and 42; L1–L12 under H0. A reviewer who violates a known-not-to-hold item has found nothing new; one who violates an intended invariant, or refutes L1–L12 under H0, has found a defect in the rules of §6.5 and the rules they guard, to be ruled on.

**F.9 For the formalizers.** [A-114.4] [A-124.4] State per epoch is `(K, D, P_w, t_set)` plus `τ`, the head hash, and for `PR_eff`, §6.5.7 and invariants 31, 34 the records `authority`, `budget`, `session`, `counterparty`, `witnessPolicy`. Lean may take `PR_eff` and the foreign-credential predicates as uninterpreted Booleans over `(body, σ)`; `M_eff` then takes `PR_eff` as an argument (§6.5.3); every theorem of F.2 except 31 (full form) and 34 is then provable without them, and 31's reduced form is a one-line lemma. The two readings of G6.1 are closed: `keepsEveryKey = sameMember` is the definition (§6.5.4), `popPerKey` is replaced by the guard `G(e, p)` (§6.5.4 (iv)). ◇/▲ events are modelled only for invariant 33 (their timestamp and their kind); their signatures are not. Reduced statements accepted for Lean: 3 (challenge as an injective structure), 19 and 35 (the predicates, not the resume machinery), 20 (equality of verdicts and epochs with the r4 reference), 21 (migrated genesis), 22 (the three codes), 31 (reduced form). The checker checks the full forms of 31, 33, 34, 35 over its state [A-114.4]. `foreign : R → List Cred` and `attCreds` are parameters as before; the owner-key rule of §6.5.4 is a rule over every key of `ownerKeys` under `G`. `PR_eff`'s record rows take the clauses of the §6.5.3 table; the concrete model gains a `BUDGET_DECLARED` transition (append a budget), prohibition records with `parentAuthorityId` and `grantorId`, the anchor-path reading of ASM-2 (a prohibition ends when any actor on its anchor path is terminated [A-127.3]; in the 17 Oct edition this is `Authority.G64.terminationGuard` of the Termination family, while OwnerPolicy's own model still carries the G6.3 grantor-only clause, A-205.2), and the fence's session clause; ASM-1…5 are the concrete model's definition of the authority procedure, and invariant 31's full form is proved for every row over it, rollover included, for `t ≥ e.timestamp`. T2 is a Boolean over (`C(e)` classes, `e.timestamp`, `τ`, `P(p).attestations`), checked after counting. Invariant 42 and L12 are theorems; L10 is stated over every `t ≥ τ`; invariant 12 carries the class-order clause; invariant 32 carries the "for every `t ≥ t_set`" consequence. F.6 (`accept ⇒ acceptSF`) is a theorem over the main model with `acceptSF` defined by deleting the named rules; `acceptSF_state_free` is the name reserved for the theorem that states §7.4's computability sentence (§8.1.1 LP-X-18; not in the 17 Oct edition; ordered, A-208). F.7's regression witness is the probe `CheckpointQuorum.lean` as accepted [A-151.3]; its general forms are the Committee family's `chosen_humans_remove_committee` (a), `accepted_attested_removal_counts_only_human` (b) and `accepted_partial_restoration_then_removal` and `joined_checkpoint_mixed_removal` (c followed by a), each read with the premise that an attester named in `P` issues the renewal statements (L5; G.1 H3b), which the claims state [A-205.3]. These notes are read with L3, L9, L12, invariant 42 and invariant 31's reduced form as amended above; both formalizations already prove the corrected forms [A-126.6]. Reduced statements accepted for Lean are unchanged (3, 19, 20, 21, 22, 31 (reduced form), 35) [A-124.4] [OR-01].

## Appendix G — Ceremony, SECURITY and OPERATOR statements for owner policies and time

[A-186.2] [A-113] [A-123] This appendix is new in r5. It is **informative for replay** — no sentence here is a replay rule, the checker implements nothing from it, and nothing here loosens or tightens §6.5 — and **normative for the SECURITY and OPERATOR documents**, which carry these sentences; where §6.5, §7.4, §7.5 or §18 says "Appendix G states this", the sentence is here. The scenarios and ceremony items replace the withdrawn A-103 in full and are amended as the later rulings say; the withdrawn text is listed in Appendix D only [A-113] [A-186.3].

**Terms of this appendix.** [A-113] [A-123] [A-116.1] "Reduce power" means authorizing a `PR_eff` event (§6.5.3): revoke a permission, fence, terminate, unappoint without a live role budget, tighten a non-rollover budget, declare a budget, retire a counterparty. "Agent-free" reduction counts `HUMAN` and `LAPSED` holders only; "with agents" counts willing `AGENT` co-signers, who count on `PR_eff` events and never on the floor (§6.5.3, §6.5.5). Under `ATTESTED` a single human-class signer advances time at most to the attestation horizon (T2, §6.5.6). Every row assumes legal time (`t ≥ τ`), capacity headroom and the type's own preconditions (Appendix F, H0); "survivors" counts held credentials, not people. Sessions keep writing until `expiresAt`, a fence, capacity, under `ATTESTED` the earliest unexpired attestation expiry (T1, §6.5.6) and, when the genesis sets a horizon, `ownerTime + sessionTimeHorizonMs` (T3) [A-179.3 (A-22.6)].

**G.1 Human-failure scenarios H1–H5 under this text.** [A-113] [A-123] [A-118.1] [A-121]

| scenario | outcome under §6.5 | reduce power? | recovery without a rogue party? | left to ceremony or law |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H1a** only human owner dies; one YubiKey; `recovery` absent | No ◆ event is ever accepted again (`adm(e)` has no holder, Appendix F L10). Sessions run to `expiresAt` (under `ATTESTED`, at most to the earliest unexpired attestation expiry) and can still spend within budgets; grants persist. | **No.** | **No** in Core; yes if the key material was backed up (the same key). | Backup; the choice to enable `recovery`. |
| **H1b** as H1a with `recovery` and one committed key escrowed | Under `M_rot = 1` the escrow holder signs a recovery rotation (§6.5.4) and may set any policy. Under `M_rot ≥ 2`, `h + r ≥ M_rot` is needed (Appendix F L8): one escrowed key alone is stuck as H1a. The committed key MUST be revealed under the `keyId` its pre-attestation names [A-121]. Under `ATTESTED` the next key must be pre-attested and valid at the chosen `t`; `t` must be `≥ τ`, so a head already past the pre-attestation's expiry leaves it `LAPSED`, which never counts on a recovery rotation: stuck as H1a. | **Yes** after the rotation. | **Yes**, by the escrow holder, who is as powerful as the owner was. | Who holds the next key; renewing next-key statements with current ones (MUST, G.2 (3)). |
| **H1c** one of `M` dies; `N − 1 ≥ M_rot` humans remain | Every ◆ type with `M_eff ≤ N − 1` (plus agent co-signers on non-26 types) is reachable; every policy change is. The dead key leaves `K` **only** by a rotation revealing a committed key someone holds (or out of an uncommitted epoch). Without a held committed key the key set is frozen forever (G.5); survivors neutralize the dead key instead (class `AGENT`, remove its statement, lower thresholds). Under `ATTESTED`, classing the dead key `AGENT` requires dropping its statement in the same rotation (§6.5.2 rule 3); its expired statement may instead be carried, leaving it `LAPSED` (§6.5.5). | **Yes**, agent-free iff `M_eff(e) ≤ N − 1` for the `PR_eff` event; with `a` willing agent co-signers iff `M_eff(e) ≤ N − 1 + a` (§6.5.2 rule 10 bounds `byType` by the epoch's non-agent credentials, not by survivors; G.2 (2) makes it 1). | **Yes** for everything but a key change; a key change needs committed-key custody. | Custody of next keys by distinct survivors. |
| **H2a** rogue human owner, `M_rot ≥ 2`, `≥ M_rot` other humans | The rogue alone cannot rotate keys, change the policy or lock anyone out. Alone it authorizes only events at `M_eff = 1`: under G.2 (2) and §6.5.2 rule 11 those are `PR_eff` events, which cannot expand any agent's power (Appendix F), and checkpoints or anchors if the deployment lowered them; with a compliant agent it reaches `M_eff = 2` non-26 types. Alone it chooses the timestamp of the events it writes within T2: up to the attestation horizon, never `2^53 − 1`; it can force every co-owner to lapse (recoverable by Appendix F L5) and cannot destroy future rotations. With a compliant agent at `threshold 2` it also grants and admits sessions (G.5 states this plainly). The others rotate it out if they hold a committed key, or neutralize it by policy rotation. | **Yes** (the others; and the rogue, for `PR_eff` events at 1). | **Yes**: the others hold a quorum. | Who is rogue is not a Core question. |
| **H2b** rogue owner, `M_rot = 1` | Full policy power: reclassify co-owners `AGENT`, drop `ATTESTED`, disable `recovery`, replace keys out of an uncommitted epoch or with a held committed key. A committed epoch whose next key the others hold blocks the key change, not the policy change. The others win only by acting first. | **Yes** by anyone still classed human; after a lock-out rotation, only the rogue. | Only if the others hold the committed next key **and** `recovery` is in the policy that authorizes their rotation (an earlier enablement the rogue disabled gives nothing). | `threshold: 1` with several humans is the accepted exposure (G.2 (1)). |
| **H3a** attester coerced: attests an agent-held key | A statement is inert until the owner quorum embeds it, agents counting zero; the subject must be in `K` or `D` and not classed `AGENT`; at `p > 0` it must carry this history's `historyId` (§6.5.2 rule 3). Knowing embedding is consent (Appendix F item 25). | **Yes**, unchanged. | **Yes**: the quorum removes statement and attester by policy rotation; by restoration nobody can add one. | Attester independence; evidence review. |
| **H3b** attester refuses to renew | Before lapse: the quorum replaces the attester. After lapse: a second named attester's renewals restore (two attesters are required in Core, §6.5.2 rule 2). With no cooperating attester there is no restoration (§6.5.2's policy-time rule (a) needs `≥ M_rot` renewals): `PR_eff` events and checkpoints remain; keys, attesters, thresholds and floor are frozen. Unrenewed co-owners are carried `LAPSED` through every rotation that keeps the key (§6.5.5). | **Yes** (§6.5.5, the exemption). | Yes with a second attester; otherwise **no** for anything but reduction. | `verify --as-of` ahead of expiry. |
| **H4** every attestation lapses | Only an owner-signed timestamp can cross an expiry (T1, §6.5.6): lapse is the owners' act or their absence, never an agent writer's. Lapsed keys authorize `PR_eff` events, `STATE_CHECKPOINT` and a restoration (carry or strictly renew every subject, same `D`, same policy otherwise). Grants, admissions, key and policy changes wait for restoration. Pins signed while `HUMAN` stay roots under §7.4; lapsed-signed pins do not. Each crossing of an expiry by session time needs a ◆ event at or past it (Appendix F L12); under G.2 (8) one `HUMAN` or `LAPSED` key suffices. | **Yes.** | **Yes**, by the returning humans and a named attester issuing renewals with later `notBefore` and `expiresAt`. | Renewal cadence; choice of `expiresAt`. |
| **H5a** `H < M_rot` humans survive, `recovery` absent | No rotation or policy change ever again. Types with `M_eff ≤ H + a` remain, where `a` is the number of willing `AGENT` co-signers on non-26 types with at least one human present; `PR_eff` events need no agent (§6.5.2 rule 10). Lapse then leaves `PR_eff`, checkpoints and restoration. | **Yes**, agent-free iff `M_eff(e) ≤ H`; with `a` willing agents iff `M_eff(e) ≤ H + a ∧ H ≥ 1`. Restoration needs `≥ M_rot` held human-class keys **and** `≥ M_rot` renewals; with `H < M_rot` there is none. | **No.** | G.2 (2) and the choice of `recovery`. |
| **H5b** as H5a with `recovery`, `≥ M_rot − H` committed keys held by survivors or escrow | A recovery rotation counts `HUMAN` survivors plus revealed committed keys with distinct credentials, `r ≥ 1`; under `ATTESTED` each revealed key must be pre-attested and valid at `t`, and lapsed survivors do not count. Control continues in the new epoch under any policy the rotation sets. | **Yes.** | **Yes.** | Custody of next keys by distinct custodians; pre-attestation renewal. |

The rows are derived from §6.5 and are not rules; where a row and §6.5 differ, §6.5 governs and the row is an erratum [A-113].

**G.2 Ceremony MUST and SHOULD.** [A-113.1] [A-123] [A-119.2] [A-126.4] SECURITY carries these; replay cannot check any of them.

1. **More than one human key:** SHOULD set `byType.OWNER_KEYS_ROTATED ≥ 2`, or `threshold ≥ 2` (which implies it); under `M_rot = 1` Core offers no protection against a single rogue or stolen key.
2. **`threshold ≥ 2`:** SHOULD set every `PR_types` type to 1 in `byType`, so that one present human can always fence, revoke, terminate and declare a budget; the lowered value applies only to events that reduce power by effect (`PR_eff`, §6.5.3); under `ATTESTED` T2 bounds what that one human can do to history time [A-123]. (The earlier clause "a deployment that fears a single key's control of history time sets 2 instead" is withdrawn: T2 makes 1 the safe recommended value under `ATTESTED` as under `DECLARED` [A-119.0].)
3. **`ATTESTED`:** Core requires two attesters (§6.5.2 rule 2); the ceremony MUST check their independence, MUST renew next-key statements with current ones, and MUST reveal a committed key under the `keyId` its pre-attestation names [A-123].
4. **`recovery`:** MUST custody every committed next key as a current key, with **distinct custodians** for distinct committed keys when `M_rot ≥ 2`; MUST document that once committed, every future key change needs each committed key revealed by its holder, whether or not `recovery` stays enabled.
5. **`recovery` absent:** MUST document that loss of every current key is permanent.
6. **Committed epoch, `M_rot ≥ 2`:** SHOULD keep every committed key's material backed up by its custodian; loss of all of them freezes the key set (G.5).
7. **`verify --as-of`** before each renewal period; its warnings are not refusals.
8. **`ATTESTED`:** SHOULD set `byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT = 1`, so that one `HUMAN` or `LAPSED` key can always carry session time across a co-owner's expiry (a checkpoint adds no authority); a deployment that sets it `≥ 2` accepts that every session stops at each expiry until that many human-class keys sign, and gains Appendix F invariant 35's protection against single-key resume roots [A-119.2]. Attesters SHOULD issue `expiresAt ≤ 2^53 − 2`; the maximum is legal but is crossed by no single signer (§6.5.6) [A-126.4]. This SHOULD stands beside the rule that checkpoints need the full `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` quorum, default 1 (§6.5.3) [A-174.1].
9. **Counterparty and session keys** are never reused as owner or committed keys; Core forgets a counterparty key replaced by re-registration (Appendix F item 43) [A-123].

**Collected from the sections.** [A-92.3] [A-97.3] [A-92.4] [A-97.4] The ceremony MUST commit next keys — recovery keys included — under the same class discipline as current keys: a signer admitted by HF1 that has no `keyClasses` entry is `HUMAN` by declaration, and `verify` cannot check the discipline (§6.5.3; under the `ATTESTED` floor a next key counts only if pre-attested, which closes the route for strict deployments) [A-92.3] [A-97.3]. A deployment choosing `M_rot ≥ 2` with `recovery` MUST commit at least `M_rot` next-key digests or accept that recovery needs `M_rot − |committed|` surviving current keys (§6.5.3) [A-97.4]. The ceremony protocol — how signatures are collected, transported, encrypted, timed and witnessed as present — is **not verifiable by replay**: replay sees the counting set and nothing else; a signer missing past its deadline is a ceremony failure, never a relaxation of `M`; and the encryption key of a remote signer MUST differ from its signing key (§6.5.8, §6.3) [A-92.4].

**G.3 What Core never does.** [A-113.2] No unsigned event; no agent-only quorum; no automatic `M` reduction on death or lapse; no relabelling of `AGENT` to `HUMAN` without a policy rotation; no reading of a clock, registry or URL in replay; no legal successor recognized by anything but a key; no commitment replaced by anyone who does not hold a committed key.

**G.4 What the owner can always reduce, with substitutes.** [A-116.5] [A-127.2] Under lapse or at a lowered `PR_types` threshold (requirement 3), one human-class holder can: fence any agent; terminate any agent that is on no unrevoked prohibition's anchor path (fence it otherwise) [A-127.2] [A-203.1]; revoke any `PERMISSION` under which no unrevoked prohibition is delegated (fence or terminate the grantee otherwise, or declare a zero-cap budget on it); unappoint when no live role budget can match the agent (terminate otherwise); tighten any non-rollover budget; **declare any budget**, including a zero cap on any actor, role, action, resource or metric, rollover or not; retire any counterparty. What needs the ordinary quorum: tightening a budget that rolls over (declare a new one instead, §9.5); revoking a permission that an unrevoked prohibition depends on; terminating an agent on an unrevoked prohibition's anchor path [A-203.1]. Every reduction the founder named (revoke, fence, terminate) remains reachable by one human under G.2 (2), either directly or by the substitute in parentheses [A-116.5].

**G.5 SECURITY statements.** SECURITY carries each of the following verbatim. The in-section SECURITY sentences of §8.2 (metric), §9.3 (event-timestamp time source) and §7.5 (reader identity and clock) stay where they are.

**Quorum and signatures.** [A-92.5] "Compromised or manipulated agent key: contributes one signature toward `M` on non-policy ◆ events; cannot authorize alone; cannot alter `ownerKeys` or `ownerPolicy`; cannot seed a resume; mitigated by `M ≥ 2` with ≥ 1 human and by rotation." "Surplus and non-counting signatures are visible evidence, not errors; replay lists the counting set." "Distinct keys are not distinct people, custody or judgement; a 2-of-3 proves a key quorum." A rogue human under `M_rot ≥ 2` with a compliant agent at `threshold 2` grants and admits sessions (H2a) [A-123].

**Recovery and commitments.** [A-97.6] [A-110.4] "Stolen committed next key under `recovery`: with `M_rot = 1`, rotates alone and takes the history; with `M_rot ≥ 2`, contributes one signature. Mitigation: `M_rot ≥ 2`, or leave `recovery` absent and rely on key-material backup." "Without `recovery`, loss of every current key is permanent: no unsigned, agent-only or emergency route exists, by design." "Loss of every committed next key out of a committed epoch freezes the key set permanently; the quorum keeps every other power." "A custodian who holds `M_rot` committed keys recovers alone under `recovery`; `M_rot` is a count of keys, not of custodians." Out of a committed epoch the key set changes only by a rotation that reveals a committed key with proof of possession; a quorum that holds no committed key, with or without `recovery`, can change the policy any number of times, can neutralize a dead or rogue key (class it `AGENT`, remove its statement under `ATTESTED`, lower thresholds within §6.5.2) and can reduce power, but cannot add, remove or replace a key, ever. Disabling `recovery` removes a committed key's signing power and nothing else: the commitment stays and every future key change still needs that key revealed by whoever holds it (§6.5.4; a quorum-recommit option is E-24) [A-110.4].

**Credentials.** [A-123] [A-117.2] [A-117.3] "A commitment carried into a policy epoch that names a session or counterparty credential is dead while the policy is in force; a quorum that resets the policy may reveal it." "A policy-free history whose owner credential was admitted as a session credential adopts a policy only after that key leaves the key set." The rules behind both are §6.5.4 and §6.5.7. Core holds no biometrics or personal identifiers; the evidence bundle of §6.5.5 is not a replay or export input [A-93.5].

**Time.** [A-123] [A-179.5] "A single human-class signer under `ATTESTED` can lapse every co-owner but cannot move history time past the attestation horizon or to its maximum (T2); two human-class signers can." "Replay bounds a session key's timestamp from above only by its session's `expiresAt` [A-22.2], by T1 under `ATTESTED` (statement expiries still ahead of the head) and by T3 when the genesis sets a horizon; a session-carried advance is permanent, and `CONTROL_EPOCH_ADVANCED` does not reverse it." [A-179.5] Under `timeSource: EVENT_TIMESTAMP` a writer can move time forward into later budget windows (§9.3); the owner-side limits of time are §6.5.6 and Appendix F items 26 and 37.

**Authority procedure.** [A-133.6] [A-127.4] "A proof-size refusal (`OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`) is not a denial of authority. A lowered-threshold or lapsed-key revocation or termination can remove an oversized winning path and let a smaller granted path produce a proof; the authority so used was granted and left unrevoked at the ordinary quorum. To stop an actor, use a prohibition, a zero-cap budget, a fence or a termination of the actor, never a proof-size limit." [A-133.6] "Retiring a counterparty does not deny consequential requests that name it; stop them with a prohibition or budget on the resource, or at the connector." [A-127.4]

**Ceremony protocol.** [A-92.4] Transport, encryption, timeouts and presence of signers are the ceremony protocol and are not verifiable by replay (G.2); the LLM behind an agent signer is advisory input and never the proof.

**G.6 OPERATOR statements.** OPERATOR carries the following.

**Owner-side time.** [A-109.3] [A-109.2] Owner-side time is a stated limit, not an invariant Core enforces (§6.5.6, Appendix F items 26 and 37): any owner key that can authorize a ◆ event alone chooses `e.timestamp` within T2, and writers that never advance time keep every statement valid, since Core has no clock (E-23). `verify` lists every ◆ event whose timestamp exceeds `τ(p)` by more than `--time-window` (default 30 days, `2,592,000,000` ms) and every ◆ event that crossed an attestation expiry (§18); with `--as-of` it lists the statements expiring within the window and the head epoch's class counts (§18). The listings are warnings, never refusals; run `verify --as-of` before each renewal period (G.2 (7)) [A-109.3].

**Session time horizon.** [A-179.5] [A-179.4 (A-22.6)] OPERATOR. The field bounds a session key to H past the latest time-advancing owner event. Keep H below the shortest FIXED_WINDOW lengthMs and the notBefore lead of grants that matter; `init` and `budget declare` warn when H ≥ a declared lengthMs. Unattended deployments need a scheduled owner process writing STATE_CHECKPOINT at intervals below H − maxClockSkewMs (checkpointPolicy counts events and does not suffice); this keeps an owner key online. Checkpoints are refused with CLOCK_INVALID while the head is ahead of the owner's clock, so they cannot carry an advance into `ownerTime`. After `migrate` with the field set, sessions surviving the prefix cannot write until the first post-migration owner event; `migrate` prints this. [A-179.5] (JOB37 §2's OPERATOR block verbatim, with "anchor" read as `ownerTime`, and with the "owner event" of its last sentence read as the time-advancing owner event that moves `ownerTime`, §6.5.6 [A-179.2 (A-22.6)] [A-179.4 (A-22.6)] [AR-09]; H is `sessionTimeHorizonMs`, §5.1.) A deployment that sets `sessionTimeHorizonMs` (§5.1; `init --session-time-horizon <ms>` or the `migrate` option) bounds every session-signed and administrative event to at most `H` after the latest time-advancing owner event: a session whose clock has passed `ownerTime + H` stops recording until an owner writes a ◆ event that advances time; the writer does not sign, the API throws `TRANSITION_REJECTED {reason: SESSION_TIME_HORIZON, ownerTime, horizonMs}`, recording returns the not-recorded reason `SESSION_TIME_HORIZON`, a spooled entry stays spooled until an owner event moves `ownerTime`, and `inspect` surfaces the spooled count (§6.5.8, §10.1.1 item 7). No writer invokes an owner signer to satisfy the rule. `init` and `budget declare` warn when `H ≥` a declared `FIXED_WINDOW` `lengthMs`; `migrate` with the field set prints that sessions surviving the prefix cannot write until the first post-migration time-advancing owner event [A-179.4 (A-22.6)]. The bound sentence of G.5 ("Time") is the SECURITY form of the same statement [A-179.5].

**Checkpoints and anchors under a quorum.** [A-92.3] [A-174.1] [A-119.3] A deployment that wants single-signer operational checkpoints or anchors sets `threshold: 1` and raises other types through `byType`, or lowers `byType.STATE_CHECKPOINT` under §6.5.2 rule 11 (G.2 (8)); an automatic checkpoint or handover seal the writer's signers cannot sign to `M_eff(STATE_CHECKPOINT)` is `CHECKPOINT_SUPPRESSED {reason: NO_SIGNER}` and is never written short (§7.3, §6.5.8).

**Rollover budgets.** [A-116.1] [A-132] An update of a budget that rolls over needs the ordinary quorum (never `PR_eff`, A-132; E-27 does not change this [A-192.3]); to tighten such a group at a lowered threshold or under lapse, declare a new non-rollover budget over it (G.4, §9.5). `verify` lists rollover budgets updated by the ordinary quorum (§18).

**Anchoring test profiles (pointer).** [A-183.2] [A-186.4] The operator rules of the Shadownet TEST anchoring profile (chain 127823 only, key in place, journal-before-submit, spending guideline — the 4aq guardrails) are rules of that profile and are stated in `SPEC-IRONTRELLIS-ANCHORING-PROFILES-r1.md`, not here; Core's own text about anchors is §7.4 [A-183.2].
